===Original===
“Well, look here,” said Hugh, “you just railway-train yourself round the house till you find her, and let me know where she is. I want to see her. Off you go now.”

===Chinese===
"好吧，你听我说，"休说，"你绕着房子开火车，直到找到她，然后告诉我她在哪儿。我要见她。快去吧。"

===Original===
The Stuffer steamed himself out with the action of an engine drawing a long train of cars, and disappeared round the corner of the house.

===Chinese===
贪吃鬼用机车拖着一长串车厢的动作把自己"开"了出去，消失在房子的拐角后面。

===Original===
Before long he was back, drew himself up alongside an imaginary platform, intimated that his grandmother was in the verandah, and then proceeded to let the steam hiss out of his safety-valve.

===Chinese===
不久他就回来了，在一个想象中的站台旁停稳，表示奶奶在游廊上，然后让蒸汽从安全阀中嘶嘶喷出。

===Original===
Hugh walked across the quadrangle, under the acacia tree, heavy with blossoms, in which a myriad bees were droning at their work, and through the house on to the front verandah, which looked over the wide sweep of river-flat. Here he found his mother and Miss Harriott, the governess, peeling apples for dumplings—great rosy-checked, solid-fleshed apples, that the hill-country turns out in perfection. The old lady was slight in figure, with a refined face, and a carriage erect in spite of her years. Miss Harriott was of a languid Spanish type, with black eyes and strongly-marked eyebrows. She had a petite, but well-rounded figure, with curiously small hands and feet. Though only about twenty-four years of age she had the sedate and unemotional look that one sees in doctors and nurses—people who have looked on death and birth, and sorrow and affliction. For Ellen Harriott had done her three years’ course as a nurse; she had a natural faculty for the business, and was in great request among the wild folk of the mountains, who looked upon her (and perhaps rightly) as quite equal to the Tarrong doctor in any emergency. She knew them all, for she had lived nearly all her life at Kuryong. When the family moved there from the back country a tutor was needed for the boys, and an old broken-down gentleman accepted the billet at low pay, on condition that he was allowed to bring his little daughter with him. When he died, the daughter still stayed on, and was made governess to the new generation of young folk. She was a queer, self-contained girl, saying little; and as Hugh walked in, she looked up at him, and wondered what new trouble was bringing him to his mother with the open letter in his hand.

===Chinese===
休穿过院子，走过缀满花朵的金合欢树下——无数蜜蜂在花间嗡嗡采蜜——穿过房子来到前游廊，眼前是一片开阔的河滩平地。他在这里找到了母亲和女教师哈里奥特小姐（Miss Harriott），她们正在削苹果做苹果布丁——又大又红、果肉紧实的苹果，正是山地盛产的上等货色。老太太身形纤细，面容端庄，虽然上了年纪，身姿依然挺拔。哈里奥特小姐是那种慵懒的西班牙型美人，黑眼睛，眉毛浓重。她身材娇小但曲线玲珑，手脚出奇地小。虽然只有二十四岁左右，却有着医生和护士身上常见的那种沉着而超然的神情——那是见过死亡与新生、悲伤与苦难的人才有的表情。因为埃伦·哈里奥特（Ellen Harriott）已经完成了三年的护士课程；她有做这行的天赋，在山里那些粗野的人中间极受欢迎，他们认为她（或许没错）在任何紧急情况下都能跟塔龙（Tarrong）的医生比肩。她认识他们所有人，因为她几乎一辈子都住在库里昂。当初这一家子从边远乡下搬来时，需要给孩子们请一个家庭教师，一位落魄的老先生接受了这个低薪职位，条件是允许他带着小女儿一起来。他去世后，女儿留了下来，成了新一代年轻人的女教师。她是个古怪而内敛的女孩，话很少；休走进来时，她抬起头看他，心里琢磨着是什么新的麻烦让他拿着一封拆开的信来找母亲。

===Original===
“Mother,” said Hugh, “I have had a most extraordinary letter.”

===Chinese===
"妈妈，"休说，"我收到一封极不寻常的信。"

===Original===
“From Mr. Grant?” said the old lady, “What does he say?”

===Chinese===
"是格兰特先生的？"老太太说，"他说什么？"

===Original===
She saw by her son’s face that there was something more than usual in the wind, but one who had lived her life, from fortune to poverty, through strife and trial, was prepared to take things much more easily than Hugh.

===Chinese===
她从儿子的神情看出有些非同寻常的事情，但一个从富裕到贫穷、经历过争斗与磨难的女人，处事比休从容得多。

===Original===
“Is it anything very serious?”

===Chinese===
"是什么很严重的事吗？"

===Original===
“His daughter’s coming out to live here.”

===Chinese===
"他女儿要来这儿住。"

===Original===
“What?”

===Chinese===
"什么？"

===Original===
“Yes, here’s the letter. It only came this morning. Patsy was late, the river is up. I’ll read it to you.”

===Chinese===
"是的，信在这里。今天早上才到。帕特西迟到了，河水涨了。我念给你听。"

===Original===
Seating himself at the table, Hugh spread out the letter, and read it:—

===Chinese===
休在桌旁坐下，展开信，念道：

===Original===
> “Dear Gordon,”
> “The last lot of wethers, though they topped the market, only realised 10/-. I think you would show better judgment in keeping these sheep back a little. Don’t rely upon Satton’s advice. He is generally wrong, and is always most wrong when he is most sure he is right.
> “My daughter has arrived from England, and will at once go up to the station. I have written to your mother on the subject. My daughter will represent me in everything, so I wish her to learn a little about stations. Send to meet her at the train on Wednesday next.
> “Yours truly,
> “W. G. Grant.”

===Chinese===
> "亲爱的戈登，
> "上一批阉羊虽然卖到了最高价，但每只只卖了10/-。我认为你更有眼光的做法是把这些羊留一阵子。别听萨顿（Satton）的建议。他通常都是错的，而且在他最自信的时候错得最离谱。
> "我女儿已经从英格兰到了，马上就要来牧场（station）。我已就此事写信给你母亲。我女儿将全权代表我，所以我希望她能学一点牧场的知识。下周三派车到火车站去接她。
> "你忠实的，
> "W. G. 格兰特"

===Original===
“Wednesday next!” said Hugh, “that letter is three days delayed. Patsy couldn’t cross the river. She’ll be there before we can possibly get down. If no one meets her I wonder if she’ll have pluck enough to get into the coach and come on to Donohoe’s.”

===Chinese===
"下周三！"休说，"这封信迟了三天。帕特西过不了河。她到的时候我们不可能赶过去。要是没人去接她，不知道她有没有胆量坐马车到多诺霍来。"

===Original===
“I don’t envy her the trip, if she does,” said Miss Harriott. “The coach-drive over those roads will seem awful to an English girl.”

===Chinese===
"要是她真坐马车来了，我可不羡慕她这趟旅程，"哈里奥特小姐说。"走那种路坐马车，对一个英国姑娘来说可够受的。"

===Original===
“I’ll have to go down at once, anyhow,” said Hugh, “and meet her on the road somewhere. If she is at the railway, I can get there in two days. Have you a letter, Mother?”

===Chinese===
"不管怎样，我得马上动身，"休说，"在路上某处接她。如果她在火车站，我两天就能赶到。妈妈，你收到信了吗？"

===Original===
“Yes,” said the old lady, “but I won’t show it to you now. You shall see it some other time.”

===Chinese===
"收到了，"老太太说，"不过我现在不给你看。你以后再看。"

===Original===
“Well, I’ll set about making a start,” said Hugh. “What trap had I better take?”

===Chinese===
"好，我这就准备动身，"休说。"我该赶哪辆车去？"

===Original===
“You’d better take the big wagonette,” said the old lady, in her soft voice. “A young girl just out from England is sure to have a great deal of luggage, you know. I wonder if she is anything like Mr. Grant. I hope her temper is a little bit better.”

===Chinese===
"你最好赶那辆大旅行车去，"老太太柔声说。"一个刚从英国来的年轻姑娘肯定带了好多行李。不知道她像不像格兰特先生。但愿她的脾气好一些。"

===Original===
“You’d better come down with me, Miss Harriott, to meet her,” said Hugh. “I don’t suppose your luggage would be a load there and back, anyhow.”

===Chinese===
"哈里奥特小姐，你最好跟我一起去接她，"休说。"反正你来回也带不了多少行李。"

===Original===
“What about crossing the river?” said the old lady.

===Chinese===
"过河怎么办？"老太太说。

===Original===
“Oh, we’ll get across somehow,” said Hugh, “will you come?”

===Chinese===
"哦，我们总有办法过去的，"休说，"你来吗？"

===Original===
“I think I’ll wait,” the young lady meditatively, “She’ll be tired from travelling and looking after her luggage, and she had better meet the family one at a time. You go and meet her, and your mother and I will get her room ready. Does the letter say any more about her?”

===Chinese===
"我想我还是等等吧，"年轻姑娘沉思着说，"她旅途劳顿，又要照看行李，最好先一个一个见家人。你去接她，我和你母亲把她的房间准备好。信里还说了关于她的什么吗？"

===Original===
“No, that’s all,” said Hugh. “Well, I’ll send the boy to run in the horses. I’ll take four horses in the big wagonette; I expect she’ll be waiting at Donohoe’s—that is, if she left the railway-station in the coach—if she is at Donohoe’s I’ll be back before dark.”

===Chinese===
"没了，就这些，"休说。"好，我叫孩子去把马牵来。大旅行车用四匹马；我估摸她会在多诺霍等着——就是说，如果她坐马车离开了火车站的话——如果她在多诺霍，天黑前我就能赶回来。"

===Original===
With this he went back to the office, and his mother and Miss Harriott went their separate ways to prepare for the comfort of the heiress. To Ellen Harriott the arrival was a new excitement, a change in the monotony of bush life; but to the old lady and Hugh it meant a great deal more. It meant that they would be no longer master and mistress of the big station on which they had lived so long, and which was now so much under their control that it seemed almost like their own.

===Chinese===
说完他回到办公室，他母亲和哈里奥特小姐各自去为这位女继承人的到来做准备。对埃伦·哈里奥特来说，这位继承人的到来是一种新鲜的兴奋，是枯燥的丛林生活中的一点变化；但对老太太和休来说，这意味深长。这意味着他们将不再是这座他们生活了这么久的大牧场的主人和女主人了——他们对这个牧场管控之深，几乎已经像自己的产业一样。

===Original===
Everything depended on what the girl was like. They had never even seen a photograph of her, and awaited her coming in a state of nervous expectancy. All over the district they had been practically considered owners of the big station; Hugh had taken on and dismissed employees at his will, had controlled the buying and selling of thousands of sheep and cattle, and now this strange girl was to come in with absolute power over them. They would be servants and dependants on the station, which had once belonged to them.

===Chinese===
一切取决于这姑娘是个什么样的人。他们连她的照片都没见过，在紧张的期待中等待着她的到来。在这整个地区，他们实际上一直被视为这座大牧场的主人；休可以随心所欲地雇佣和解雇工人，掌控着成千上万头羊和牛的买卖，而现在这个陌生的姑娘要来了，对他们拥有绝对的权力。他们将变成这座曾经属于他们的牧场的仆人和依附者。

===Original===
After Hugh had gone, the old lady sat back in her armchair and read over again her letter from Mr. Grant; and, lest it should be thought that that gentleman had only one side to his character, it is as well for the reader to know what was in the letter. It ran as follows:—

===Chinese===
休走后，老太太靠在扶手椅里，又把格兰特先生写给她的信读了一遍；为了让读者不至于以为这位先生性格中只有一面，还是让读者知道信中写了什么为好。信是这样的：

===Original===
> “Dear Mrs. Gordon,”
> “I am writing to you about a most important matter. Colonel Selwyn is dead, and my daughter has come out from England. I don’t know anyone to take charge of her except yourself. I am an old man now, and set in my ways, and this girl is really all I have to live for. Looking back on my life, I see where I have been a fool; and perhaps the good fortune that has followed me has been more luck than anything else. Your husband was a smarter man than I am, and he came to grief, though I will say that I always warned him against that Western place.
> “Do you remember the old days when we had the two little homesteads, and I used to ride down from the outstation of a Saturday and spend Sunday with you and Andrew, and talk over the fortunes we were going to make? If I had met a woman like you in those days I might have been a better man. As it was, I made a fool of myself. But that’s all past praying for.
> “Now about my girl. If you will take her, and make her as good a woman as yourself, or as near it as you can, you will earn my undying thanks. As to money matters, when I die she will of course have a great deal of money, so that it is well she should begin now to learn how to use it; I have, therefore, given her full power to draw all money that may be required. I may tell you that I intend to leave your boys enough to start them in life, and they will have a first-class chance to get on. I am sending Charlie out to the West, to take over a block which those fools, Sutton and Co., got me to advance money on, and on which the man cannot pay his interest. He will be away for some time.
> “Meanwhile, dear Mrs. Gordon, for the sake of old times, do what you can for the girl. I expect she has been brought up with English ideas. I can’t get her to say much to me, which I daresay is my own fault. After she has been with you for a bit, I will come up and stay for a time at the station.
> “Yours very truly,
> “W. G. Grant.”

===Chinese===
> "亲爱的戈登太太：
> "我写信给你，是要说一件极要紧的事。塞尔温上校（Colonel Selwyn）去世了，我女儿从英国来了。除了你，我不知道还能托付谁照顾她。我现在上了年纪，老习惯改不了了，这姑娘真是我活下去的全部指望。回想我的一生，我看到自己在哪里犯了傻；或许跟在我身后的那些好运，与其说是命中注定，不如说是运气使然。你丈夫比我精明，可他遭了难，不过我得说我一直警告他别去那个西部的地方。
> "你还记得当年我们有两座小宅子的时候吗？我周六从外围牧场骑马过来，和你、安德鲁（Andrew）一起过周日，谈论我们要发的大财？如果那时候遇到你这样的女人，我也许能成为一个更好的人。可事实上，我把自己搞成了一个傻瓜。但那些都已是追悔莫及的往事了。
> "现在说说我的女儿。如果你愿意收留她，把她教成像你一样好的女人，或者尽可能接近你，你将赢得我永恒的感激。至于钱的事，我死后她自然会拥有一大笔钱，所以她现在开始学习如何使用它也不为早；因此，我已经给了她全权，可以支取所需的一切费用。我还要告诉你，我打算留给你的儿子们足够的钱，让他们立足社会，他们将有一流的机会出人头地。我正派查理去西部，接管一片地——那些蠢货萨顿公司（Sutton and Co.）劝我预付了钱款，可那个人付不起利息。他要离开一段时间。
> "与此同时，亲爱的戈登太太，看在旧日情分上，对这姑娘尽你所能吧。我想她是在英国式教育下长大的。我没法让她跟我说多少话，这恐怕是我自己的错。等她在你那儿住一阵子，我会到牧场来住一段时间。
> "你非常忠实的，
> "W. G. 格兰特"

===Original===
Reading this letter called back the whole panorama of the past—the old days when she and her husband were struggling in the rough, hard, pioneering life, and the blacks were thick round the station; the birth of her children, and the ups and downs of her husband’s fortunes; then the burial of her husband out on the sandhills, and her flight to this haven of rest at Kuryong. Though she had lost interest in things for herself, she felt keenly for her children, and was sick at heart when she thought what this girl, who was to wield such power over them, might turn out to be. But she hoped that Grant’s daughter, whatever else she might be, would at any rate be a genuine, straightforward girl; and filled with this hope, she sat down to answer him:

===Chinese===
读完这封信，往昔的全景在她眼前一一浮现——她和丈夫在粗砺艰辛的拓荒岁月里打拼的那些日子，牧场周围到处是黑人；孩子们的降生，丈夫命运的起起落落；然后丈夫被安葬在沙丘上，她逃到了库里昂这个安身之所。虽然她自己已对世事不甚挂怀，但一想到这个即将对她的孩子们掌握如此大权的姑娘不知会是什么样子，她就揪心。不过她希望格兰特的女儿不管别的方面怎么样，至少是个真诚坦率的姑娘；带着这个希望，她坐下来给他回信：

===Original===
> “Dear Mr. Grant,” she wrote, “I have received your letter. Hugh has gone down to meet your daughter, but the mails were delayed owing to the river being up, and he may not get to the railway station as soon as she arrives. I will do what I can for her, and I thank you for what you say you will do for my boys. I will let you know the moment she arrives. I wish you would come up and live on the station for a time. It would be better for you than life in the club, without a friend to care for you. If ever you feel inclined to stay here for a time, I hope you will at once let me know. With thanks and best wishes,
> “Yours truly,
> “Annette Gordon.”

===Chinese===
> "亲爱的格兰特先生，"她写道，"来信收到。休已经去接你女儿了，但邮件因河水上涨而延误，他可能赶不到火车站那么早。我会尽我所能照顾她，也谢谢你答应为我的儿子们做的事。她一到我就通知你。我希望你能来牧场住一阵子。对你来说这比待在俱乐部里、没有一个朋友关心你要好得多。如果你什么时候想来住一段时间，希望你立刻告诉我。谨致谢意和良好祝愿。
> "你忠实的，
> "安妮特·戈登（Annette Gordon）"

## VI A Coach Accident / 马车事故

===Original===
The coach from Tarrong railway station to Emu Flat, and then on to Donohoe's Hotel, ran twice a week. Pat Donohoe was mailman, contractor and driver, and his admirers said that Pat could hit his five horses in more places at once than any other man on the face of the earth. His coach was horsed by the neighbouring squatters, through whose stations the road ran; and any horse that developed homicidal tendencies, or exhibited a disinclination to work, was at once handed over to the mailman to be licked into shape. The result was that, as a rule, Pat was driving teams composed of animals that would do anything but go straight, but under his handling they were generally persuaded, after a day or two, to settle down to their work.

===Chinese===
从塔龙（Tarrong）火车站到鸸鹋平地（Emu Flat），再到多诺霍旅馆的马车，每周跑两趟。帕特·多诺霍（Pat Donohoe）既是邮差、承包商，又是赶车人，崇拜他的人说，帕特同时鞭打五匹马的本事，世上没人比得上。他的马车由沿途经过的牧场主们提供马匹，哪匹马要是生出杀人的脾气，或是摆出不肯干活的架势，立马就被送给邮差去收拾。结果呢，帕特赶的通常是一群除了不走直路什么都干得出来的牲口，但在他的调教下，一般熬上一两天也就老实了。

===Original===
On the day when Hugh and Mrs. Gordon read Mr. Grant's letter at Kuryong, the train deposited at Tarrong a self-reliant young lady of about twenty, accompanied by nearly a truck-full of luggage—solid leather portmanteaux, canvas-covered bags, iron boxes, and so on—which produced a great sensation among the rustics. She was handsome enough to be called a beauty, and everything about her spoke of exuberant health and vitality. Her figure was supple, and she had the clear pink and white complexion which belongs to cold climates.

===Chinese===
就在休和戈登夫人在库里昂（Kuryong）读格兰特先生那封信的那天，火车在塔龙卸下了一位约莫二十岁的年轻姑娘，独立自主，身边跟着满满一车厢行李——结实的皮箱、帆布袋、铁箱子，等等——在当地乡民中引起了不小的轰动。她长得够漂亮，称得上美人，举手投足都透着旺盛的健康与活力。身段柔韧，肤色是那种寒冷气候里特有的白里透红。

===Original===
She seemed accustomed to being waited on, and watched without emotion the guard and the solitary railway official—porter, stationmaster, telegraph-operator and lantern-man, all rolled into one—haul her hundredweights of luggage out of the train. Then she told the perspiring stationmaster, etc., to please have the luggage sent to the hotel, and marched over to that building in quite an assured way, carrying a small handbag. Three commercial travellers, who had come up by the same趟 train, followed her off the platform, and the most gallant of the three winked at his friends, and then stepped up and offered to carry her bag. The young lady gave him a pleasant smile, and handed him the bag; together they crossed the street, while the other commercials marched disconsolately behind. At the door of the hotel she took the bag from her cavalier, and there and then, in broad Australian daylight, rewarded him with twopence—a disaster which caused him to apply to his firm for transfer to some foreign country at once. She marched into the bar, where Dan, the landlord's son, was sweeping, while Mrs. Connellan, the landlady, was wiping glasses in the midst of a stale fragrance of overnight beer and tobacco-smoke.

===Chinese===
她似乎习惯了被人服侍，不动声色地看着列车员和那个身兼搬运工、站长、电报员和提灯人的铁路员工，把她几百磅重的行李从车上拖下来。然后她请汗流浃背的站长把行李送到旅馆去，自己拎着小手提包，昂首阔步地走向旅馆。三个同车来的商务推销员跟在她后面下了站台，其中最殷勤的那个冲朋友们使了个眼色，然后上前主动要帮她提包。姑娘给了他一个愉快的微笑，把包递给了他；两人一起穿过街道，其余两个推销员闷闷不乐地跟在后面。到了旅馆门口，她从这位骑士手中拿过包，然后在光天化日之下赏了他两便士——这一灾难性打击让他立刻向公司申请调到外国去。她走进酒吧，店主的儿子丹正在扫地，老板娘康纳兰太太（Mrs. Connellan）在隔夜啤酒和烟草的陈腐气味中擦着杯子。

===Original===
"I am going to Kuryong," said the young lady, "and I expected to meet Mr. Gordon here. Is he here?"

Mrs. Connellan looked at her open-eyed. Such an apparition was not often seen in Tarrong. Mr. and Mrs. Connellan had only just "taken the pub," and what with trying to keep Connellan sober and refusing drinks to tramps, loafers, and blackfellows, Mrs. Connellan was pretty well worn out. As for making the hotel pay, that idea had been given up long ago. It was against Mrs. Connellan's instincts of hospitality to charge anyone for a meal or a bed, and when any great rush of bar trade took place it generally turned out to be "Connellan's shout," so the hotel was not exactly a goldmine. In fact, Mrs. Connellan had decided that the less business she did, the more money she would make; and she rather preferred that people should not stop at her hotel. This girl looked as if she would give trouble; might even expect clean beds and clean sheets when there were none within the hotel, and might object to fleas, of which there were plenty. So the landlady pulled herself together, and decided to speed the parting guest as speedily as possible.

===Chinese===
"我要去库里昂，"年轻姑娘说，"我本来指望在这里见到戈登先生。他在这儿吗？"

康纳兰太太瞪大眼睛看着她。塔龙不常见到这般人物。康纳兰夫妇刚"盘下这家酒馆"，一边要让康纳兰保持清醒，一边要对流浪汉、闲汉和黑鬼（blackfellows）拒不出酒，康纳兰太太已经精疲力竭。至于让旅馆赚钱，那个念头早就放弃了。康纳兰太太天生好客，向人收餐费住宿费有违她的天性，偶尔酒吧生意火爆，到头来也总是"康纳兰请客"，所以这家旅馆绝不是什么摇钱树。事实上，康纳兰太太已经认定，生意做得越少，挣得越多；她倒宁愿没人住她的旅馆。这姑娘看上去会惹麻烦——说不定会要求干净的床铺和床单，而旅馆里根本没有；说不定还会嫌跳蚤多，而跳蚤确实不少。于是老板娘打起精神，决定尽快送客。

===Original===
"Mr. Gordon couldn't git in," she said. "The cricks (creeks) is all up. The coach is going down to Kiley's Crossing today. You had better go with that."

"How soon does the coach start?"

"In an hour or two. As soon as Pat Donohoe, the mailman, has got a horse shod. Come in and have a wash, and fix yourself up till breakfast is ready. Where's your bag?"

"My luggage is at the railway-station."

"I'll send Dan over for it. Dan, Dan, Dan!"

"'Ello," said Dan's voice, from the passage, where, with the wild-eyed servant-girl, he had been taking stock of the new arrival.

"Go over to the station and git this lady's bag. Is there much to carry?"

"There are only four portmanteaux and three bags, and two boxes and a hatbox, and a roll of rugs; and please be careful of the hatbox."

"You'd better git the barrer, Dan."

"Better git the bloomin' bullock-dray," growled Dan, quite keen to see this aggregation of luggage; and foreseeing something to talk about for the next three months. "She must ha' come up to start a store, I reckon," said Dan; and off he went to struggle with boxes for the next half-hour or so.

===Chinese===
"戈登先生进不来，"她说，"河都涨了。马车今天改走基利渡口（Kiley's Crossing）。你最好搭那趟车。"

"马车什么时候出发？"

"一两个钟头吧。等邮差帕特·多诺霍给马钉好蹄铁就走。进来洗把脸，收拾一下，等早饭好了再走。你的包呢？"

"我的行李在火车站。"

"我让丹去拿。丹！丹！丹！"

"来了，"走廊那头传来丹的声音，他和那个眼神惊恐的女仆一直在窥视这位新来的客人。

"去火车站把这位女士的行李拿来。东西多吗？"

"也就四个手提箱、三个袋子、两个箱子加一个帽盒，外加一条毛毯；帽盒请小心点。"

"你最好推个手推车去，丹。"

"最好推那该死的牛车去，"丹嘟囔着，对这堆行李充满好奇，而且已经预见到接下来三个月都有得聊了。"我估摸她是来开店的，"丹说；然后就出发去跟箱子搏斗了，折腾了大约半个钟头。

===Original===
Over Mary Grant's experiences at the Tarrong Hotel we will not linger. The dirty water, peopled by wriggling animalculae, that she poured out of the bedroom jug; the damp, cloudy, unhealthy-smelling towel on which she dried her face; the broken window through which she could hear herself being discussed by loafers in the yard; all these things are matters of course in bush townships, for the Australian, having a soul above details, does not shine at hotel-keeping. The breakfast was enlivened by snatches of song from the big, good-natured bush-girl who waited at table, and who "fancied" her voice somewhat, and marched into the breakfast-room singing in an earsplitting Soprano:

"It's a vilet from me"—
(*Spoken.*) "What you'll have, there's chops, steaks, and bacon and eggs"—"Chops, please."
(*Singer continues.*) "Sainted mother's"—
(*Spoken.*) "Tea or coffee"—"Tea, please."
(*Singer finishes.*)—"grave."

===Chinese===
玛丽·格兰特在塔龙旅馆的经历我们就不多费笔墨了。从卧室水罐里倒出来的浑浊液体中游动着微小的生物；擦脸的毛巾又潮又黏，散发着不健康的气味；破窗户外面能听见院子里闲汉对她的评头论足——这些在丛林小镇都是家常便饭。澳大利亚人对细节向来不在意，经营旅馆自然也不在行。早餐时，那位身材魁梧、性情温和的丛林姑娘端菜送饭，时不时哼上几段歌，对自己的嗓子颇为得意，她大步走进餐厅，用刺耳的女高音唱道：

"这是来自我的一朵紫罗兰"——
（念白）"你们想吃点什么，有排骨、牛排、培根和鸡蛋"——"请来排骨。"
（继续唱）"圣母的"——
（念白）"茶还是咖啡"——"请来茶。"
（唱完）——"坟墓。"

===Original===
While she ate, Miss Grant had an uneasy feeling that she was being stared at; all the female staff and hangers-on of the place having gathered round the door to peer in at her and to appraise to the last farthing her hat, her tailor-made gown, and her solid English walking-shoes, and to indulge in wild speculation as to who or what she could be. A Kickapoo Indian in full war-paint, arriving suddenly in a little English village, could not have created more excitement than she did at Tarrong. After breakfast she walked out on the verandah that ran round the little one-story weatherboard hotel, and looked down the mile and-a-half of road, with little galvanised-iron-roofed cottages at intervals of a quarter of a mile or so, that constituted the township. She watched Conroy, the policeman, resplendent in breeches and polished boots, swagger out from the courthouse yard, leading his horse to water. The town was waking to its daily routine; Garry, the butcher, took down the clumsy board that passed for a window-shutter, and McDermott, the carter, passed the hotel, riding a huge rough-coated draught-horse, barebacked. Everyone gave him a "Mornin', Billy!" as he passed, and he returned the greeting as he did every morning of his life. A few children loitered past to the little schoolhouse, staring at her as though she were some animal.

===Chinese===
格兰特小姐吃饭时总觉得有人在盯着她看——旅馆所有的女员工和闲杂人等都挤在门口，把她的帽子、裁缝定制的连衣裙和结实的英式步行鞋估价到最后一分钱，然后肆意猜测她是什么来头。一个浑身涂满战漆的基卡普印第安人（Kickapoo Indian）突然出现在英国小村庄里，也不会像她在塔龙引起的轰动大。早饭后，她走到那座单层木板旅馆的游廊上，眺望那一英里半长的街道，每隔四分之一英里左右就有镀锌铁皮屋顶的小屋，构成了这个小镇的全貌。她看见警察康罗伊（Conroy）穿着马裤和锃亮的皮靴，牵着马去饮水，从法院院子里大摇大摆地走出来。小镇正从沉睡中醒来，开始一天的例行公事；屠夫加里（Garry）卸下充当窗板的笨重木板，赶车人麦克德莫特（McDermott）骑着一匹高大粗毛的挽马，光着马背经过旅馆。每个人都冲他喊"早上好，比利！"，他也像每天早晨一样回打招呼。几个孩子磨磨蹭蹭地走向小学校舍，用看动物的眼神盯着她。

===Original===
She was in a hurry to get away—English people always are—but in the bright lexicon of the bush there is no such word as hurry. Tracey, the blacksmith, had not by any means finished shoeing the coach-horse yet. So Mrs. Connellan made an attempt to find out who she was, and why she was going to Kuryong.

"You'll have a nice trip in the coach," she said. "Lier (lawyer) Blake's going down. He's a nice feller."

"Yes?"

"Father Kelly, too. He's good company."

"Yes?"

"Are you staying long at Kuryong?"

"Some time, I expect."

"Are you going to teach the children?"

"No, I'm going to live there. My father owns Kuryong. My father is Mr. Grant."

===Chinese===
她急着要走——英国人向来如此——但在丛林的明亮词典里根本没有"匆忙"这个词。铁匠特雷西（Tracey）给马车马钉蹄铁的活儿还远没干完。于是康纳兰太太试探着打听她的身份和去向。

"坐马车挺舒服的，"她说，"律师（lier/lawyer）布莱克也要下去。他是个好小伙儿。"

"是吗？"

"还有神父凯利（Father Kelly）。跟他在一起不闷。"

"是吗？"

"你要在库里昂待很久吗？"

"预计待一段时间。"

"你是去教孩子们吗？"

"不，我是去那里住的。我父亲拥有库里昂。我父亲就是格兰特先生。"

===Original===
Mrs. Connellan was simply staggered at this colossal treasure-trove, this majestic piece of gossip that had fallen on her like rain from Heaven. Mr. Grant's daughter! Going out to Kuryong! What a piece of news! Hardly knowing what she did, she shuffled out of the room, and interrupted the singing waitress who was wiping plates, and had just got back to "It's a vilet" when Mrs. Connellan burst in on her.

"Maggie! Maggie! Do you know who that is? Grant's daughter! The one that used to be in England. She must be going to Kuryong to live, with all that luggage. What'll the Gordons say? The old lady won't like it, will she? This'll be a bit of news, won't it?" And she went off to tell the cook, while Maggie darted to the door to meet Dan, and tell him.

Dan told the stationmaster when he went back for the next load, and when he had finished carting the luggage he got on a horse and went round telling everybody in the little town. The stationmaster told the ganger of the four navvies who went by on their trolly down the line to work. At the end of their four-mile length they told the ration-carrier of Eubindal station, who happened to call in at their camp for a drink of tea. He hurried off to the head-station with the news, and on his way told three teamsters, an inspector of selections, and a black boy belonging to Mylong station, whom he happened to meet on the road. Each of them told everybody that they met, pulling up and standing in their stirrups to discuss the matter in all its bearings, in the leisurely style of the bush; and wondering what she had come out for, whether the Gordons would get the sack from Kuryong, whether she would marry Hugh Gordon, whether she was engaged already, whether she was good-looking, how much money she had, and how much old Grant would leave her. In fact, before twenty-four hours were over, all the district knew of her arrival; which possibly explains how news travels in Africa among the Kaffirs, who are supposed to have a signalling system that no one has yet fathomed; but the way it gets round in Australia is just as wonderful as among the Kaffirs, in fact, for speed and thoroughness of information we should be inclined to think that our coloured brethren run a bad second.

===Chinese===
康纳兰太太被这个天大的发现惊呆了，这份从天而降的重磅八卦简直让她不知所措。格兰特先生的女儿！要去库里昂！多大的新闻！她几乎不知道自己在干什么，跌跌撞撞地走出房间，打断了那个边擦盘子边唱歌的女服务员——她刚唱回"这是来自我的一朵紫罗兰"，康纳兰太太就冲了进来。

"玛吉！玛吉！你知道那是谁吗？格兰特的女儿！就是那个一直在英国的。看那堆行李，肯定是去库里昂住的。戈登家的人会怎么说？老太太不会高兴的，对吧？这可是条大新闻，不是吗？"说完她就跑去告诉厨师了，玛吉则冲到门口去迎接丹，把消息告诉他。

丹在回去取第二批行李时告诉了站长，等行李搬完，他骑上马在小镇上转了一圈，逢人便说。站长告诉了在铁轨上坐着手推车去干活的四个筑路工的工头。这四英里路的尽头，他们又告诉了尤宾达尔（Eubindal）牧场的运粮工，那人碰巧来他们营地喝茶。他赶忙带着消息奔向主牧场，路上又告诉了三个赶车人、一个选地督察和一个梅隆（Mylong）牧场的黑人男孩，全是路上碰见的。每个人都停下马，踩着马鞍，在丛林人特有的从容中，把这事从头到尾议论个遍；猜她来干什么，戈登家会不会被赶出库里昂，她会不会嫁给休·戈登，是不是已经订婚了，长得好不好看，有多少钱，老格兰特会给她留多少遗产。总之，不到二十四小时，整个地区都知道她来了；这或许能解释非洲卡菲尔人（Kaffirs）的消息传播——据说他们有一套至今无人参透的信号系统；但在澳大利亚，消息传播的速度和彻底程度丝毫不逊于卡菲尔人，甚至可以说，我们的有色人种兄弟在这方面只能屈居第二。

===Original===
At last, however, Tracey had finished shoeing the coach-horse, and Miss Grant, with part of her luggage, took a seat on the coach behind five of Donohoe's worst horses, next to a well-dressed, powerfully-built man of about five-and-twenty. He looked and talked like a gentleman, and she heard the coachman address him as "Mr. Blake." She and he shared the box-seat with the driver, and just at the last moment the local priest hurried up and climbed on the coach. In some unaccountable way he had missed hearing who the young lady was, and for a time he could only look at her back-hair and wonder.

===Chinese===
不过特雷西终于钉好了马车马的蹄铁，格兰特小姐带着部分行李上了马车，坐在后排，旁边是一位衣着讲究、体格健壮的二十五六岁的男人。他言谈举止都像绅士，她听见赶车人叫他"布莱克先生"。她和他与赶车人一起坐在前座，就在最后一刻，当地的神父匆忙赶来爬上了马车。不知怎么回事，他没听说这位年轻姑娘的身份，一时间只能看着她的后脑勺发愣。

===Original===
It was not long before, in the free and easy Australian style, the passengers began to talk to each other as the coach bumped along its monotonous road—up one hill, through an avenue of dusty, tired-looking gum-trees, down the other side through a similar avenue, up another hill precisely the same as the last, and so on.

Blake was the first to make advances. "Not much to be seen on this sort of journey, Miss Grant," he said.

The young lady looked at him with serious eyes. "No," she said, "we've only seen two houses since we left the town. All the rest of the country seems to be a wilderness."

===Chinese===
没过多久，马车在单调的路上颠簸前行——翻过一座山丘，穿过一条尘土飞扬、无精打采的桉树林荫道，下了山坡又穿过一条差不多的林荫道，再爬上一座和前一座一模一样的山丘，如此循环——乘客们便按照澳大利亚人随和的做派，开始攀谈起来。

布莱克首先打破沉默。"这条路没什么风景可看，格兰特小姐，"他说。

年轻姑娘认真地看着他。"确实，"她说，"离开镇子以后只看到两栋房子。其余全是荒野。"

===Original===
Here the priest broke in. He was a broth of a boy from Maynooth, just the man to handle the Doyle and Donohoe congregation.

"It's the big stations is the roon of the country," he said. "How is the country to go ahead at all wid all the good land locked up? There's Kuryong on ahead here would support two hundthred fam'lies, and what does it employ now? Half a dozen shepherds, widout a rag to their back."

"I am going to Kuryong," said the girl; and the priest was silent.

===Chinese===
这时神父插话了。他是从梅努斯（Maynooth）出来的爽朗小伙子，正适合对付多尔家和多诺霍家这帮会众。

"大牧场就是这个国家的祸害，"他说，"好地都被圈起来了，国家还怎么往前走？前面的库里昂（Kuryong）养活两百户人家都不成问题，现在雇了多少人？六个牧羊人，连件像样的衣服都没有。"

"我要去库里昂，"姑娘说；神父沉默了。

===Original===
By four in the afternoon they reached Kiley's River, running yellow and froth-covered with melting snow. The coachman pulled his horses up on the bank, and took a good, long look at the bearings. As they waited, the Kuryong vehicle came down on the other side of the river.

"There's Mr. Gordon," said the coachman. "I don't think he'll try it. I reckon it's a trifle deep for me. Do you want to get across particular, Mr. Blake?"

"Yes, very particularly, Pat. I've told Martin Donohoe to meet me down here with some witnesses in a cattle-stealing case."

"What about you, Father Kelly?"

"I'm go'n on to Tim Murphy's dyin' bed. Put 'em into the wather, they'll take it aisy."

The driver turned to the third passenger. "It's a bit dangerous-like, Miss. If you like to get out, it's up to you to say so. The coach might wash over. There's a settler's place up the river a mile. You can go and stay there till the river goes down, and Mr. Gordon'll come and meet you."

"Thanks, I'll go on," said the lady.

===Chinese===
下午四点，他们到达基利河（Kiley's River），河水因融雪而泛黄，翻着泡沫。赶车人把马停在岸边，仔细打量了一下水情。正等着呢，库里昂的马车从河对岸驶了过来。

"那是戈登先生，"赶车人说，"我看他不敢过来。对我来说水也有点深。布莱克先生，你非过不可吗？"

"非过不可，帕特。我约了马丁·多诺霍（Martin Donohoe）带证人在这里碰头，一桩盗牛案。"

"你呢，神父凯利？"

"我要赶去蒂姆·墨菲（Tim Murphy）的临终床前。把他们赶下水去，它们会慢慢蹚的。"

赶车人转向第三位乘客。"有点危险，小姐。你要想下车，自己说一声就行。马车说不定会被冲走。上游一英里有户人家，你可以去那儿等水退了，戈登先生会来接你。"

"谢谢，我继续走，"姑娘说。

===Original===
Preparations for crossing the river were soon made. Anything that would spoil by getting wet, or that would float out of the coach, was lifted up and packed on the roof. The passengers stood up on the seats. Then Pat Donohoe put the whip on his leaders, and calling to his two wheelers, old-seasoned veterans, he put them at it.

Snorting and trembling, the leaders picked their way into the yellow water, the coach bumping over the rubble of the crossing-place. Hugh Gordon, watching from the far-side of the river, saw the coach dip and rock and plunge over the boulders. On it came till the water was actually lapping into the body of the coach, roaring and swirling round the horses' legs, up to their flanks and bellies, while the driver called out to them and kept them straight with voice and reins. Every spring he had a similar crossing, and he knew almost to an inch at what height it was safe to go into the river. But this time, as ill-luck would have it, the offside leader was a young, vicious, thoroughbred colt, who had been handed over to him to be cured of a propensity for striking people with his forefeet. As the horses worked their way into the river, the colt, with the courage of his breeding, pulled manfully, and breasted the current fearlessly. But suddenly a floating log drifted down, and struck him on the front legs. In an instant he reared up, and threw himself heavily sideways against his mate, bringing him to his knees; then the two of them, floundering and scrambling, were borne away with the current, dragging the coach after them. In a few yards they were off the causeway; the coach, striking deep water, settled like a boat, and turned over on its side, with the leaders swimming for their lives. As for the wheelers, they were pulled down with the vehicle, and were almost drowning in their harness.

===Chinese===
渡河的准备很快就绪。凡是一沾水就坏、或会从马车里漂走的东西，都搬上去捆在车顶。乘客们站在座位上。帕特·多诺霍鞭策着领头马，吆喝着两匹身经百战的辕老马，开冲了。

领头马打着响鼻、浑身发抖，试探着踏进泛黄的河水，马车在渡口的碎石上一路颠簸。休·戈登在河对岸看着，只见马车一路沉浮摇晃，碾过巨石。越往前走，河水越是漫进了车厢，轰鸣着、翻卷着冲刷马腿，直没到马腹和肚皮，赶车人一边呼喝一边用嗓门和缰绳控制方向。每年春天他都要经历这样的渡河，安全水位他精确到一英寸都差不了。但这一次运气不好，右边的领头马是匹年轻凶悍的纯血小公马，被送来调教它用前蹄踢人的毛病。马匹涉水前进，小公马凭着血统的勇气奋力拉车，无惧急流。突然一根漂浮的原木冲下来撞上了它的前腿。它瞬间人立起来，重重地撞向旁边的同伴，把那匹马撞得前腿跪地；两匹马在水中挣扎翻滚，被急流冲走，连带着把马车也拖了过去。没几码它们就偏离了石滩；马车陷入深水，像船一样沉下去，侧翻了，领头马拼命游泳保命。辕马则被车体拖住，几乎在挽具中溺毙。

===Original===
Cool as a cucumber, Blake had turned to the girl. "Can you swim?" he said. And she answered him as cooly, "Yes, a little."

"Well, put your hands on my shoulders, and leave everything to me." Just then the coach settled over with one final surge, and they were in the water.

Away they went with the roaring current, the girl clinging fast to his shoulders, while he gave his whole attention to dodging the stumps and snags that were showing their formidable teeth above water. For a while she was able to hold on. Then, with a sickening sense of helplessness, she felt herself torn from him, and whirled away like a leaf. The rank smell of the muddy water was in her nostrils, the fear of death in her heart. She struggled to keep afloat. Suddenly a blood-streaked face appeared, and Blake, bleeding from a cut on the forehead, caught her with a strong grip and drew her to him. A few more seconds of whirling chaos, and she felt land under her feet, and Blake half-carrying her to the bank. They had been swept on to one of the many sandbanks which ran out into the stream, and were safe.

===Chinese===
布莱克像没事人似的转向姑娘。"你会游泳吗？"他问。她也同样冷静地回答："会一点。"

"好，把手搭在我肩上，其余的交给我。"就在这时，马车猛地一侧翻，他们落了水。

两人被咆哮的急流卷走，她死死抓住他的肩膀，他则全神贯注地躲避水面上露出的狰狞树桩。有那么一会儿她还抓得住。接着，一阵令人作呕的无力感袭来，她感觉自己被撕开，像树叶一样被卷走。浑浊的水腥味灌满了鼻孔，死亡的恐惧攥住了她的心。她拼命挣扎着浮出水面。突然，一张满是血污的脸出现了——布莱克额头上淌着血，却一把抓住了她，用力将她拉向自己。又过了几秒钟天旋地转，她感觉脚下踩到了实地，布莱克半扶半抱着她上了岸。他们被冲到了伸入河中的一片沙洲上，脱险了。

===Original===
Half-hysterical, she sat down on a huge log, and waited while Blake ran upstream to give help to the coachman. While the two had been battling in the water, the priest had stayed with the coachman to cut the horses free, till at last all four got clear of the wreck, and swam ashore. Then the men followed them, drifting down the current and fighting their way to shore at about the same place.

Hugh Gordon drove the wagonette down to pick up the party when they landed. The scene on the bank would have made a good picture. The horses, dripping with water and shaking with cold, were snorting and staring, while the coachman was trying to fix up some gear out of the wreck, so that he could ride one of them. The priest, his broad Irish face ornamented by a black clay pipe, was tramping up and down in his wet clothes. Blake was helping Miss Grant to wring the water out of her clothes, and she was somewhat incoherently trying to thank him. As Hugh drove up, Blake looked up and caught his eye, and there flashed between the two men an unmistakable look of hostility. Then Hugh jumped from the wagonette, and walked up to Miss Grant, holding out his hand.

===Chinese===
她半歇斯底里地坐在一根大圆木上，等着布莱克跑去上游帮助车夫。在两人水里搏斗的时候，神父一直留在车夫身边帮忙解马，直到四匹马全部脱险，游上了岸。男人们随后也顺流而下，在差不多同一个地方挣扎着上了岸。

休·戈登驾着轻便马车来接他们。河岸上的场景活像一幅画：马匹浑身滴水、冻得发抖，打着响鼻，瞪着眼睛；车夫从残骸里翻找装备，想凑出一套马具骑一匹马回去；神父宽宽的爱尔兰脸庞上叼着黑泥烟斗，穿着湿衣服来回踱步；布莱克正帮格兰特小姐拧衣服上的水，她语无伦次地向他道谢。休驱车过来时，布莱克抬起头，两人的目光相接，彼此间闪过一丝毫不掩饰的敌意。然后休从马车上跳下来，走向格兰特小姐，伸出了手。

===Original===
"I'm Hugh Gordon," he said. "We only got your father's letter today, or I would have been down to meet you. I hope you are not hurt. Jump into the trap, and I'll run down to the Donohoes', and get you some dry things." Then, turning to Blake, he said somewhat stiffly, "Will you get in, Mr. Blake?"

"Thanks," said Blake, equally stiffly, "I can ride one of the mail horses. It's no distance. I wont trouble you."

But the girl turned and put her hand into Blake's, and spoke with the air of a queen.

"I am very much obliged to you—more than I can tell you. You have saved my life. If ever I can do anything to repay you I will."

"Oh, nonsense," said Blake, "that's nothing. It was only a matter of dodging the stumps. You'd better get on now to Donohoe's Hotel, and get Mrs. Donohoe to find some dry things for you."

The mere fact of his refusing a lift showed that there was some hostility between himself and Hugh Gordon; but the priest, who had climbed into the Kuryong vehicle as a matter of course, settled the matter offhand.

"Get in the trap," he said. "Get in the trap, man. What's the use for two of ye to ride the mail horses, and get your death o' cold? Get in the trap!"

"Of course I'll give you a lift," said Hugh. "Jump in, and let us get away before you all get colds. What will you do about the coach and the luggage, Pat?"

"I'll borry them two old draught horses from Martin Donohoe, and they'll haul it out. Bedad, some o' that luggage'll be washed down to the Murrumbidgee before night; but the most of it is strapped on. Push along, Mr. Gordon, and tell Martin I'm coming."

===Chinese===
"我是休·戈登（Hugh Gordon），"他说，"今天才收到你父亲的信，不然我早就来接你了。希望你没受伤。上车吧，我送你去多诺霍家，给你找些干衣服换。"然后他转向布莱克，语气有些生硬地说，"布莱克先生，要上来吗？"

"谢谢，"布莱克同样生硬地回答，"我骑匹邮马就行。没多远，不麻烦你了。"

但姑娘转过身，把手伸到布莱克手中，以女王般的气度说：

"我非常感激你——感激不尽。你救了我的命。将来只要我能报答你的，一定做到。"

"哦，哪里的话，"布莱克说，"不算什么。不过是躲躲树桩的事。你现在最好去多诺霍旅馆，让多诺霍太太给你找些干衣服换。"

他拒绝搭车，足以说明他和休·戈登之间存在某种敌意；但神父已经理所当然地爬上了库里昂的马车，三言两语就把事情定了。

"上车，"他说，"上车，伙计。你们两个骑邮马，冻出病来有什么用？上车！"

"我当然要捎你一程，"休说，"上车吧，趁你们还没着凉赶紧走。帕特，马车和行李怎么办？"

"我找马丁·多诺霍借那两匹老挽马，拉出来就是了。老天，有些行李天黑前会被冲到默伦比奇河（Murrumbidgee）去；但大部分都绑结实了。走吧，戈登先生，告诉马丁我随后就到。"

===Original===
With some reluctance Blake got into the wagonette; before long they were at Donohoe's Hotel, and Mary Grant was soon rigged out in an outfit from Mrs. Donohoe's best clothes—a pale-green linsey bodice and purple skirt—everything, including Mrs. Donohoe's boots, being about four sizes too big. But she looked by no means an unattractive little figure, with her brown eyes and healthy colour showing above the shapeless garments.

She came into the little sitting-room laughing at the figure she cut, sat down, and drank scalding tea, and ate Mrs. Donohoe's cakes, while talking with Father Kelly and Blake over the great adventure.

When she was ready to start she got into the wagonette alongside Hugh, and waved goodbye to the priest and Blake and Mrs. Donohoe, as though they were old friends. She had had her first touch of colonial experience.

===Chinese===
布莱克有些勉强地上了马车；不一会儿他们就到了多诺霍旅馆，玛丽·格兰特很快穿上了多诺霍太太最好的行头——一件浅绿色毛料紧身上衣和一条紫色长裙——包括多诺霍太太的靴子在内，所有东西都大了大约四号。但配上她的棕色眼睛和健康的气色从不成形的衣服上方露出来，这个小人儿看起来一点也不难看。

她走进小客厅，为自己的模样发笑，坐下来喝着滚烫的茶，吃着多诺霍太太的蛋糕，一边和神父凯利、布莱克聊着这番惊天历险。

等她准备好出发时，她坐到休旁边的马车上，向神父、布莱克和多诺霍太太挥手告别，仿佛他们是老朋友一般。她尝到了殖民地生活的第一道滋味。

## VII Mr. Blake's Relations / VII 布莱克先生的亲戚

===Original===
As soon as Hugh got his team swinging along at a steady ten miles an hour on the mountain road, Mary Grant opened the conversation.
"Mr. Gordon," she said, "who is Mr. Blake?"
"He's the lawyer from Tarrong."
"Yes, I know. Mrs. Connellan called him the 'lier.' But I thought you didn't seem to like him. Isn't he nice?"
"I suppose so. His father was a gentleman—the police magistrate up here."

===Chinese===
休刚把马队赶上山路，稳稳当当地跑起十英里时速，玛丽·格兰特（Mary Grant）就打开了话匣子。
"戈登先生，"她说，"布莱克先生（Mr. Blake）是什么人？"
"他是塔龙（Tarrong）的律师。"
"嗯，我知道。康纳兰太太（Mrs. Connellan）管他叫'骗子'。可我觉得你好像不太喜欢他。他不好吗？"
"还行吧。他父亲是个体面人——是这边的治安法官。"

===Original===
"Then, why don't you like him? Is there anything wrong about him?"
Hugh straightened his leaders and steadied the vehicle over a little gully.
"There's nothing wrong about him," he said, "only—his mother was one of the Donohoes—not a lady, you know—and he always goes with those people; and, of course, that means he doesn't go much with us."
"Why not?"
"Well, you see, they're selectors, and they look on the station people as—well, rather against them, you know—sort of enemies—and he has never come to the station. But there is no reason why he shouldn't."

===Chinese===
"那你为什么不喜欢他？他有什么问题吗？"
休拉直了领头马的缰绳，稳稳当当地把车驶过一道小沟。
"他本人没什么问题，"他说，"只是——他母亲是多诺霍家（Donohoes）的人——你知道，不是什么淑女——他老跟那些人来往；那自然就意味着跟我们这边走动不多。"
"为什么不行？"
"你看，那些人是自耕地主（selectors），他们看牧场（station）上的人——嗯，觉得跟他们对着干，你知道——有点像是敌人——他从来没来过牧场。不过也没什么理由不可以来。"

===Original===
"He saved my life," said Mary Grant.
"Certainly he did," said Hugh. "I'll say that for Blake, he fears nothing. One of the pluckiest men alive. And how did you feel? Were you much frightened?"
"Yes, horribly. I have often wondered whether I should be brave, you know, and now I don't think I am. Not the least bit. But Mr. Blake seemed so strong—directly he caught hold of me I felt quite safe, somehow. If you don't mind, I would like to ask him out to the station."
"Certainly, Miss Grant. My mother will only be too glad. She was sorry that we did not get down to meet you. The letter was delayed."

===Chinese===
"他救了我的命，"玛丽·格兰特说。
"那倒是，"休说。"布莱克这点我服——他什么都不怕。活着的人里头数他胆子最大。你当时什么感觉？吓坏了吗？"
"是的，怕得要命。你知道我常常想自己会不会勇敢，现在我觉得自己一点也不勇敢，一点都不。可布莱克先生好像特别强壮——他一抓住我，不知怎的我就觉得安全了。你要是不介意的话，我想请他到牧场来。"
"当然可以，格兰特小姐。我母亲求之不得。她很遗憾我们没能下来接你。信被耽搁了。"

===Original===
Mary Grant laughed as she looked down at Mrs. Donohoe's clothes. "What a sight I am!" she said.
"But, after all, it's Australia, isn't it? And I have had such adventures already! You know you will have to show me all about the station and the sheep and cattle. Will you do that?"
Hugh thought there was nothing in the world he would like better, but contented himself with a formal offer to teach her the noble art of squatting.
"You must begin at once and tell me things. What estate are we on now?" she asked.

===Chinese===
玛丽·格兰特低头看了看多诺霍太太（Mrs. Donohoe）的衣服，笑了起来。"看看我这副模样！"她说。
"不过，毕竟这是澳大利亚嘛，对吧？而且我已经有了这么多冒险！你知道你得带我去看看牧场，看看羊群和牛群。你愿意吗？"
休觉得这世上再没有比这更让他乐意的事了，但只是客客气气地提出教她大牧场主（squatter）的崇高技艺。
"你现在就得开始给我讲。我们现在是在谁的地盘上？"她问。

===Original===
"This is your father's station. All you can see around belongs to him; but after the next gate we come on some land held by selectors."
"Who are they?"
"Well," said Hugh, a little awkwardly, "they are relations of Mr. Blake's. You'll see what an Australian farmer's homestead is like."
They drove through a rickety wire-and-sapling gate and across about a mile of bush, and suddenly came on a little slab house nestling under the side of a hill. At the back were the stockyards and the killing-pen, where a contrivance for raising dead cattle—called a gallows—waved its arms to the sky. In front of the house there was rather a nice little garden. At the back were a lot of dilapidated sheds, leaning in all directions. A mob of sheep was penned in a yard outside one of the sheds; and in the garden an old woman, white-haired and wrinkled, with a very short dress showing a lot of dirty stocking and slipshod elastic-sided boot, was bending over a spade, digging potatoes.

===Chinese===
"这是你父亲的牧场。你能看到的四周都属于他；但过了下一道门，就是一些自耕地主（selectors）的地了。"
"他们是谁？"
"嗯，"休有些尴尬地说，"他们是布莱克先生的亲戚。你会看到一个澳大利亚农民的家是什么样子。"
他们穿过一道摇摇晃晃的铁丝木桩门，走过大约一英里灌木丛，突然看见一栋小木板房依偎在山坡下。屋后是牲口围栏和宰杀栏，一个用来吊起死牛的装置——叫作绞架——高举着双臂指向天空。屋前有一个相当不错的小花园。屋后是一大堆歪歪斜斜的破棚子，朝各个方向倾斜。一群羊被圈在一个棚子外面的围场里；花园里，一个白发苍苍、满脸皱纹的老太太，穿着极短的裙子，露出一截脏兮兮的长袜和一双松松垮垮的松紧口便鞋，正弯腰用铁锹挖土豆。

===Original===
The old woman straightened herself as they drove up.
"Good daah to you, Misther Gordon," she said. "Good daah to you, Miss."
"Good day, Mrs. Doyle," said Hugh. "Hard work that, this weather. How's all the family?"
"Mag—Marg'rut, I mane—she's inside. That's her playin' the pianny. She just got it up from Sydney."
"And where's Peter?"
"Peter's shearin' the sheep. He's in that shed there beyant. He's the only shearer we have, so we tell him he's the ringer of the shed. He works terr'ble hard, does Peter. He's not—" and the old woman dropped her voice—"he's not all there in the head, is Peter, you know."

===Chinese===
老太太看到他们的车过来，直起了腰。
"日安，戈登先生，"她说。"日安，小姐。"
"你好，多尔太太（Mrs. Doyle），"休说。"这种天气干活可不轻省。一家人都好吗？"
"玛吉（Mag）——我是说玛格丽特（Marg'rut）——她在屋里。就是她在弹钢琴，刚从悉尼运来的。"
"彼得（Peter）呢？"
"彼得在剪羊毛（shearing）。就在那边那个棚子里。他是我们唯一的剪毛工，所以我们都叫他棚里的头牌（ringer）。他干活可拼命了，彼得。他不——"老太太压低了声音，"——他脑子不太灵光，你知道，彼得。"

===Original===
"And where's Mick?"
"Mick, bad scran to him! He's bought a jumpin' haarse (horse), and he's gone to hell leppin! Down at one of the shows he is, some place. He has too much sense to work, has Mick. Won't you come in and have a cup of tay?"
"No, we must get on, thank you," and Hugh and Mary drove off, watched by the old lady and the lanky-legged, shock-headed youth—Peter himself—who came to the door of the big shed to stare at them.

===Chinese===
"米克（Mick）呢？"
"米克，让他见鬼去吧！他买了匹跳栏马，跑去参加赛马会了！在哪个地方的展会上呢。他太聪明了，不肯干活，米克。你们进来喝杯茶吧？"
"不了，我们得赶路，谢谢，"休和玛丽驱车离去，老太太和那个瘦腿蓬头的年轻人——彼得本人——站在大棚门口目送他们。

===Original===
As they drove off Hugh was silent, wondering what effect the sight of the selectors might have had on Miss Grant.
She seemed to read his thoughts, and after a little while she spoke.
"So those are Mr. Blake's poor relations, are they? Well, that is not his fault. My father was poor once, just as poor as those people are. And Mr. Blake saved my life."
Hugh felt that she was half-consciously putting him in the wrong for having more or less disapproved of Mr. Blake; so he kept silence.

===Chinese===
驱车离去时，休沉默不语，不知道这些自耕地主（selectors）给格兰特小姐留下了什么印象。
她似乎读懂了他的心思，过了一会儿开口说道。
"所以那些就是布莱克先生的穷亲戚，是吗？不过那不是他的错。我父亲以前也穷过，跟那些人一样穷。布莱克先生可是救过我的命。"
休觉得她在有意无意地暗示他不该对布莱克先生有意见，所以沉默不语。

===Original===
As the team bore them along at a flying trot, they climbed higher and higher up the range; at last, as they rounded a shoulder of the hillside, the whole valley of Kiley's River lay beneath them, stretching away to the far blue foothills. Beyond again was a great mountain, its top streaked with snow. At their feet was a gorgeous scheme of colour, greens and greys of the grass, bright tints of willow and poplar, and the speckled forms of the cattle, so far down that they looked like pygmy stock feeding in fairy paddocks. Across the valley there came now and again, softened by distance, the song of the river; and up in the river-bend, on a spur of the hills, were white walls rising from clustered greenery.
"How beautiful!" said the girl, half standing up in the wagonette, "and is that—"
"That's Kuryong, Miss Grant. Your home station."

===Chinese===
马队飞速小跑着载他们越爬越高，翻上山脊；终于绕过山腰的一道肩梁，基利河（Kiley's River）的整个山谷展现在他们脚下，一直延伸到远方蓝色的丘陵。再远处是一座大山，山顶残雪斑驳。他们脚下是一幅绚丽的色彩画卷——草场的青绿与灰绿，柳树和白杨的明艳色调，还有花斑点点的牛群，因为太远，看上去就像童话牧场里进食的微型牲畜。山谷那边不时传来河水的歌声，被距离柔化了；在河湾处，山脊上的白墙从一片葱翠中拔地而起。
"多美啊！"姑娘半站在马车里说，"那是——"
"那是库里昂（Kuryong），格兰特小姐。你的家，你的牧场。"

## VIII At the Homestead / 庄园

===Original===
Miss Grant's arrival at Kuryong homestead caused great excitement among the inhabitants. Mrs. Gordon received her in a motherly way, trying hard not to feel that a new mistress had come into the house; she was anxious to see whether the girl exhibited any signs of her father's fiery temper and imperious disposition. The two servant-girls at the homestead﻿—great herculean, good-natured bush-girls, daughters of a boundary-rider, whose highest ideal of style and refinement was Kuryong drawing-room﻿—breathed hard and stared round-eyed, like wild fillies, at the unconscious intruder. The station-hands﻿—Joe, the wood-and-water boy, old Alfred the groom, Bill the horse-team driver, and Harry Warden the married man, who helped with sheep, mended fences, and did station-work in general﻿—all watched for a sight of her. They exchanged opinions about her over their smoke at night by the huge open fireplace in the men's hut, where they sat in a semicircle, toasting their shins at the blaze till their trousers smoked again, each man with a pipe of black tobacco going full swing from tea till bedtime. But the person who felt the most intense excitement over the arrival of the heiress was Miss Harriott.

===Chinese===
格兰特小姐到达库里昂（Kuryong）庄园时，在庄园上下引起了一阵巨大的骚动。戈登太太以母亲般的姿态接待了她，努力不去想家里来了一个新的女主人；她急切地想看看这姑娘是否表现出她父亲那暴烈的脾气和专横的性情。庄园里的两个女仆——身材魁梧、性情爽朗的丛林姑娘，一个边境巡栏人的女儿，在她们心目中，优雅精致的最高典范就是库里昂的客厅——像两匹受惊的小母马一样，喘着粗气、瞪大了眼睛，盯着这个浑然不觉的闯入者。牧场（station）的工人们——负责柴火和用水的乔、老马夫阿尔弗雷德、赶马车的比尔，以及已婚的哈里·沃登（Harry Warden，帮忙照看羊群、修篱笆、干牧场杂活）——都在等着看她一眼。晚上他们在工人小屋那个巨大的敞开式壁炉旁，一边抽烟一边议论她，围坐成半圆形，对着炉火烤小腿，直到裤子都冒了烟，每人一管黑烟丝从晚饭后一直抽到就寝。但对女继承人的到来感到最强烈兴奋的，莫过于哈里奥特小姐（Miss Harriott）。

===Original===
For all her nurse's experience, Ellen Harriott was not a woman of the world. Except for the period of her hospital training, she had passed all her life shut up among the mountains. Her dreamworld was mostly constructed out of high-class novels, and she united a shrewd wit and a clever brain to a dense ignorance of the real world, that left her like a ship without a rudder. She was, like most bush-reared girls, a great visionary﻿—many a castle-in-the-air had she built while taking her daily walk by the river under the drooping willows. The visions, curiously enough, always took the direction of magnificence. She pictured herself as a leader of society, covered with diamonds, standing at the head of a broad marble staircase and receiving Counts by the dozen (vide Ouida's novels, read by stealth); or else as a rich man's wife who dispensed hospitality regally, and was presented at Court, and set the fashion in dress and jewels. At the back of all her dreams there was always a man﻿—a girl's picture is never complete without a man﻿—a strong, masterful man, whose will should crush down opposition, and whose abilities should make his name﻿—and incidentally her name﻿—famous all over the world. She herself, of course, was always the foremost figure, the handsomest woman, the best-dressed, the most admired; for Ellen Harriott, though only a girl, and a friendless governess at Kuryong, was not inclined to put herself second to anyone. Having learnt from her father's papers that he was of an old family, she considered herself anybody's equal. Her brain held a crazy enough jumble of ideas, no doubt; but given a strong imagination, no experience, and omnivorous reading, a young girl's mind is exactly the place where fantastic ideas will breed and multiply.

===Chinese===
尽管做过护士，埃伦·哈里奥特（Ellen Harriott）却并非世故之人。除了在医院受训的那段日子，她一辈子都窝在群山之间。她的梦幻世界大多是用上流小说搭建起来的，聪明的头脑和敏锐的机智与对真实世界的极度无知混在一起，让她像一条没有舵的船。和大多数在丛林中长大的姑娘一样，她是个爱做白日梦的人——多少座空中楼阁，都是在河边垂柳下每日散步时筑起来的。说来也怪，她的幻梦总是朝着辉煌的方向。她把自己想象成社交界的领袖，满身钻石，站在宽阔的大理石楼梯顶端，成打成打地接见伯爵们（参见她偷偷读的奥伊达小说）；或者是一个富有的妻子，以王者之风广施款待，被引见入宫，在服饰和珠宝上引领风尚。她所有梦想的底色里始终有一个人——一个姑娘的画卷里要是没有男人就不完整——一个强悍、威严的男人，他的意志能压倒一切反对，他的才干能使他的名字——顺便也使她的名字——闻名天下。她自己当然永远是最突出的人物，最漂亮的女人，衣着最考究，最受人仰慕。因为埃伦·哈里奥特虽然只是个姑娘，在库里昂当着一个孤零零的家庭教师，却天生不甘居于人后。从父亲的文件中得知他出身名门之后，她便觉得自己配得上任何人。她的脑中无疑塞满了一堆荒唐的念头；不过，一个想象力旺盛、毫无阅历、又什么书都读的年轻姑娘的头脑，恰恰是各种奇思妙想滋生繁衍的温床。

===Original===
She went about with Mrs. Gordon to the small festivities of the district, and was welcomed everywhere, and deferred to by the local settlers; she had yet to know what a snub meant; so the world to her seemed a very easy sort of place to get along in. The coming of the heiress was as light over a trackless ocean. Here was someone who had seen, known, and done all the things which she herself wished to see, know, and do; someone who had travelled on the Continent, tobogganed in Switzerland, ridden in Rotten Row, voyaged in private yachts, hunted in the shires; here was the world at last come to her door﻿—the world of which she had read so much and knew so little.

===Chinese===
她跟着戈登太太出入本地区的小型社交活动，到处都受欢迎，当地的定居者们都对她恭敬有加；她还不知道什么叫碰壁；所以世界在她看来是一个非常好混的地方。女继承人的到来，就像光照进了一片没有航迹的海洋。眼前这个人，见过、知道、做过所有她自己渴望去见、去知、去做的事；这个人游历过欧洲大陆，在瑞士坐过雪橇，在伦敦的罗登街骑过马，乘私人游艇出过海，在各郡猎过狐——世界终于来到了她的门前——那个她读了无数书却知之甚少的世界。

===Original===
On the second morning after Miss Grant's arrival, that young lady turned up at breakfast in a tailor-made suit with short skirt and heavy boots, and announced her intention of "walking round the estate;" but as Kuryong﻿—though only a small station, as stations go﻿—was, roughly, ten miles square, this project had to be abandoned. Then she asked Hugh if he would have the servants mustered. He told her that the two servants were in the kitchen, but it turned out that she wanted to interview all the station hands, and it had to be explained that the horse-driver was six miles out on the run with his team, drawing in a load of bark to roof the hay shed, and that Harry Warden was down at the drafting yards, putting in a new trough to hold an arsenical solution, through which the sheep had to tramp to cure their feet; and that everybody else was away out on some business or other. But the young lady stuck to her point, and had the groom and the wood-and-water boy paraded, they being the only two available. The groom was an English importation, and earned her approval by standing in a rigid and deferential attitude, and saying "Yes, Miss," and "No, Miss," when spoken to; but the wood-and-water boy stood with his arms akimbo and his mouth open, and when she asked him how he liked being on the station he said, "Oh, it's not too bad," accompanying his remark with a sickly grin that nearly earned him summary dismissal.

===Chinese===
格兰特小姐到达的第二天早上，这位年轻女士穿了一身裁剪得体的短裙套装和厚底靴来吃早饭，宣布要"在庄园里走一圈"；但库里昂——虽然在牧场中只算小的——方圆大约有十英里，这个计划只好作罢。然后她问休能不能把仆人们集合起来。他告诉她两个仆人都在厨房里，结果她是要面试所有的牧场工人。于是不得不解释说赶马车的带着车队在六英里外的牧场上，运木皮回来给干草棚搭顶；哈里·沃登在分栏场里，正安放一个新的药浴槽，里面装的是砷溶液，羊群得从里面趟过去治蹄病；其他所有人都各有各的事出去了。但这位年轻女士坚持己见，于是把马夫和负责柴水的男孩叫来列队——这是仅有的两个闲人。马夫是个从英国来的，规规矩矩、恭恭敬敬地站着，问话时只会答"是，小姐"和"不，小姐"，这赢得了她的赞许；可那个柴水男孩双手叉腰、张着嘴站着，她问他在牧场干得怎么样，他说"哦，还行吧"，还配了一个讨好似的苦笑，差点被当场辞退。

===Original===
The young lady returned to the house in rather a sharp temper, and found Hugh standing by a cart, which had just got back with her shipwrecked luggage.

"Well, Miss Grant," he said, "the things are pretty right. The water went down in an hour or so, and the luggage on the top only got a little wetting﻿—just a wave now and again. How have you been getting on?"

"Not at all well," she laughed. "I don't understand the people here. I will get you to take me round before I do another thing. It is so different from England. Are you sure my clothes are all right?"

"I can't be sure, of course, but you can unpack them as soon as you like."

===Chinese===
年轻女士带着一肚子火气回到屋里，发现休站在一辆马车旁——马车刚把她那些遭了水难的行李运回来。

"格兰特小姐，"他说，"东西基本没事。水大概一个小时就退了，放在上面的行李只是沾了一点湿——就是偶尔一个浪头打过来。你那边怎么样？"

"一点都不好，"她笑着说。"我不了解这里的人。你得先带我转转，别的事以后再说。跟英格兰完全不一样。我的衣服真没问题吗？"

"当然不能打包票，不过你想拆包随时可以拆。"

===Original===
It was not long before the various boxes were opened. Ellen Harriott was called in to assist, and the two girls had a real good afternoon, looking at and talking over clothes and jewellery. The things had come fairly well out of the coach disaster. When an English firm makes a watertight cover for a bag or box, it *is* watertight; even the waters of Kiley's River had swept over the canvas of Miss Grant's luggage in vain. And when the sacred boxes were opened, what a treasure-trove was unveiled!

The noblest study of mankind is man, but the most fascinating study of womankind is another woman's wardrobe, and the Australian girl found something to marvel at in the quality of the visitor's apparel. Dainty shoes, tailor-made jackets, fashionable short riding-habits, mannish-looking riding-boots, silk undergarments, beautiful jewellery, all were taken out of their packages and duly admired. As each successive treasure was produced, Ellen Harriott's eyes grew rounder with astonishment; and when, out of a travelling bag, there appeared a complete dressing-table outfit of silverware﻿—silver-backed hairbrushes, silver manicure set, silver handglass, and so forth﻿—she drew a long breath of wonder and admiration.

It was her first sight of the vanities of the world, the things that she had only dreamed of. The outfit was not anything extraordinary from an English point of view, but to the bush-bred girl it was a revelation.

===Chinese===
箱子很快都打开了。埃伦·哈里奥特被叫来帮忙，两个姑娘度过了一个名副其实的愉快下午，翻看着衣服和珠宝，一边看一边聊。这些东西从马车事故中完好地保存了下来。英国公司制作的防水包套，那是真的*防水*；基利河（Kiley's River）的水冲刷过格兰特小姐行李上的帆布也是徒劳。当那些神圣的箱子打开时，展现在眼前的简直是宝藏！

研究人类最高尚的课题是研究人本身，但对女人来说最迷人的话题莫过于另一个女人的衣橱。这位澳大利亚姑娘对访客衣装的品质惊叹不已。精致的鞋子、裁剪合身的短上衣、时髦的短骑装、男式风格的骑马靴、丝绸内衣、精美的珠宝，一一从包装中取出，被端详赞叹。每一件新宝贝亮相，埃伦·哈里奥特的眼睛就睁得更圆；当一只旅行袋里掏出一整套银制梳妆用品——银背发刷、银制美甲套装、银柄手镜等等——她发出了一声由衷的惊叹。

这是她第一次目睹世俗的虚荣之物，那些她只在梦中见过的东西。从英国人的角度来看，这套装备算不上什么稀罕物，但对在丛林中长大的姑娘来说，这简直是天启。

===Original===
"What beautiful things!" she said. "Now, when you go visiting to a country-house in England, do you always take things like these, all these riding-boots and things?"

"Oh, yes. You wouldn't ride without them."

"And do you take a maid to look after them?"

"Well, you must have a maid."

"And when you travel on the Continent, do you take a maid?"

"I always took one."

"What is Paris like? Isn't it just a dream? Did you go to the opera?﻿—Have you been on the Riviera?﻿—Oh, do tell me about those places﻿—is it like you read about in books?﻿—all beautiful, well-dressed women and men with nothing to do﻿—and did you go to Monte Carlo?"

This was all poured out in a rush of words; but in Mary's experience the Continent was merely a place where the Continentals got the better of the English, and she said so.

"Travelling is so mixed up with discomfort, that it loses half its plumage," she said. "I'll tell you all I can about Paris some other time. Now you tell me," she went on, folding carefully a silk blouse and putting it in a drawer, "are there any neighbours here? Will anyone come to call?"

===Chinese===
"多漂亮的东西啊！"她说。"你们去英格兰乡间别墅做客的时候，每次都带这些东西吗，所有这些骑马靴什么的？"

"哦，是的。没有它们没法骑马。"

"你们还带女仆来照看？"

"那是必须的。"

"那你们去欧洲大陆旅行呢，也带女仆吗？"

"我每次都带。"

"巴黎是什么样的？是不是像做梦一样？你去听歌剧了吗？——你去过里维埃拉吗？——哦，快跟我说说那些地方——是不是像书里写的那样？——到处都是美丽、衣着光鲜的女人和无所事事的男人——你去过蒙特卡洛吗？"

这一连串的话像决了堤似的涌出来；但在玛丽的经历中，欧洲大陆不过是欧洲人占英国人便宜的地方，她便照实说了。

"旅行总是和不舒服搅在一起，少了大半的光彩，"她说。"改天我再跟你讲巴黎的事。现在你告诉我，"她一边小心地叠着一件丝绸衬衫放进抽屉，一边说，"这儿有什么邻居吗？会有人来拜访吗？"

===Original===
"I'm afraid you'll find it very dull here," said Ellen. "There are no neighbours at all except Poss and Binjie, two young fellows on the next station. The people in town are just the publicans and the storekeeper, and all the selectors around us are a very wild lot. Very few strangers come that we can have in the house. They are nearly all cattle and sheep buyers, and they are either too nervous to say a word, or they talk horses. They always come just after mealtime, too, and we have to get everything laid on the table again﻿—sometimes we have ten meals a day in this house. And the swagmen come all day long, and Mrs. Gordon or I have to go and give them something to eat; there's plenty to do, always. So you see, there are plenty of strangers, but no neighbours."

"What about Mr. Blake?" said Miss Grant. "Isn't he a neighbour?"

It would have needed a much quicker eye than Mary's to catch the half-involuntary movement Ellen Harriott made when Blake's name was mentioned. She flashed a look of enquiry at the heiress that seemed to say, "What interest do you take in Mr. Blake? What is he to you?"

Then the long eyelashes shut down over the dark eyes again, and with an air of indifference she said﻿—

"Oh Mr. Blake? Of course I know him. I dance with him sometimes at the show balls, and all that. I have been out for a ride with him, too. I think he's nice, but Hugh and Mrs. Gordon won't ask him here because he belongs to the selectors, and his mother was a Miss Donohoe. He takes up their cases﻿—and wins them, too. But he never comes here. He always stays down at the hotel when he comes out this way."

"I intend to ask him here," said Miss Grant. "He saved my life."

Again the long eyelashes dropped so as to hide the sparkle of the eyes.

"Of course, if you like to ask him﻿—"

"Do you think he'd come?"

"Yes, I'm sure he would. If you like to write and ask him, Peter could ride down to Donohoe's today with a note."

From which it would seem that one, at any rate, of the Kuryong household was not wholly indifferent to Mr. Blake.

===Chinese===
"我怕你会觉得很无聊，"埃伦说。"附近根本没有邻居，除了波斯（Poss）和宾吉（Binjie），他们是隔壁牧场的两个年轻人。镇上的人就是些酒馆老板和店主，周围所有的自耕农（selectors）都是些野得很的家伙。很少有生人来家里坐坐。他们几乎全是牛羊贩子，要么紧张得一句话也说不出，要么就只会聊马。而且他们总是在饭后才来，我们得把所有东西重新摆上桌——这栋房子里一天有时要吃十顿饭。还有背袋客整天来来去去，戈登太太或者我就得去给他们弄点吃的；总有干不完的活。所以你看，生人倒是不少，可就是没邻居。"

"布莱克先生（Mr. Blake）呢？"格兰特小姐说。"他不算邻居吗？"

要是一般人，恐怕注意不到埃伦·哈里奥特听到布莱克这个名字时不经意间的那一丝微妙反应。她飞快地朝女继承人投去一个探询的目光，仿佛在说："你对布莱克先生有什么兴趣？他跟你有什么关系？"

然后长长的睫毛又垂下来，遮住了眼中的光芒，她用一种漫不经心的口气说——

"哦，布莱克先生？我当然认识他。有时候在展览舞会上跟他跳跳舞什么的。我也跟他一起出去骑过马。我觉得他挺好的，但休和戈登太太不会请他来这儿，因为他替自耕农打官司——而且打赢了。他母亲过去是多诺霍家的小姐。但他从不来这里。他来这一带的时候总是住在旅馆里。"

"我打算请他来这儿，"格兰特小姐说。"他救过我的命。"

长长的睫毛再次垂下，遮住了眼中闪烁的光。

"当然，你愿意请就请——"

"你觉得他会来吗？"

"嗯，我相信他会来。你要是愿意写封信请他，彼得今天就能骑马送到多诺霍家去。"

看来，至少有一个人对布莱克先生并非完全无动于衷。

## IX Some Visitors / 几位来客

===Original===
After breakfast next morning Mary decided to spend the day in the company of the children, who were having holidays.

"Just as well for you to learn the house first," said Hugh, "before you tackle the property. The youngsters know where everything is—within four miles, anyhow."

Two little girls were impressed, and were told to take Miss Grant round and show her the way about the place; and they set off together in the bright morning sunlight, on a trip of exploration.

===Chinese===
第二天早饭后，玛丽（Mary）决定跟孩子们一起度过这一天——他们正在放寒假。

"你先熟悉一下房子也好，"休（Hugh）说，"然后再去了解牧场。这帮小家伙对什么都门儿清——方圆四英里之内，至少。"

两个小女孩被委以重任，要带着格兰特小姐（Miss Grant）四处转转，熟悉一下周围的路；于是一行人趁着明媚的晨光出发了，开始一场探险之旅。

===Original===
Now, no true Australian, young or old, ever takes any trouble or undergoes any exertion or goes anywhere without an object in view. So the children considered it the height of stupidity to walk simply for the sake of walking, and kept asking where they were to walk to.

"What shall we see if we go along this road?" asked Miss Grant, pointing with her dainty parasol along the wheel-track that meandered across the open flat and lost itself in the timber.

"Nothing," said both children together.

"Then, what is there up that way?" she asked, waving her hand up towards the foothills and the blue mountains. "There must be some pretty flowers to look at up there?"

"No, there isn't," said the children.

"Well, let us go into the woods and see if we can't find something," she said determinedly; and with her reluctant guides she set off, trudging across the open forest through an interminable vista of gum trees.

===Chinese===
说来也是，在澳大利亚，不论老少，没有哪个真正的澳洲人会无端端地费力气、吃苦头，或者跑到一个毫无目的的地方去。因此，孩子们觉得单纯为了走路而走路简直是蠢透了，一路上不停地问他们这是要走到哪儿去。

"我们沿着这条路走能看到什么？"格兰特小姐问道，用她那精巧的阳伞指着那条蜿蜒穿过开阔平地、最终消失在林间的车辙道。

"什么也没有，"两个孩子异口同声地回答。

"那往那边走呢？"她朝山脚和远处的蓝山挥了挥手问道，"那边总该有些漂亮的花儿可以看看吧？"

"没有，"孩子们答道。

"好吧，那我们到树林里去找找，看能不能发现点什么，"她下定决心说道；于是，带着这两个不情愿的小向导，她踏上了旅程，穿过一片又一片桉树林构成的无穷无尽的林间空地，深一脚浅一脚地走着。

===Original===
After a while one of the girls said, "Hello, there's Poss!"

Miss Grant looked up, and saw through the trees a large and very frightened bay horse, with a white face. On further inspection, a youth of about eighteen or twenty was noticed on the horse's back, but he seemed so much a part of the animal that one might easily overlook him at a first glance. The horse had stopped at the sight of them, and was visibly affected with terror.

They advanced slowly, and the animal began snorting and sidling away among the timber, its rider meanwhile urging it forward. Then Emily cried,

"Hello, Poss!" and the horse gave a snort, wheeled round, jumped a huge fallen tree, and fled through the timber like a wild thing, with its rider still apparently glued to its back. In half a second they were out of sight.

===Chinese===
走了一会儿，其中一个女孩忽然叫道："嘿，是波斯（Poss）！"

格兰特小姐抬起头，透过树丛看到一匹高大的枣红色马，白脸膛，满脸惊恐。再仔细一看，马背上坐着一个十八九岁到二十岁上下的年轻人，但他和那匹马简直浑然一体，乍一看很容易就把人忽略了。那马一看到他们便停住了脚步，恐惧之色肉眼可见。

他们慢慢走近，那马开始喷着响鼻，在林间横挪着躲闪，骑手则不停地催它往前走。这时艾米丽（Emily）喊道：

"嘿，波斯！"那马打了个响鼻，猛地调转方向，跃过一棵巨大的倒木，驮着骑手——那骑手看上去还牢牢粘在马背上——像野兽一般穿过林子狂奔而去。转眼间便不见了踪影。

===Original===
"Who is it? and why does he go away?" asked Miss Grant.

"That's Poss," said Emily carelessly. "He and Binjie live over at Dunderalligo. He often comes here. They and their father live over there That's a colt he's breaking in. He's very nice. So is Binjie."

"Well, here he comes again," said Miss Grant, as the horseman reappeared, riding slowly round them in ever-lessening circles; the colt meanwhile eyeing them with every aspect of intense dislike and hatred, and snorting between whiles like a locomotive.

Emily waited till the rider came fairly close, and said, "Poss, this is Miss Grant."

The rider blushed, and lifted his hand to his hat. Fatal error! For the hundredth-part of a second the horse seemed to cower under him as if about to sink to the ground, then tucked his head in between his front legs, and his tail in between the hind ones, forming himself into a kind of circle, and began a series of gigantic bounds at the rate of about a hundred to the minute; while in the air above him his rider described a catherine wheel before he came to earth, landing on his head at Miss Grant's feet. The horse was soon out of sight, making bounds that would have cleared a house if one had been in the way. The rider got up, pulled his hat from over his eyes, brushed some mud off his clothes, and came up to shake hands as if nothing had happened; his motto apparently being *toujours la politesse*.

===Chinese===
"那是谁？他为什么跑了？"格兰特小姐问道。

"那是波斯，"艾米丽漫不经心地说，"他和宾吉（Binjie）住在邓德拉利戈（Dunderalligo）那边。他常到这儿来。他们和父亲一起住在那边。那是他在驯的一匹小马驹。他人很好。宾吉也是。"

"瞧，他又回来了，"格兰特小姐说道。只见那骑手又出现了，骑着马绕着他们慢慢兜圈子，圈子越来越小；那匹小马驹则一脸厌恶和仇恨地瞪着他们，不时像火车头一样喷着响鼻。

艾米丽等骑手走近了些，开口道："波斯，这位是格兰特小姐。"

骑手红了脸，抬手去扶帽子。这下可坏了！就在那百分之一秒的工夫，那匹马仿佛在他身下猛然一缩，像是要瘫倒在地，随即把脑袋缩进两条前腿之间，尾巴夹进后腿之间，整个身体蜷成一团，然后开始了以每分钟大约一百次的频率的疯狂弹跳；而他头顶上的骑手则在空中画了个车轮状的弧线，最后头朝下摔在了格兰特小姐脚边。那马转眼便消失在视野之外，跳跃之高，若路上有房子怕是也能一跃而过。骑手爬起来，把帽子从眼前拉正，抖落衣服上的泥巴，走上前来握手，仿佛什么事也没发生；他奉行的信条显然是 *toujours la politesse*（永远彬彬有礼）。

===Original===
"My word, can't he buck, Poss!" said the child. "He chucked you all right, didn't he?"

"He got a mean advantage," said the young fellow, in a slow drawl. "Makes me look a fair chump, doesn't it, getting chucked before a lady? I'll take it out of him when I get on him again. How d' you do?"

"I'm very well, thank you," said Miss Grant. "I hope you are not hurt. What a nasty beast! I wonder you aren't afraid to ride him."

"I ain't afraid of him, the cow! He can't sling me fair work, not the best day ever he saw. He can't buck," he added, in tones of the deepest contempt, "and he won't try when I've got a fair hold of him; only goes at it underhanded. It's up to me to give him a hidin' next time I ride him, I promise you."

"Where will he go to?" said Miss Grant, looking for the vanished steed. "Won't he run away?"

"He can't get out of the paddick," drawled the youth. "Let's go up to the house, and get one of the boys to run him in. He had a go-in this morning with me—the bit came out of his mouth somehow, and he did get to work proper. He went round and round the paddick at home, with me on him, buckin' like a brumby. Binjie had to come out with another horse and run me back into the yard. He's a pretty clever colt, too. The timber is tremendous thick in that paddick, and he never hit me against anything. Binjie reckons any other colt'd have killed me. Come on up to the house, or he'll have my saddle smashed before I get him."

===Chinese===
"老天，波斯，这马真能蹦啊！"那孩子叫道，"把你颠下来了，对吧？"

"它耍了阴招，"年轻人慢吞吞地拖着腔调说，"在女士面前被甩下来，够丢人的，是不是？等我再骑上去，非治它不可。你好啊。"

"我很好，谢谢，"格兰特小姐说，"希望你没伤着。真是个讨厌的畜生！你骑它居然不害怕。"

"我才不怕它呢，这怂包！它别想公平地把我甩下去，这辈子都别想。它根本不会蹦，"他用极其轻蔑的语气补充道，"只要我好好抓住它，它就不敢耍花样；只会在暗地里使坏。我向你保证，下次骑它，我非揍它一顿不可。"

"它跑到哪儿去了？"格兰特小姐问道，望着那消失的坐骑，"它不会跑丢吧？"

"跑不出围场去，"年轻人慢悠悠地说，"我们回屋子去，叫个伙计把它赶回来。今天早上它跟我闹了一场——嚼子不知怎么从嘴里脱落了，它可算撒开了欢。驮着我在家那边的围场里转了一圈又一圈，蹦得跟野马似的。宾吉只好骑另一匹马出来，把我赶回了围栏。这小马驹还挺聪明。那围场里的树林子密得不得了，它居然没把我撞到任何东西上。宾吉说换别的小马驹早把我撞死了。走吧，回屋子去，要不然等我把它弄回来，鞍子早被它折腾碎了。"

===Original===
As they hurried home, Miss Grant had a good look at the stranger—a pleasant, brown-skinned brown-handed youth, with the down of a black moustache growing on his upper lip. His frank and open face was easy to read. He looked with boyish admiration at Miss Grant, who immediately stooped to conquer, and began an animated conversation about nothing in particular—a conversation which was broken in upon by one of the girls.

"Where is Binjie?" she asked. "Isn't he coming over?"

"Not he," said the youth, with an air of great certainty. "We're busy over at our place, I tell you. The water is all gone in the nine-mile paddick. Binj an me and Andy Kelly had to muster all the sheep and shift 'em across to the home paddick. Binj is musterin' away there now. I just rode over to see Hugh about some of your sheep that's in the river paddick."

"Won't Binjie be over, then?" persisted Emily.

"No, of course he won't. Don't I tell you he's got three days' work musterin' there? I must be off at daylight tomorrow, home again, or the old man'll know the reason why."

===Chinese===
他们快步往回走的时候，格兰特小姐好好打量了一下这个陌生人——一个讨人喜欢的年轻人，棕色的皮肤，棕色的手，上唇上刚长出黑黑的短髭。他那坦率开朗的面孔让人一看便知底细。他用少年人那种倾慕的目光望着格兰特小姐，而格兰特小姐则立刻放下身段去征服对方，开始了一场关于什么具体内容也谈不上的热烈谈话——这场谈话被其中一个女孩打断了。

"宾吉呢？"她问道，"他不过来了吗？"

"他才不会来呢，"年轻人语气十分肯定地说，"告诉你，我们那边忙着呢。九英里围场里的水全干了。宾吉、我还有安迪·凯利（Andy Kelly）得把所有的羊都聚拢起来，赶到家那边的围场去。宾吉这会儿正在那边聚拢羊群呢。我刚骑过来是要跟休说一声，你们有些断奶的小羊跑到河围场那边去了。"

"那宾吉真的不来了？"艾米丽追问。

"当然不来了。我不是跟你说了吗，他得在那边干三天的活儿聚拢羊群呢？我明天天一亮就得赶回去，不然老头子知道了可不得了。"

===Original===
By this time they had reached the homestead, and Poss went off with the children to the stables. Here he secured the "knockabout" horse, always kept saddled and bridled about the station for generally-useful work, and set off at a swinging canter up the paddock after his own steed. Miss Grant went in and found Mrs. Gordon at her jam-making.

"Well, and have you found anything to amuse you?" asked the old lady in her soft, even voice.

"Oh, I've had quite a lot of experiences; and I went for a walk and met Poss. Who is Poss?"

The old lady laughed as she gave the jam a stir. "He's a young Hunter," she said. "Was Binjie there?"

"No; and he isn't coming either; he has work to do. I learnt that much. But who is Poss? and who is Binjie? I'm greatly taken with Poss."

"He's a nice-looking young fellow, isn't he? His father has a small station away among the hills, and Poss and Binjie help him on it. Those are only nicknames, of course. Poss's name is Arthur, and Binjie's is George, I think. They're nice young fellows, but very bushified; they have lived here all their lives. Their father—well, he isn't very steady; and they like to get over here when they can, and each tries to come without the other knowing it. Binjie will be here before long, I expect. They're great admirers of Miss Harriott, both of them, and they come over on all sorts of ridiculous pretexts. Poor fellows, it must be very dull for them over there. Fancy, week after week without seeing anyone but their father, the station-hands, and the sheep! Now that you're here, I expect they'll come more than ever."

===Chinese===
这时他们已经到了庄园，波斯带着孩子们去了马厩。他在那里牵出了那匹"通用马"——牧场里常年备好鞍辔、随时听候差遣干杂活的马——然后策着那马以轻快的小跑沿围场奔去，追赶他自己的坐骑。格兰特小姐走进屋里，发现戈登太太（Mrs. Gordon）正在熬果酱。

"怎么样，找到什么消遣了吗？"老太太用她那柔和而平稳的语调问道。

"哦，经历可不少呢；我去散了个步，遇到了波斯。波斯是什么人？"

老太太一边搅着果酱，一边笑了。"他是个年轻的亨特（Hunter），"她说，"宾吉在那儿吗？"

"不在；他也不打算过来；他有活儿要干。这我知道。可是波斯到底是什么人？宾吉又是什么人？我挺喜欢波斯的。"

"他是个好看的小伙子，是不是？他父亲在山里有个小牧场，波斯和宾吉在那边帮他干活。当然，那些都是绰号。波斯的大名叫亚瑟（Arthur），宾吉嘛，我想叫乔治（George）。都是好小伙子，不过太丛林气了；他们一辈子都在这儿长大。他们父亲——嗯，不太稳重；所以他们一有机会就往这边跑，而且各人都想瞒着另一个偷偷来。宾吉过不了多久也会来的，我估计。他们俩都是哈里奥特小姐（Miss Harriott）的仰慕者，变着各种荒唐的借口往这儿跑。可怜的小伙子们，在那边一定闷得慌。想想看，一周又一周，除了父亲、牧场伙计和羊群，什么人也见不着！现在你来了，我估摸着他们跑得更勤了。"

===Original===
As she spoke, the tramp of a horse's hoofs was heard in the yard and, looking out, Miss Grant saw a duplicate of Poss dismounting from a duplicate of Poss's horse. And Mrs. Gordon, looking over her shoulder, said, "Here's Binjie. I thought he'd be here before long."

"Why do they call him Binjie?" asked Miss Grant, watching the new arrival tying up his horse. "What does it mean?"

"It's a blackfellow's word, meaning stomach," said the old lady. "He used to be very fat, and the name stuck to him. Good day, Binjie!"

"Good day, Mrs. Gordon. Hugh at home?"

"No, he won't be back till dark," said the old lady. "Won't you let your horse go?"

"Well, I don't know if I can," replied the new arrival thoughtfully. "I've left Poss at home clearing the sheep out of that big paddock at the Crossing. There's five thousand sheep, and no water there; I'll have to go back and help him. I only came over to tell Hugh there were some of his weaners in the river paddock. I must go straight back, or Poss'll make a row. We've a lot of work to do."

"I think Poss is here," said Mrs. Gordon.

"Poss is here, is he? Well, if that don't beat everything! And when we started to muster that paddock I went to the top, and he went the other way, and he reckoned to be at it all day. He's a nice fellow, he is! I wonder what the old man'll say?"

"Oh, I expect he won't mind very much. This is Mr. George Hunter, Miss Grant."

Binjie extended much the same greeting as Poss had done; and by dinnertime that evening—or, as it is always called in the bush, teatime—they had all made each other's acquaintance, and both the youths were worshipping at the new shrine.

===Chinese===
她正说着，院子里传来了马蹄声。格兰特小姐朝外望去，看见一个跟波斯一模一样的人从一匹跟波斯那匹一模一样的马上跳了下来。戈登太太回过头说道："宾吉来了。我早就知道他过不了多久准来。"

"他们为什么叫他宾吉？"格兰特小姐一边看新来的人拴马，一边问道，"这名字什么意思？"

"那是土著话，意思是'肚子'，"老太太说，"他以前胖得很，这名字就叫开了。你好啊，宾吉！"

"你好，戈登太太。休在家吗？"

"不在，天黑前回不来，"老太太说，"你不把马放了吗？"

"嗯，恐怕不行，"新来的人若有所思地回答道，"我把波斯留在家里，让他把大围场里的羊赶出来——就是十字路口那个。五千只羊，那边一滴水也没有；我得回去帮他。我过来就是想告诉休，他有几只断奶的小羊跑到河围场那边去了。我得马上回去，不然波斯又要闹了。我们活儿多着呢。"

"我想波斯在这儿，"戈登太太说。

"波斯在这儿？嘿，这可真邪了门了！我们开始聚拢那个围场的时候，我往上面走，他往另一边走，他还说要在那儿干一整天呢。这人可真行！我倒想看看老头子怎么说？"

"哦，我想他不会太在意的。这位是乔治·亨特先生（Mr. George Hunter），格兰特小姐。"

宾吉跟波斯差不多一样的招呼方式；到了当天晚上吃晚饭的时候——在丛林里 always 叫"吃茶点"（teatime）——大家已经彼此认识了，两个年轻人都在新的偶像面前顶礼膜拜。

===Original===
At tea the talk flowed freely, and the two bush boys, shy at first, began to expand as Mary Grant talked to them. Put a pretty girl and a young and impressionable bushman together, and in the twinkling of an eye you have a Sir Galahad ready to do anything for the service of his lady.

Lightheartedly they consented to stay the night, in the hope of seeing Hugh, to deliver their message about the weaners—they seemed to have satisfactorily arranged the question of mustering. And when Miss Grant said, "Won't your sheep be dying of thirst in that paddock, where there is no water?" both brothers replied, "Oh, we'll be off at crack of dawn in the morning and fix 'em up all right."

"They always say that," said the old lady, "and generally stay three days. I expect they'll make it four, now that you're here."

===Chinese===
吃茶点的时候谈笑风生，两个丛林小伙子起初还有些拘束，但随着玛丽·格兰特跟他们聊起来，便渐渐放开了。把一个漂亮姑娘和一个年轻而易动情的丛林小伙子搁在一起，眨眼之间就能催生出一位加拉哈德爵士（Sir Galahad），甘愿为他的意中人赴汤蹈火。

他们爽快地答应留下来过夜，希望能见到休，把关于断奶小羊的事告诉他——聚拢羊群的问题看来已经妥善解决了。格兰特小姐说："围场里一滴水也没有，你们的羊不会渴死吗？"两兄弟齐声答道："哦，我们明天一大早就赶回去，准保把它们安顿好。"

"他们老是这么说，"老太太说，"结果一待就是三天。我估摸着，现在你来了，他们怕是要待四天了。"

## X A Lawyer in the Bush / 第十章 丛林里的律师

===Original===
Gavan Blake, attorney and solicitor, sat in his office at Tarrong, opening his morning's letters. The office was in a small weatherboard cottage in the "main street" of Tarrong (at any rate it might fairly claim to be the main street, as it was the only street that had any houses in it). The front room, where he sat, was fitted up with a table and a set of pigeonholes full of dusty papers, a leather couch, a small fireproof safe, and a bookcase containing about equal proportions of law-books and novels. A few maps of Tarrong township and neighbouring stations hung on the walls. The wooden partition of the house only ran up to the rafters, and over it could plainly be heard his housekeeper scrubbing his bedroom. Across the little passage was his sitting-room, furnished in the style of most bachelors' rooms, an important item of furniture being a cupboard where whisky was always to be found. At the back of the main cottage were servants' quarters and kitchen. Behind the house, on a spare allotment, were two or three loose-boxes for racehorses, a saddle-room and a groom's room. This was the whole establishment. A woman came in every day to do up his rooms from the hotel, where he had his meals. It was an inexpensive mode of life, but one that conduced to the drinking of a good many whiskies-and-sodas at the hotel with clients and casual callers, and to a good deal of card-playing and late hours. The racehorses, too, like most racehorses, ate up more money than they earned. So that Mr. Gavan Blake, though a clever man, with a good practice, always seemed to find himself hard up.

===Chinese===
加文·布莱克（Gavan Blake），律师兼事务律师，坐在塔龙（Tarrong）的办公室里拆早晨的信件。办公室设在塔龙"主街"上一栋小小的木板房里（至少它有资格称为主街，因为它是唯一一条有房子的街道）。他坐的前屋摆着一张桌子和一排塞满灰尘文件的鸽巢格、一张皮沙发、一个小保险柜，以及一个法律书和小说各占一半的书柜。墙上挂着几幅塔龙镇和周边牧场（station）的地图。房子的木隔板只到椽子那么高，隔板上方能清楚地听到管家在擦洗他的卧室。穿过小走廊就是他的起居室，布置跟大多数单身汉的房间差不多，其中一件重要的家具是一个柜子，里面总能找到威士忌。主屋后面是仆人住处和厨房。房子后面的空地上有两三个赛马用的散养马厩、一间鞍具室和一间马夫房间。这就是全部家当了。每天从他吃饭的旅馆来一个女人帮他打扫房间。这种生活方式开销不大，但也助长了他在旅馆里和客户、来访者喝大量威士忌苏打水的习惯，还催生了不少牌局和熬夜。那些赛马也像大多数赛马一样，花掉的钱比赚来的多。因此，加文·布莱克先生虽然精明能干、业务不错，却总是手头拮据。

===Original===
It was so on this particular morning. Every letter that he opened seemed to have some reference to money. One, from the local storekeeper, was a pretentious account embracing all sorts of items—ammunition, stationery, saddlery and station supplies (the latter being on account of a small station that Blake had taken over for a bad debt, which seemed likely to turn out an equally bad asset). Station supplies, even for bad stations, run into a lot of money, and the store account was approaching a hundred pounds. Then there was a letter from a horse-trainer in Sydney to whom he had sent a racehorse, and though this animal had done such brilliant gallops that the trainer had three times telegraphed him that a race was a certainty—once he went so far as to say that the horse could stop to throw a somersault and still win the race—on each occasion it had always come in among the ruck; and every time forty or fifty pounds of Blake's money had been lost in betting. For Blake was a confirmed gambler, a heavy cardplayer and backer of horses, and he had the contempt for other people's skill and opinions which seems to be an inevitable ingredient in the character of brilliant men of a certain type.

===Chinese===
这个 particular 早晨也是如此。他拆开的每封信似乎都跟钱有关。一封来自当地店主，是一份张扬的账单，涵盖各种名目——弹药、文具、马具和牧场（station）用品（后者是布莱克接手的一座小牧场因坏账抵来的，看起来也会变成同样糟糕的资产）。牧场用品，哪怕是给糟糕的牧场用的，花销也不少，店铺账目已接近一百英镑。还有一封信来自悉尼的驯马师，他把一匹赛马送去那里，虽然这匹马跑出了惊人的成绩，驯马师三次发电报说赢定了——有一次甚至说这匹马就算停下来翻个筋斗也能赢——可每次它都跑进了马群中间；布莱克每次都在赌注上赔掉四五十英镑。因为布莱克是个无可救药的赌徒，沉迷于纸牌和赛马投注，他对别人技能和意见的蔑视，似乎是某一类聪明人性格中不可避免的成分。

===Original===
He was a man of splendid presence, with strong features and clear blue-grey eyes—the type of face that is seen on the Bench and among the Queen's Counsel in the English Courts. He was quick-witted, eloquent, and logical of mind. Among the Doyles and Donohoes he was little short of a king. Wild, uneducated, and suspicious, they believed in him implicitly. They swore exactly the things that he told them to swear, spoke or were silent according as he ordered, and trusted him with secrets which they would not entrust to their own brothers. In that district he wielded a power greater than the law.

===Chinese===
他仪表堂堂，五官刚毅，一双清澈的蓝灰色眼睛——那种在英国法庭上法官席和御用大律师中常见的面孔。他才思敏捷，能言善辩，逻辑清晰。在多尔家（Doyles）和多诺霍家（Donohoes）人中间，他简直像个国王。这些粗野、没受过教育、生性多疑的人对他深信不疑。他们严格按他的指示发誓作证，按他的命令说话或沉默，把连亲兄弟都不肯告诉的秘密托付给他。在那个地区，他掌握着凌驾于法律之上的权力。

===Original===
On this particular day, after opening the trainer's letter asking for cheque to pay training expenses (£50), and one from a client, saying "I got your note, and will pay you when I get the wool money," he came upon a letter that startled him. It was written in an old-fashioned, lady's hand, angular and spidery. It ran—

===Chinese===
就在这个 particular 日子，拆开驯马师索要训练费支票（50英镑）的信，又拆开一封客户来信说"我收到你的便条了，等我拿到卖羊毛的钱就付你"之后，他发现了一封让他吃了一惊的信。信是一个女人的手笔，字体古雅，棱角分明，纤细如蛛丝。信写道——

===Original===
> "Dear Mr. Blake,
