Hopal in the rough, well, it's 'ard to tell what it'll turn out, and they'll give 'er a 'unk as sometimes turns out a fair dazzler. She's a hay-one judge of it in the rough, too. If she buys a bit of hopal, yer bet yer life it ain't a bad bit when it's cut. What about these 'ere stores? Goin' to take 'em with yer?"

===Chinese===
"你不认得她？"那人说，那语气跟布雷特·哈特笔下那位英雄大吃一惊时发现——

> "不认识弗林——
 
 "弗林，弗吉尼亚的弗林。"

——如出一辙。

"你不认得她？"他又问了一遍，手里正从箱底铲出一些发黑的、像蟑螂屎似的糖。"那可是蛋白石女王（Opal Queen）！她往南边去。你也搭马车走？"

"是，"查理说。"我们搭马车。跟这么一位阔太太同车，不会另外加钱吧？她那些蛋白石到底从哪儿弄来的？"

"嗬，都是那些从蛋白石矿场回来的人送她的。原矿！你知道的，蛋白石原矿嘛，鬼知道切出来是什么样，可他们有时候就送她一块，切开来竟然是极品。她对原矿的鉴赏力也是顶好的。她要是买了一块蛋白石，我包你切出来绝对差不了。这些补给怎么办？你们要带走？"

===Original===
"No," said Charlie. "The black boy is here for them. He's going to take them back with him."

"What, Keogh's black boy! Well, I don't know as Pike'll stand old Paddy Keogh any longer. Paddy's 'ad a dorg tied hup 'ere" (i.e., an account outstanding) "this two years, and last time Pike was 'ome 'e was reck'nin' it was about hup to Keogh to pay something."

"They're not for Keogh," said Charlie. "They're for me. I've taken Keogh's block over."

The old man looked at him dubiously.

"Well, but y'aint goin' to tie hup no dorg on us for 'em, are yer? I s'pose it's all right, though?"

"Right, yes," said Gordon. "It's for Mr. Grant, Old Man Grant﻿—you've heard of Grant of Kuryong?"

"Never 'eard of him," said the aged man, "but it makes no hodds. Pay when yer like. Yer'd better git on the coach, for I see the Hopal Queen's ready for a start. Yer'll know her all right before long, I bet. Some of the fellers from round about 'as come in to give her a send-off like. There's the coach ready; yer'd better git aboard, and yer'll hear the﻿—the send-off like. Young Stacy out there reckons 'e's going to make a speech."

===Chinese===
"不用，"查理说。"黑人男孩来取货，他把东西带回去。"

"啥，基奥（Keogh）的黑人男孩！我说啊，派克大概不会再让老帕迪·基奥挂账了。帕迪在这儿欠了两年的账了"（指账目拖欠），"上回派克在家，正盘算着该让基奥还点钱了。"

"这不是给基奥的，"查理说。"给我的。我已经接下了基奥的那块地。"

老头狐疑地看着他。

"那你总不会在我们这儿也挂账吧？我看应该没问题吧？"

"没问题，"戈登说。"是给格兰特先生的，老格兰特——库里昂（Kuryong）的格兰特，你听说过吧？"

"没听说过，"老头说，"不过无所谓。什么时候给都行。你们最好赶紧上车，蛋白石女王马上要出发了。我敢赌你们很快就会见识到她。附近的一些伙计都来给她送行了。马车已经备好了，你们快上吧，一会儿就该——送行了。那个小斯塔西（Stacy）在外面，说是要发表演讲呢。"

===Original===
Charlie and Carew climbed upon the coach. The fat lady kissed Pike's wife and children with great solemnity. "Goodbye, Alice! Goodbye, Nora darlin'," she said. Then she marched in a stately way towards the vehicle, with the children forming a bodyguard round her. A group of men hung about uneasily, looked sheepish, and waved large, helpless red hands, till a young fellow about seven feet high﻿—who looked more uneasy and had even larger hands than the rest﻿—was hustled forward, and began to mutter something that nobody could hear.

"Speak up, George," said a friend. The young man raised his voice to a shout, and said﻿—

"And so I propose three cheers and long life to the Hopal Queen!"

As he spoke he ran two or three paces forward towards a stump, meaning, no doubt, to get on it and lead the cheering; but, just as he was going to jump, a wretched little mongrel that had been in and out among the people's feet made a dash at him, fixed its teeth in the calf of his leg, and ran away howling at its own temerity. The young giant rushed after it, but the Opal Queen interposed.

"George," she said, "don't ye dare go for to kick my dog!"

"Well, what did he bite me for, then?" said the giant, speaking out now in a voice that could be heard half a mile off. "What did he bite me for?"

"Never mind, George! Don't ye go for to kick him, that's all."

The Opal Queen, snorting like a grampus, climbed into the coach; the driver cracked his whip, and off they went, leaving the audience spellbound, and the gigantic young man rubbing his leg. Soon Pike's faded away in the distance. As the coach jolted along, Carew and Charlie on the box seat occasionally peered in at the large swaying figure, half-hidden in the dust.

===Chinese===
查理和卡鲁爬上了马车。那位胖女士庄严地亲了亲派克的妻子和孩子。"再见，爱丽丝！再见，亲爱的诺拉，"她说。然后她仪态万方地朝马车走去，孩子们组成一支护卫队围着她转。一群男人局促不安地站在周围，一脸窘态，挥着又大又笨的红手掌，直到一个约七英尺高的年轻人——他看起来比其他人更不自在，手也更大——被推搡着走到前面，嘴里咕哝着谁也听不清的话。

"大声点，乔治，"一个朋友说。那年轻人扯开嗓门喊道——

"我提议为蛋白石女王欢呼三声，祝她长寿！"

他一边说，一边朝一个树桩跑了两三步，毫无疑问是想站上去带头欢呼。可就在他正要跳上去时，一只在人们脚边钻来钻去的可怜小杂种狗猛地扑向他，一口咬住了他的小腿肚，然后夹着尾巴嚎叫着跑了——显然是为自己的鲁莽后怕。年轻的巨人追了上去，但蛋白石女王出面拦住了。

"乔治，"她说，"你敢踢我的狗试试！"

"那它咬我干嘛？"巨人吼道，声音大得半里地外都听得见。"它咬我干嘛？"

"少废话，乔治！不许踢它，就这一句。"

蛋白石女王像头海牛似的哼哧着爬上了马车；车夫甩响了鞭子，马车便出发了，留下目瞪口呆的观众和那个揉着腿的高大年轻人。派克镇很快消失在远方。马车颠簸前行，坐在前座上的卡鲁和卡鲁偶尔朝车厢里那个在尘土中若隐若现的硕大身影瞥上一眼。

===Original===
About two miles out of town Considine, with all his earthly belongings in a small valise, stopped the coach and got on board, sitting in front with them.

"Have a look inside," said Charlie. "There's a woman in there looks rather like﻿—the lady you were talking about."

Considine looked in. Then he sank back in his seat, with a white face. "By Heavens!" he said, "it's my wife."

"This is funny," said Charlie. "Wonder what she's after. She must have heard, somehow. She'll never lose sight of you, now, Considine."

Here the driver struck into the conversation. "See her inside?" he said, indicating the inside passenger with a nod of his head. "She's off to Sydney, full rip. She reckons her husband's dead, and she's came in for a fortune."

"Oh, she reckons he's dead, does she?" said Charlie carelessly. "Didn't know she had a husband."

"Ho yes," said the driver. "She came up here passin' by the name of Keogh, but it seems that ain't her husband's name at all."

"Oh, indeed! Do you happen to have heard what her husband's name is? And when did he die?"

===Chinese===
出了镇子约莫两英里，康西丁提着一只装着全部家当的小手提箱拦下了马车，上了车，坐在他们前面。

"往里看看，"查理说。"里面有个女人，长得像——你之前说的那位。"

康西丁往里看了一眼。随即面色苍白地瘫回了座位。"老天爷！"他说，"是我老婆。"

"这可有意思了，"查理说。"不知道她想干什么。她一定不知怎么听到了消息。康西丁，她这回可不会放过你了。"

这时车夫插进了谈话。"看见里面那位没有？"他用脑袋朝车厢里那位乘客点了点。"她奔悉尼去，火急火燎的。她觉得自己丈夫死了，发财了。"

"哦，她觉得他死了，是吗？"查理漫不经心地说。"不知道她还有个丈夫。"

"哎，有，"车夫说。"她来这儿的时候用的是基奥这个姓，可那根本不是她丈夫的姓。"

"哦，是吗！你碰巧知道她丈夫叫什么名字吗？他是什么时候死的？"

===Original===
"I never heard the noo husband's name," replied the driver. "Keogh was *her* name. I dessay if I arst her she'd tell me. Shall I arst her?" "No," said Considine firmly. "Don't annoy her at all. Leave well alone, young feller. What odds is it to you how many husbands the poor woman has had?"

"No," said the driver dispassionately. "It's no odds to me, nor yet to you, I don't suppose. She's in for a real big thing, I believe. A telegram came to the telegraph station after I left last trip, and young Jack Sheehan, he brought it on after me﻿—rode a hundred miles pretty well, to ketch me up. He reckoned she was coming in for a hundred thousand pounds. I wouldn't mind marryin' her meself, if it's true; plenty worse-looking sorts than her about. What do you think, eh, Mister?" addressing Considine.

"Marry her, and be blowed," said that worthy, sociably; and the driver stiffened and refused to talk further on the subject.

Meanwhile the three discussed the matter in low tones. It was practically impossible that anyone could have heard of the identity of Keogh with the missing Considine. How then had the story got about that her husband was dead, and that she had come into money? She must have seen Considine get on the coach, but she had made no sign. Their astonishment was deeper than ever when the coach stopped for a midday halt. It was quite impossible for Considine to conceal himself. The house, where the coach changed horses, was a galvanised-iron, one-roomed edifice in the middle of a glaring expanse of treeless plain, in which a quail could scarcely have hidden successfully. It was clear that Considine and his wife would have to come face to face.

===Chinese===
"新丈夫的名字我没听过，"车夫回答。"基奥是*她*的姓。我要是去问她，她八成会告诉我。我去问问？"

"别，"康西丁果断地说。"别去烦她。别去招惹是非，年轻人。那可怜女人嫁过几个丈夫，跟你有什么相干？"

"倒也是，"车夫面无表情地说。"跟我是不相干，跟你我猜也不相干。不过她可要发大财了。上次我走后，有封电报到了电报站，小杰克·希恩（Jack Sheehan）骑着马追了差不多一百英里才赶上我。他说她要拿到十万英镑呢。要是真的，我倒是不介意娶她；比她难看的女人满大街都是。先生您怎么看？"他转向康西丁问道。

"娶她，见鬼去吧，"这位先生倒是客气得很。车夫脸一沉，不再往下说了。

三人低声商议起来。几乎不可能有人知道基奥就是失踪的康西丁。那"丈夫已死、她继承了遗产"的消息又是从哪儿传出来的？她肯定看见康西丁上了马车，却不动声色。当中午停车歇脚时，他们的惊讶更甚了。康西丁完全无处藏身。换马的站点是一间铁皮屋，孤零零地立在一片刺眼的、寸木不生的平原上，连只鹌鹑都藏不住。毫无疑问，康西丁和他妻子非得面对面不可了。

===Original===
Carew and Charlie looked expectantly at each other, and clambered down quickly when the coach stopped. Considine descended more slowly; straightening his figure and looking fixedly before him, he marched up to the door of the change-house.

His wife got leisurely out of the coach, put on her bonnet, and walked straight over to him; then she looked him full in the face for at least three seconds, and passed by without a sign of recognition.

The three men looked at each other.

"Well, this bangs all," said Considine. "She knew me all right. Why didn't she speak? She's afraid I'll clear out, and she's shammin' not to know me, so's she'll have me arrested as soon as she sights a bobby. I know her. Perhaps I'd better offer her something to go back and leave me alone, hey?"

This was vetoed by a majority of two to one, and once more the coach started. They plodded away on the weary, dusty journey, until the iron roofs and walls of Barcoo gleamed like a mirage in the distance, and the coach rolled up to the hotel. A telegraph official came lounging forward.

"Anyone here the name of Charles Gordon?" he said.

"That's me," said Charlie.

"Telegram for you," he said. "It's been all over the country after you."

Gordon tore it open, read it, and stood spellbound. Then he silently handed it to Carew. It was several weeks old, and was from Pinnock, the solicitor. It read as follows﻿—"William Grant died suddenly yesterday. Will made years ago leaves everything to his wife. Reported that he married Margaret Donohoe, and that she is still alive. Am making all inquiries. Wire me anything you know."

===Chinese===
查理和卡鲁期待地对视一眼，马车一停便迅速跳了下去。康西丁下车慢了些；他挺直身子，目光直视前方，大步走向换马房的门口。

他妻子不慌不忙地下了车，戴上帽子，径直朝他走过来；然后她直直地盯着他的脸看了至少三秒钟，接着擦身而过，丝毫没有认出他的意思。

三个男人面面相觑。

"这可真绝了，"康西丁说。"她明明认得我。为什么不吭声？她怕我跑了，装作不认识我，好等她一瞧见警察就把我抓起来。我了解她。也许我该给她点钱让她回去，别来烦我，嗯？"

这个提议被二比一否决了。马车再次启动。他们在漫长尘土飞扬的路上颠簸前行，直到巴库的铁皮屋顶在远处像海市蜃楼般闪烁，马车在旅馆前停了下来。一个电报局的人慢悠悠地走过来。

"哪位是查理·戈登（Charles Gordon）？"他问。

"是我，"查理说。

"有你的电报，"他说。"为了找你，电报都跑遍了这一带。"

戈登拆开电报，读了一遍，愣住了。然后他默默地把电报递给了卡鲁。电报已经过期好几周，来自律师平诺克（Pinnock）。电文如下——"威廉·格兰特（William Grant）昨日猝然去世。多年前所立遗嘱将一切留予其妻。据报他曾娶玛格丽特·多诺霍（Margaret Donohoe），且该女至今在世。正在全力调查。如有任何消息请电告。"

===Original===
Charlie's face never changed a muscle.

"That's lively!" he said. "He never married that woman; and, if he did, she died long ago."

As he spoke, the lady passenger, having had some talk with the hotel people, came over to him with a beaming smile. "And ye're Charlie Gordon," she said with a mellifluous mixture of brogue and bush-drawl. "An' ye don't know me now, a little bit? Ye were a little felly when we last met. I'm Peggy Donohoe that was﻿—Peggy Grant now, since I married poor dear Grant that's dead. And, sure, rest his sowl!"﻿—here she sniffed a little﻿—"though he treated me cruel bad, so he did! Ye'll remember me brother Mick﻿—Mick with the red hair?"

"Yes," said Charlie, slowly and deliberately, "I remember him well; and you too. And look here, Peggy Donohoe﻿—or Peggy Keogh, whichever you call yourself﻿—you and Red Mick will have the most uphill fight you ever fought before you get one sixpence of William Grant's money. Why, your real husband is here on the coach with us!"

He turned and pulled Considine forward, and once more husband and wife stood face to face. Considine, alias Keogh, smiled in a sickly way, tried to meet his wife's eyes, and failed altogether. She regarded him with a bold, unwinking stare.

"Him!" she said. "Him me husban'! This old crockerdile? I never seen him before in me life."

===Chinese===
查理的脸上纹丝不动。

"可真够热闹的！"他说。"他根本没娶过那女人；就算娶了，她也早死了。"

正说着，那位女乘客跟旅馆的人聊完，满脸堆笑地走了过来。"你就是查理·戈登啊，"她用甜腻腻的爱尔兰腔和丛林土话混在一起的腔调说。"你一点也认不出我了吗？我们上次见面的时候你还是个小不点儿呢。我过去叫佩吉·多诺霍（Peggy Donohoe）——现在叫佩吉·格兰特，因为嫁给了可怜的好人格兰特，他可已经去了。愿他的灵魂安息！"——她抽了一下鼻子——"虽说他对我坏透了，真的！你还记得我兄弟米克吧——红头发的米克？"

"记得，"查理不紧不慢地说，"我记得很清楚；你也记得。听好了，佩吉·多诺霍——或者佩吉·基奥（Peggy Keogh），随你叫什么——你和红米克（Red Mick）要想拿到威廉·格兰特的一分钱，得先打赢一场前所未有的苦仗。告诉你吧，你真正的丈夫现在就在马车上跟我们在一起呢！"

他转身把康西丁拉到前面，夫妻二人再次面对面站着。康西丁，也就是基奥，勉强挤出一个难看的笑，试着迎上妻子的目光，却完全做不到。她用大胆而毫不闪避的眼神打量着他。

"他！"她说。"他是我丈夫！这条老鳄鱼？我这辈子从没见过他。"

===Original===
A look of hopeless perplexity settled on Considine's features for a moment, and then a ray of intelligence seemed to break in on him. She repeated her statement.

"I never seen this man before in me life. Did I? Speak up, now, and say, did I?"

Considine hesitated for a moment in visible distress. Then, pulling himself together, and looking boldly from one to the other, he replied﻿—

"Now that you mention it, ma'am, I don't think as ever you did. I must ha' made some mistake."

He walked rapidly away, leaving Gordon and Peggy face to face.

"There y'are," she said, "what did I tell ye? Husban'? He's no husban' o' mine. Ye're makin' a mistake, Charlie."

===Chinese===
一丝无望的困惑掠过康西丁的面庞，随即似乎有一线灵光闪过他的脑海。她又重复了一遍自己的话。

"我这辈子从没见过这个人。是吗？你说啊，我到底见没见过？"

康西丁显而易见地为难了片刻。随后他定了定神，大大方方地看了看这个又看了看那个，答道——

"您这么一说，太太，我确实不记得您见过。一定是我搞错了。"

他快步走开了，留下戈登和佩吉面对面站着。

"你瞧，"她说，"我说什么来着？丈夫？他才不是我的丈夫。查理，你搞错了。"

===Original===
Charlie looked after the retreating bushman, and back at the good lady who was beaming at him.

"Don't call me Charlie," he said. "That old man has come in for a whole lot of money in England. His name is Considine, and he pretends he isn't your husband so that he can get the money and leave you out of it. Don't you be a fool. It's a lot better for you to stick to him than to try for William Grant's money. Mr. Carew and I can prove he said you were his wife."

"Och, look at that now! Said I was his wife! And his name was Considine, the lyin' old vaggybond. His name's not Considine, and I'm not his wife, nor never was. Grant was my husban', and I'll prove it in a coort of law, so I will!" Her voice began to rise like a southeasterly gale, and Charlie beat a retreat. He went to look for the old man, but could not find him anywhere.

Talking the matter over with Carew he got no satisfaction from the wisdom of that Solon. "Deuced awkward thing, don't you know," was his only comment.

Things were even more awkward when the coach drew up to start, and no sign of the old man could be found. He had strolled off to the back of the hotel, and vanished as absolutely as if the earth had swallowed him.

The Chinese cook was severely cross-questioned, but relapsed into idiotic smiles and plentiful "No savee's." A blackfellow, loafing about the back of the hotel, was asked if he had seen a tall, thin old man with a beard going down the street. He said, "Yowi, he bin go longa other pub;" but as, on further questioning, he modified his statement by asserting that the man he saw was young, short and very fat, no heed was paid to his evidence﻿—it being the habit of blacks to give any answer that they think will please the questioner.

===Chinese===
查理望着那个远去的灌木丛人，又回头看了看正朝他笑逐颜开的这位好心女士。

"别叫我查理，"他说。"那老头在英国继承了一大笔钱。他叫康西丁（Considine），他假装不是你丈夫，是为了把钱弄到手，把你甩掉。你可别犯傻。跟着他可比去争威廉·格兰特的钱强多了。卡鲁先生和我能证明他说过你是他妻子。"

"哦，你瞧瞧！说我是他妻子！还叫康西丁，这个撒谎的老混蛋。他不叫康西丁，我也不是他妻子，从来都不是。格兰特才是我的丈夫，我要在法庭上证明这一点，一定！"她的声音像东南风一样越来越大，查理连忙撤退。他去找那个老头，哪儿都找不到。

他跟卡鲁商量此事，但从这位梭伦（Solon）那里什么高见也没讨到。"真是棘手透顶，"这是他唯一的评论。

事情在马车准备出发时更加棘手了——老头毫无踪影。他踱到旅馆后面去了，消失得无影无踪，仿佛大地把他吞了似的。

那个中国厨师被严厉地盘问了一番，但只会傻笑着不停说"不晓得"。旅馆后面闲逛的一个黑人被问到有没有看见一个高瘦的胡子老头沿街走过。他说："呦，他去了另一家酒店；"但经进一步追问，他又改口说他看到的那个人又矮又胖还很年轻，所以没人理会他的话——黑人向来喜欢说任何他们觉得能让问话者高兴的回答。

===Original===
"He'll play us some dog's trick, that old fellow," said Charlie. "I can't wait here looking for him, though. I'll find him when I want him if he's above ground. Now let's go on. Can't keep the coach waiting forever while we unearth him. Let's get aboard."

Just as the coach was about to start a drover came out of the bar of the hotel, wiping his lips with the back of his hand. He stared vacantly about him, first up the street and then down, looked hard at a post in front of the hotel, then stared up and down the street again. At last he walked over, and, addressing the passengers in a body, said, "Did any of you's see e'er a horse anywheres? I left my prad here, and he's gorn."

A bystander, languidly cutting up a pipeful of tobacco, jerked his elbow down the road.

"That old bloke took 'im," he said. "Old bloke that come in the coach. While yous was all talking in the pub, he sneaks out here and nabs that 'orse, and away like a rabbit. See that dust on the plain? That's 'im."

The drover looked helplessly out over the stretch of plain. He seemed quite incapable of grappling with the problem.

"Took my horse, did he? Well, I'm blowed! By Cripes!"

He had another good stare over the plain, and back at the party.

"My oath!" he added.

Then the natural stoicism of the bushman came to his aid, and he said, in a resigned tone,

"Oh, well, anyways, I s'pose﻿—s'pose he must have been in a hurry to go somewheres. I s'pose he'll fetch him back some time or other."

===Chinese===
"那老头准要耍什么花招，"查理说。"可我不能在这儿干等着找他。只要他还在地面上，需要的时候我自会找到他。走吧。不能让马车一直等着，就为了把他挖出来。上车吧。"

马车正要启动，一个赶牲口的人从旅馆酒吧里出来了，用手背擦着嘴。他茫然地四下张望，先朝街这头看看，又朝那头看看，死死盯着旅馆前的一根柱子，然后再把街上来回扫视了一遍。最后他走过来，对着所有乘客说："你们哪位看见我的马了没有？我把它拴在这儿，它不见了。"

一个旁观者懒洋洋地切着烟丝，用胳膊肘朝路那边一指。

"那个老头把它牵走了，"他说。"坐马车来的那个老头。你们都在酒吧里聊天的时候，他偷偷溜出来，把马牵走了，跑得比兔子还快。看见平原上那片尘土了吗？那就是他。"

赶牲口的人茫然地望着那一片平原。他似乎完全无力应对这个问题。

"骑走了我的马？天哪！老天爷！"

他又好好看了一遍平原，又看了看这群人。

"我的老天！"他补了一句。

接着，灌木丛人天生的坚忍帮了他，他用一种认了命的口气说：

"噢，好吧，不管怎么说，我猜——他大概急着去什么地方吧。我猜他早晚总会把马送回来的。"

===Original===
Gordon leant down from the box of the coach.

"You tell him," he said, "when he does fetch him back, that if I'd had a rifle, and had seen him sneaking off like that he'd have wanted an ambulance before he got much farther. Tell him I'll find him if I have to hunt him to death. Tell him that, will you?"

"All right, Mister!" said the drover, obligingly, "I'll tell him!"

The horses plunged into their collars; off went the coach into long stretches of dusty road, with the fat red lady inside, and our two friends outside. And in course of time they found themselves once more in Sydney, where they took the earliest opportunity to call on Pinnock, and hold a council of war against Peggy.

===Chinese===
戈登从马车前座探出身来。

"你告诉他，"他说，"等他把马送回来的时候，如果我手上有杆枪，又看到他那样偷偷摸摸地溜走，他不叫救护车休想走出多远。告诉他，我会找到他的，哪怕追到他断气也绝不放过。告诉他，听到了吗？"

"好嘞，先生！"赶牲口的人痛快地答应，"我一定告诉他！"

马匹猛地套紧了挽具；马车驶上漫漫长路，一路尘土飞扬，胖胖的红发女士坐在车厢里面，我们的两位朋友坐在外面。不知不觉间，他们又回到了悉尼，一有机会便去拜访平诺克（Pinnock），商讨对付佩吉的对策。

## XX A Consultation at Kiley’s / 在基利（Kiley's）商议

===Original===
Within twenty-four hours after Peggy got back to her old home, it was known all over the mountains that she meant business, and would make a claim on William Grant’s estate. Rumour, of course, supplied all the needful details. It was said, and even sworn to, that Peggy had her marriage lines put by in a big iron box, ready to be produced at the proper time. Other authorities knew for a fact that she had no proofs, but that the family at Kuryong were going to give her any sum from a thousand pounds to a million, to cancel her claim and save exposure.

As a matter of fact, none of those who talked knew anything whatever. Peggy confided in no one but Red Mick, and that worthy had had enough legal experience of a rough and ready sort to know that things must be kept quiet till the proper time. But by way of getting ready for action Red Mick and his sister one fine morning rode up to Gavan Blake’s office to consult him as to what they should do.

Blake was not at all surprised to see them. He, of course, had heard all the rumours that were afloat, and knew that if Peggy brought forward any claim he would be asked to act for her professionally. He had not quite decided whether he would act or not. In his hard commonsense mind he saw next to no possibility of Peggy having a bona fide case. He did not suppose for a moment that William Grant would have run his neck into a bigamy noose; and it would put the young lawyer in a very awkward position with Mary Grant if, after saving her life and posing as her friend, he carried on a blackmailing suit against her. At the same time, he felt that it could do no harm to either side to investigate Peggy’s case; there might be awkward things that he could help to suppress. So with expectancy and not a little amusement he saw his clients ride up and tie their horses to the fence outside his office, and watched Peggy straighten her ruffled plumage before entering.

===Chinese===
佩吉（Peggy）回到她的老家不到二十四小时，整个山区都知道她是当真了，要对威廉·格兰特（William Grant）的遗产提出索赔。当然，谣言补全了所有必要的细节。有人说，甚至有人发誓说，佩吉把她的结婚证书放在一个大铁盒里，准备在适当的时候拿出来。其他知情者则确切地知道她没有任何证据，但库里昂（Kuryong）那家人打算给她从一千英镑到一百万不等的钱，以撤销她的索赔并避免曝光。

事实上，那些议论纷纷的人什么都不知道。佩吉只对红米克（Red Mick）透露过，而这位可敬的人物有足够的粗略法律经验，知道事情必须保密到合适的时机。但为了做好行动准备，红米克和他的妹妹在一个晴朗的早晨骑马来到加文·布莱克（Gavan Blake）的办公室，咨询他们该怎么做。

布莱克看到他们一点也不惊讶。当然，他听到了所有流传的谣言，知道如果佩吉提出任何索赔，他会被要求以律师身份代理。他还没有完全决定是否要代理。在他务实的头脑里，佩吉拥有一个真诚案件的可能性几乎为零。他一刻也不认为威廉·格兰特会把脖子伸进重婚的绞索里；而且如果这位年轻律师在救了玛丽·格兰特的命并假装是她的朋友之后，又对她提起一桩敲诈勒索的诉讼，这会让玛丽对他非常尴尬。同时，他认为调查佩吉的案件对双方都没有害处；可能会有些尴尬的事情需要他帮助压制。于是，他怀着期待和不小的愉悦，看着他的当事人骑马过来，把马拴在他办公室外的围栏上，看着佩吉在进去前整理了一下凌乱的羽毛。

===Original===
They came in at the door with a seriousness worthy of the occasion. Peggy heaved a subdued sigh and settled in a chair. Red Mick opened the conversation.

“Mornin’ to you, Gavan,” he said.

By virtue of his relationship Mick was privileged to call his brilliant nephew by his Christian name. To the rest of the clans Gavan was Mr. Blake.

“Good morning, Mick. Good morning, Peggy. Have you had any rain?”

In the bush no one would think of introducing discussion without a remark about the weather.

“Jist a few drops,” said Red Mick gloomily. “Do us no good at all. Things is looking terrible bad, so they are. But we want to see ye﻿—” and here he dropped his voice, rose, and cautiously closed the door﻿—“Peggy here, Mrs. Grant, d’ye see,”﻿—Mick got the name out without an effort﻿—“she wants to see ye about making a claim on the estate. ’Tis time she done somethin’. All these years left to shift for herself﻿—”

Here Blake broke in on him. He meant to probe Peggy’s case thoroughly, and knew that it would be no easy matter to get at the truth while she had Red Mick alongside to prompt her. He had not dealt with the mountain folk for nothing, and handled his clients in a way that would astonish a more conservative practitioner.

“Mick,” he said, “you go over to Isaacstein’s store and wait till I send for you.”

“I want Mick to be wid me,” began Peggy.

Blake blazed up. He knew that he must keep his ascendancy over these wild people by force of determination.

“You heard what I said,” he thundered, turning fiercely on Peggy. “You want this and you want that! It’s not what you want, it’s what *I* want! You do what you’re told. If you don’t﻿—I won’t help you. Mick, you go over to the store, and wait till I send for you.” And Mick shambled off.

===Chinese===
他们以配得上这场合的严肃表情走进门。佩吉压低声音叹了口气，在椅子上坐定。红米克开口了。

「早上好，加文，」他说。

凭着他和侄子的关系，米克有特权直呼这位出色的侄子的名字。对其他族人来说，加文是布莱克先生。

「早上好，米克。早上好，佩吉。你们那儿下雨了吗？」

在丛林里，没人会在引入讨论前不先聊聊天气。

「就下了几滴，」红米克阴郁地说。「一点用都没有。情况糟透了，真的。但我们想见您——」说到这里，他压低声音，站起来，小心翼翼地关上门——「佩吉，格兰特太太，您知道，」——米克毫不费力地把这个名字说了出来——「她想跟您谈谈对遗产提出索赔的事。她该做点什么了。这么多年都让她自己挣扎——」

布莱克打断了他。他打算彻底探查佩吉的案子，知道只要红米克在旁边提示她，就不容易弄清真相。他跟山地人打交道可不是白费的，处理当事人的方法会让更保守的律师大吃一惊。

「米克，」他说，「你去艾萨克斯坦（Isaacstein）的商店，等我派人叫你。」

「我要米克陪着我，」佩吉开口了。

布莱克发火了。他知道自己必须靠坚定的意志来保持对这些野蛮人的优势。

「你听到我说什么了，」他吼道，猛地转向佩吉。「你想要这个想要那个！这不是你想要什么，是*我*想要什么！你照我说的做。如果你不——我就不帮你。米克，你去商店，等我派人叫你。」米克蹒跚地走了。

===Original===
Peggy, still inclined to be defiant, settled herself in her chair. She had battled in North Queensland so long that she neither feared nor respected anybody; but her native shrewdness told her she had all to gain and nothing to lose by doing what her lawyer advised.

“Now, Peggy,” he said, “do you want to make a claim against William Grant’s estate?”

“Yis.”

“On the ground that you’re his widow?”

“Yis. I’ll tell yer﻿—”

“No, you won’t tell me anything. I’ll tell *you*. If you are to have any hope of succeeding in this case, you must furnish me with the name of the priest or parson who married you, the place where you were married, and the date. It must be a real priest or parson, a real place, and a real date. It’s no use coming along with a story of a marriage by a parson and you’ve forgotten his name, at a place you can’t remember where it was, and a date that’s slipped your memory. You must have a story to tell, and it must hold water. Now, can you tell such a story? Have you got any proofs at all?”

Peggy shifted about uneasily.

“Can I see Mick?” she said.

“No, you can not. You must out with it here and now. Listen to me, Peggy,” he went on, sinking his voice suddenly and looking hard at her. “I’ve got to know all about this. It’s no use keeping anything back. Were you ever married to William Grant?”

Peggy dropped her voice too.

“Yis. I was married twenty-five years ago at a place called Pike’s pub, out in the Never-never country.”

“Who read the service, parson or priest?”

“Neither. A mish’nary. Mish’nary to the blacks.”

“Is he alive?”

“No, he died out there. He was sick then, wid the Queensland fever.”

“What was his name?”

“Mr. Nettleship.”

“Was the marriage ever registered?”

“Sorra one of me knows. He giv us each a bit of paper﻿—our marriage lines. ’Twas written in pencil. He had no ink in the place, and he had no books wid him. He tore the sheet of paper and give us each half, wid the writing on it; his horses got stole and he had to camp there. He stayed round wid Pike and the blacks till he died.”

“And where is the certificate? Have you lost it?”

“I sint mine down to Mick to keep for me﻿—jist a bit of paper written in pencil it was﻿—and it got lost some ways; but I have a copy of it I med at the time.”

“Where is the copy now?”

“At Mick’s place.”

“You must tell Mick to bring it in. Now where is this place, Pike’s?”

“Out this side of the opal-fields. It’s wild and rough now, but what it was then﻿—well ’twas more like a black’s camp nor a white man’s place at all.”

===Chinese===
佩吉仍然有些挑衅地在椅子上坐定。她在北昆士兰战斗了太久，既不害怕也不尊敬任何人；但她的天生精明告诉她，照律师的建议做，只有好处没有坏处。

「好了，佩吉，」他说，「你想对威廉·格兰特的遗产提出索赔吗？」

「是的。」

「理由是你是他遗孀？」

「是的。我告诉您——」

「不，你不用告诉我任何事。我来告诉*你*。如果你想在这个案子上有成功的希望，你必须提供给我主持婚礼的牧师或神父的名字、你结婚的地点和日期。必须是一个真实的牧师或神父、一个真实的地点和一个真实的日期。带着一个故事来说你跟一个牧师结了婚但忘了他的名字、在一个记不清的地方、日期也记不清了，那是没用的。你必须有一个说得通的故事。现在，你能讲出这样一个故事吗？你有任何证据吗？」

佩吉不安地动了动。

「我能见米克吗？」她说。

「不，你不能。你必须在这里、现在就全说出来。听我说，佩吉，」他突然压低声音，严厉地看着她继续说道。「我必须了解这一切。隐瞒任何事都没用。你曾经跟威廉·格兰特结过婚吗？」

佩吉也压低了声音。

「是的。我二十五年前在「无人之地」（Never-never country）一个叫派克酒馆（Pike's pub）的地方结了婚。」

「谁主持的婚礼，牧师还是神父？」

「都不是。一个传教士（mish'nary）。给黑人传教的传教士。」

「他还活着吗？」

「不，他死在那儿了。他那时病了，得了昆士兰热病。」

「他叫什么名字？」

「内特尔希普先生（Mr. Nettleship）。」

「婚礼登记了吗？」

「天知道。他给了我们每人一张纸——我们的结婚证书。是用铅笔写的。他那儿没有墨水，也没有书。他把一张纸撕开，给了我们每人一半，上面写着字；他的马被偷了，他不得不在那里露营。他跟派克和黑人待在一起直到去世。」

「那证书在哪儿？你弄丢了吗？」

「我寄给米克保管了——就是一张铅笔写的纸——不知道怎么弄丢了；但我当时抄了一份。」

「抄本现在在哪儿？」

「在米克那儿。」

「你必须让米克把它带来。那派克酒馆在哪儿？」

「在蛋白石矿区（opal-fields）这边。现在很荒凉，但那时候——嗯，更像是黑人的营地，根本不像白人的地方。

===Original===
Blake thought the story had gone far enough. He did not believe a word of it. “Look here, Peggy,” he said. “You have given the place, the date, the name of the parson, and everything. Now you know that if you are telling a lie it will be easily found out. They will soon find out if there was such a missionary, and if he was up there at the time, and if Mr. Grant was up there; and if you are caught out in a lie it may go hard with you. Have you any witnesses?”

“Martin Doyle was there, Black Martin’s son.”

“What! Martin Doyle that’s out at the nine-mile?”

“Yis. He was up driving the buggy and horses for Grant. He can swear to the wedding.”

“He can.”

“Yis.”

Blake sat back in his chair and looked at her. “Do you mean to tell me,” he said, “that you can show me a certificate and a witness to your marriage with William Grant?”

Peggy looked doggedly down at the floor and said, in the tones of one who is repeating the burial service or some other solemn function, “I can prove the marriage.”

Blake was puzzled. He had known the mountain folk all his life, and knew that for uneducated people﻿—or perhaps because they were uneducated people﻿—they were surprisingly clever liars. But he never dreamt that any of them could hoodwink him; so he put Peggy once more through the whole story﻿—made her describe all her actions on the day of the wedding, where she stood, where the witness stood, what the parson said, what her husband said. He went through the whole thing, and could see no flaw in it. He knew that Peggy would not scruple to lie to him; but, with the contempt of a clever man, he felt satisfied that he could soon upset any concocted story. This story seemed to hold water, and the more he cross-examined her the more sure he was that there was something genuine about it; at the same time, he was sure that it was not all genuine. Then a thought occurred to him.

“Would you settle this case if they offered you something?” he said.

“I’ll do whatever you say,” said Peggy, rising. “ ’Tis for you to say what I ought to do. ’Tis not for the like of me, that is no scholar.”

“Leave it to me,” said Blake. “I’ll do what is best for you. Send Martin Doyle in to see me, Martin that was the witness. And about this copy of the certificate, tell Mick to bring it in here. Now you go home, and don’t you say to one living soul one word of what has passed in here. Tell them you are going on with the case, but don’t say any more, or you may land yourself in gaol. Do you hear me?”

And the cowed and flustered Peggy hurried away to join her brother, who was far too wise to ask questions.

“Least said soonest mended,” he said, when told that Blake required silence.

After his clients had gone, Gavan Blake sat for half an hour almost dazed. If Peggy’s story was true, then Mary Grant was an outcast instead of a great heiress. And while he had become genuinely fond of her (which he never was of Ellen Harriott), he had no idea of asking her to share his debts with him. He puzzled over the affair for a long time, and at last his clear brain saw a way out of all difficulties. He would go over to the old station, put the whole case before Mary Grant, and induce her for peace’ sake to give Peggy money to withdraw her claim. Out of this money he himself would keep enough to pay all his pressing debts. He would be that much to the good whatever happened, and afterwards would have an added claim on Mary Grant’s sympathies for having relieved her of a vast lawsuit in which her fortune, and even her very name, were involved.

This plan seemed to him the best for all parties﻿—for himself especially, which was the most important thing. If he could get a large sum to settle the case, he could make Peggy give him a big share for his trouble, and then at last be free from the haunting fear of exposure and ruin. He felt sure that he was doing quite right in advising Mary Grant to pay.

Again and again he ran over Peggy’s case in his mind, and could see no flaw in it. In the old days haphazard marriages were rather the rule than the exception, and such things as registers were never heard of in far-out parts. His trained mind, going through the various questions that a cross-examiner would ask, and supplying the requisite answers, decided that, though it might seem a trifle improbable, there was nothing contradictory about Peggy’s story. A jury would sympathise with her, and the decisions of the Courts all leaned towards presuming marriage where certain circumstances existed. By settling the case he would do Mary Grant a real kindness. And afterwards﻿—well, she would probably be as grateful as when he had saved her life. He saw himself the hero of the hour: ever prompt to decide, he saddled a horse, and at once rode off to Kuryong to put the matter before her.

===Chinese===
布莱克觉得这个故事讲得够长了。他一个字也不信。「听着，佩吉，」他说。「你已经给出了地点、日期、牧师的名字和一切。你知道如果你在撒谎，很容易就会被发现。他们会很快查出是否有这样一个传教士，他当时是否在那儿，格兰特先生是否在那儿；如果你被抓住撒谎，你会很不好过。你有证人吗？」

「马丁·多尔（Martin Doyle）在那儿，黑人马丁（Black Martin）的儿子。」

「什么！九英里外的马丁·多尔？」

「是的。他当时是给格兰特赶马车的。他可以为婚礼作证。」

「他可以。」

「是的。」

布莱克靠在椅子上看着她。「你是说，」他说，「你能给我看一张证书和一个证人来证明你与威廉·格兰特的婚姻？」

佩吉固执地盯着地板，用念葬礼祷文或其他庄严仪式的语气说：「我能证明这段婚姻。」

布莱克感到困惑。他一生都认识山地人，知道对于未受过教育的人——或者也许因为他们未受过教育——他们是令人惊讶的聪明骗子。但他从未想到他们中的任何人能愚弄他；于是他又让佩吉把整个故事讲了一遍——让她描述婚礼当天的所有行动，她站在哪里，证人站在哪里，牧师说了什么，她丈夫说了什么。他把整个过程过了一遍，没发现任何破绽。他知道佩吉会毫不犹豫地对他撒谎；但作为一个聪明人，他带着轻蔑，确信自己很快就能推翻任何编造的故事。这个故事似乎站得住脚，他越是盘问她，就越确信其中有真实成分；同时，他也确信它不全是真实的。然后一个念头闪过他的脑海。

「如果他们给你点什么，你会接受这个案子吗？」他说。

「您说什么我就做什么，」佩吉站起来说。「该怎么做由您决定。像我这样没文化的人没资格说话。」

「交给我吧，」布莱克说。「我会为你做最好的打算。让马丁·多尔来见我，那个当证人的马丁。关于证书抄本，让米克带到这里来。现在你回家去，不要对任何人说这里发生的事。告诉他们你将继续这个案子，但不要多说，否则你可能会进监狱。你听到了吗？」

被吓坏了的佩吉赶紧去和她哥哥会合，他明智地没有问任何问题。

「话越少越好，」当他得知布莱克要求保密时说。

当事人走后，加文·布莱克坐了半个小时，几乎茫然若失。如果佩吉的故事是真的，那么玛丽·格兰特（Mary Grant）就是一个弃儿，而不是一位伟大的女继承人。虽然他真心喜欢她（他对埃伦·哈里奥特从未如此），但他从未想过要她分担他的债务。他思考这件事很长时间，最后他清晰的头脑想出了一个摆脱困境的办法。他要去老牧场，把整个案子告诉玛丽·格兰特，劝她为了安宁，给佩吉钱让她撤销索赔。他自己会从这笔钱中留够还清所有急债的钱。无论发生什么，他都会因此受益，而且之后还会因为替她免除了一场涉及她财产甚至身份的巨额诉讼，而获得玛丽·格兰特更多的同情。

这个计划在他看来对各方都是最好的——尤其是对他自己，这才是最重要的。如果他能拿到一大笔钱来解决这个案子，他就能让佩吉分给他一大份作为辛苦费，然后终于摆脱暴露和毁灭的萦绕之忧。他确信建议玛丽·格兰特付款是完全正确的。

他一遍又一遍地回想佩吉的案子，看不出任何破绽。在过去，随意结婚是常事而非例外，登记之类的事情在偏远地区从未听说过。他训练有素的头脑，经过盘问者会问的各种问题，并提供了必要的答案，认定虽然佩吉的故事可能显得有点不太可能，但没有什么矛盾之处。陪审团会同情她，法院的判决也都倾向于在某些情况下推定婚姻成立。通过解决这个案子，他将给玛丽·格兰特一个真正的帮助。而且之后——嗯，她可能会像他救了她命时那样感激他。他看到自己成了当下的英雄：他果断地备好马，立刻骑马前往库里昂，把这件事告诉她。

## XXI No Compromise / 第二十一章 不妥协

===Original===
While Gavan Blake was conferring with his clients, a very different sort of conference was being held at Kuryong. The return of Charlie Gordon, accompanied by Carew, had been voted by common consent an occasion for holiday; and although, according to theory, a bush holiday is invariably spent in kangaroo-hunting, yet the fact is that men who are in the saddle from daylight to dark, from weekend to weekend, generally spend a holiday resting legs that are cramped from the saddle, and arms that ache from lifting sheep over hurdles or swinging the gates of drafting-yards.

Thus it was that, on the holiday at Kuryong, the Bachelors' Quarters—two large dormitory-like rooms that opened into one another—were full of athletic male figures sprawling on the beds, smoking black pipes all day, and yarning interminably. The main topic of conversation was Peggy's claim against the estate. They had all heard the rumours that were going round; each had quietly been trying to find out what Peggy had to go on, and this powwow was utilised for the purpose of comparing notes. They had one advantage over Gavan Blake—they knew all about Considine, which Blake did not.

On one bed lay Pinnock, who had come up to make arrangements for carrying on the station till the will was proved. On another bed sprawled Carew, who, by virtue of his trip out back, was looked upon as a bit of an oracle by Poss and Binjie, who had never been further than the mountains. Poss and Binjie had dragged an old couch out of the next room and were stretched on that, listening to the talk, and occasionally throwing in a word of such wisdom as they had. Hugh sat in an armchair by the window, smoking and dreaming.

===Chinese===
当加文·布莱克（Gavan Blake）与他的客户商议时，库里昂（Kuryong）正在举行一场截然不同的会议。查理·戈登（Charlie Gordon）和卡鲁（Carew）的归来被一致认定为放假的理由；尽管按照理论，丛林假日总是用来猎袋鼠，但事实上那些从天亮到天黑、从周末到周末都骑在马上的人，通常会在假日里休息因骑马而僵硬的腿，和因把羊举过栅栏或开关分群围栏大门而酸痛的手臂。

因此，在库里昂假日这天，单身汉宿舍——两间相通的大房间，像宿舍一样——里躺满了健壮的男性，他们整天躺在床铺上，抽着黑烟斗，没完没了地闲聊。主要话题是佩格（Peggy）对遗产的索赔。他们都听过四处流传的谣言；每个人都曾悄悄试图打听佩格有什么依据，而这次聚会正是为了交换信息。他们有一个加文·布莱克所没有的优势——他们了解康西丁（Considine）的一切，而布莱克不知道。

平诺克（Pinnock）躺在一张床上，他是来安排在遗嘱验证期间继续经营牧场的事务。卡鲁摊在另一张床上，由于他去过内陆深处，波斯（Poss）和宾吉（Binjie）——他们从未越过山脉——把他当作某种先知。波斯和宾吉从隔壁房间拖来一张旧躺椅，躺在上面听他们聊天，偶尔插上一两句自以为聪明的话。休（Hugh）坐在窗边的扶手椅里，抽着烟，做着梦。

===Original===
Poss's voice cut knife-like through a cloud of tobacco smoke. He spoke as one on the defensive.

"Well, I believe there's something in it, anyhow. Briney Donohoe told me—"

Charlie Cordon's cold drawl interrupted the youth. "It's all rot," he said. "Briney Donohoe told you—what does *he* know about it? You two boys and Hugh have been stuck at home here so long, you believe anything. I tell you, they'll do nothing. It's all talk, just to make themselves big people. They have nothing to do just now, so it comes in handy as an excuse to ride from one selection to another all day long and leave our gates open. We have Peggy's measure, haven't we, Carew? That long-lost relation of yours, old Considine!"

"I wish you did have him," said the lawyer. "He might come in very handy. With a big property like this to go for, they are nearly sure to have a try at it."

Poss took heart at finding himself supported by this new champion. "Yes," he said. "Red Mick and Peggy are down at Gavan Blake's today. I saw their horses hanging up outside as I came through. And Briney Donohoe told me—"

"What do you think, Carew?" said Charlie, cutting Briney Donohoe off again. "Don't you think that old fellow was telling the truth when he said he married Peggy?"

"Sure he was," said the Englishman. "Never saw a fellow in such a funk in my life."

"What about Peggy?" said Pinnock. "How did she take it?"

"Bold as brass! I thought she was going to kiss Charlie there, when she found out who he was."

===Chinese===
波斯的声音像刀子一样穿透烟雾。他说话时像在自卫。

"嗯，不管怎样，我相信这里面有点东西。布赖尼·多诺霍（Briney Donohoe）告诉我——"

查理·戈登（Charlie Gordon）冷冷的拖腔打断了这个年轻人。"全是胡扯，"他说，"布赖尼·多诺霍告诉你——*他*知道什么？你们两个小子和休在家里待太久了，什么都信。我告诉你，他们什么也不会做。全是空谈，只是为了让自己显得重要。他们现在没事做，所以这正好成了他们整天从一个农场骑到另一个农场、让我们大门敞开的借口。我们了解佩格的底细，对吧，卡鲁？你那个失散多年的亲戚，老康西丁！"

"我倒希望你真的有他，"律师说，"他可能很派得上用场。像这么大一笔财产，他们几乎肯定会试一试的。"

波斯发现这位新支持者站在自己一边，来了精神。"是啊，"他说，"红米克（Red Mick）和佩格今天在加文·布莱克那里。我经过时看到他们的马挂在门外。布赖尼·多诺霍告诉我——"

"你怎么看，卡鲁？"查理又打断了布赖尼·多诺霍，"你不觉得那老头说他娶了佩格是在说实话吗？"

"当然是说实话，"英国人说，"我这辈子从没见过那么害怕的人。"

"佩格呢？"平诺克问，"她反应如何？"

"厚脸皮得很！我发现她认出查理时，差点就要亲他了。"

===Original===
Pinnock laughed. "Funny thing," he said, "a woman like Peggy having the chance to choose between two fortunes. Pity we couldn't induce her to take the old bushman and be done with it. How much money has he come into, Carew?"

"Oh, plenty of money. But of course there's an old place to keep up, and the death duties are very heavy. Very expensive thing having money left you in England, you know."

Charlie Gordon turned to Pinnock. "What you ought to do," he said (the far-out man who has to shift for himself is always quite sure he can settle all difficulties better than those whose profession it is), "what you ought to do," he repeated, "is to send someone to Peggy and tell her not to be such a fool. Tell her to stick to old Considine. That's what you ought to do."

"Well, suppose *you* go and do it. You know the lady better than anyone here, seemingly. But if she has been to see Blake, I expect the fat's in the fire by this time."

"I don't think much of Blake takin' up the case," said Binjie, "after the old lady asked him here. It's doing the blacksnake act, I call it. I don't suppose he'll come here any more after this."

Hugh still sat looking out of the window, smoking silently. "Here comes Blake now, anyhow," he said. "He's just coming up the flat."

" Wants to see me, I expect," said Pinnock. "We'll know all about it now. Must have heard I was here, and is come to declare war or sue for peace. Someone had better go and meet him, I suppose."

===Chinese===
平诺克笑了。"真有趣，"他说，"像佩格这样的女人居然有机会在两笔财产之间做选择。可惜我们没能劝她接受那个老丛林人，一了百了。他继承了多少钱，卡鲁？"

"哦，很多钱。但当然有个老宅子要维护，遗产税也很重。在英格兰继承遗产是件很花钱的事，你知道的。"

查理·戈登转向平诺克。"你应该做的，"他说（那个必须自力更生的远方人总觉得自己比专业人士更能解决所有困难），"你应该做的，"他重复道，"是派人去找佩格，告诉她别犯傻。告诉她坚持和老康西丁在一起。这是你应该做的。"

"嗯，*你*去做好了。看来你比这里任何人都了解这位女士。但如果她已经去见过布莱克，我想现在事情已经闹大了。"

"我不太看得起布莱克接手这个案子，"宾吉说，"老太太请他来这里之后。我认为这是黑蛇的行径。我猜他以后不会再来了。"

休仍然坐在那里望着窗外，默默地抽烟。"不管怎样，布莱克来了，"他说，"他刚走上平地。"

"我想他是来见我的，"平诺克说，"我们现在会知道一切了。他一定听说我在这里，是来宣战还是求和的。我想最好有人去迎接他。"

===Original===
"Dashed if I'll go," said Poss. "I don't care about a chap that doesn't act white. I saw Red Mick's and Peggy's horses at his office today, and now he comes up here as bold as brass."

"Let him go round to the front," said Hugh, "and then he can ask the servants for whoever he wants. If we go out and meet him, we'll have to ask him to stay."

The approach to houses in the bush is generally by way of the yard where the horses arrive, and it is very unusual for anyone, except a stranger making a formal visit, to be allowed to find their way round to the front.

Blake rode up and gave his horse to the horse-boy. "Put him in the stable for a while," he said. "I may want him again." Then he went round to the front door and asked for Mrs. Gordon.

"I have come to see Miss Grant on very important business," he said when the old lady came in. "Would you ask her if she would see me?"

The old lady was in a quandary. She had heard all the rumours that were going about, but she knew that they had been kept from Mary Grant, and she thought that if Blake meant to talk business he might shock or startle the girl terribly.

"Mr. Pinnock the lawyer is here," she said. "Perhaps you had better see him. Miss Grant does not know—"

"I am come as a friend of Miss Grant's, Mrs. Gordon," he said. "But, if Mr. Pinnock is here, perhaps it would be better for me to see him first. Shall I wait for him here?"

"If you will go into the office I will send him in there," and the old lady withdrew to talk of commonplace matters with Mary, all the time feeling that a great crisis was at hand.

===Chinese===
"我才不去呢，"波斯说，"我不喜欢那种不光明正大的家伙。我今天看到红米克和佩格的马挂在他办公室外，现在他倒厚着脸皮上这儿来了。"

"让他绕到前门去，"休说，"这样他可以向仆人打听他要找谁。如果我们出去迎接他，就得请他留下来。"

在丛林里，房子的入口通常是马匹到达的院子，除了正式拜访的陌生人，很少有人被允许绕到前门去。

布莱克骑马过来，把马交给马倌。"把他关进马厩一会儿，"他说，"我可能还需要他。"然后他绕到前门，请求见戈登太太。

"我是来见格兰特小姐（Miss Grant），有非常重要的事，"老太太进来时他说，"您能问问她是否愿意见我吗？"

老太太很为难。她听过所有流传的谣言，但她知道这些谣言一直瞒着玛丽·格兰特（Mary Grant），她觉得如果布莱克是要谈正事，可能会让姑娘非常震惊或惊吓。

"律师平诺克先生在这里，"她说，"也许你最好见他。格兰特小姐不知道——"

"我是作为格兰特小姐的朋友来的，戈登太太，"他说，"但是，如果平诺克先生在这里，也许我最好先见他。我在这里等他好吗？"

"如果你去办公室，我会让他去那里找你，"老太太退下去和玛丽聊些家常，心里一直感觉大难临头。

===Original===
Soon the two lawyers faced one another over the office table, and Blake got to business at once.

"Mr. Pinnock," he said, "I am asked to act for Margaret Donohoe, or Margaret Grant as she claims to be; and I want you to believe that I am seriously telling you what I believe to be the truth, when I say that Miss Grant had better settle this case."

"Why should she pay one penny? What proofs have you? It looks to me, with all respect to you, Mr. Blake, like an ordinary case of blackmail."

"If it were blackmail," said Blake quietly, "do you think that I would be here, giving you particulars of the case? I tell you, man, I am ready now to give you all particulars, and you can soon see whether to advise a settlement or not."

"Fire away, then," said Pinnock. "It will take a lot to convince me, though, and so I tell you."

Blake gave him the particulars gleaned from Peggy. "I have examined and cross-examined and re-cross-examined her, and I can't shake her story."

Pinnock listened with an immovable face, but his mind was working like lightning. As the name of the missionary and Pike's Hotel were mentioned, he remembered that he had seen these very names on the butts of Grant's chequebooks. Getting Blake to excuse him for a moment, he hurried to his room and pulled out a bundle of cheque-butts. The best diary of many a man is found in his cheque-butts. There he saw on the very date mentioned by Blake, cheques drawn to "Self and P.", also one drawn to "Pike accommodation," and one simply to the name of Nettleship for five pounds. Of course it was quite possible that the latter was only a donation to charity, such as old Bully was occasionally very free with; but, taken together, the whole lot made Blake's story look unpleasantly probable. Pinnock whistled to himself as he tied the bundle up again. "Case of settle or be sorry," he said to himself. "I wonder how much will settle it?"

When he faced Blake again, he had pulled the mask of professional stolidity over his features; also he lied boldly.

"I can see nothing to corroborate this story," he said; "but it may be that Miss Grant would rather pay a few pounds than have the unpleasantness of a trial. I will get her in and ask her if you like, but I don't think it will lead to anything."

===Chinese===
很快，两位律师在办公桌前面对面坐下，布莱克立刻切入正题。

"平诺克先生，"他说，"我是受玛格丽特·多诺霍（Margaret Donohoe）——或者她自称的玛格丽特·格兰特（Margaret Grant）——委托来的；我希望你相信我是在认真地告诉你我认为的真相，即格兰特小姐最好了结这个案子。"

"她为什么要付一分钱？你有什么证据？恕我直言，布莱克先生，这在我看来就是一起普通的敲诈案。"

"如果是敲诈，"布莱克平静地说，"你觉得我会在这里向你透露案子的细节吗？我告诉你，老兄，我现在准备把所有细节都告诉你，你很快就能判断是否应该建议和解了。"

"那就说吧，"平诺克说，"不过我告诉你，要让我信服可不容易。"

布莱克把从佩格那里打听到的细节告诉了他。"我反复盘问、交叉盘问、再交叉盘问她，无法动摇她的说法。"

平诺克面无表情地听着，但他的脑子像闪电一样运转。当提到传教士和派克旅馆（Pike's Hotel）的名字时，他记起在格兰特的支票簿存根上见过这些名字。他让布莱克稍等片刻，赶紧回到房间，拿出一叠支票存根。许多人的最佳日记都在他的支票存根里。他看到在布莱克提到的日期，有开给"本人和P."的支票，还有一张开给"派克住宿"的，还有一张开给内特尔希普（Nettleship）五英镑的。当然，后者可能只是慈善捐款，老"恶霸"格兰特偶尔也会很慷慨；但综合来看，整件事让布莱克的故事显得不祥地可信。平诺克一边重新捆好支票存根，一边自言自语地吹口哨。"要么和解，要么后悔，"他自言自语，"不知道要多少钱才能了结？"

当他再次面对布莱克时，已经戴上了职业性的冷漠面具；而且他大胆地撒了谎。

"我看不出什么能证实这个故事，"他说，"不过格兰特小姐可能宁愿付几英镑，也不愿经历不愉快的审判。如果你愿意，我可以请她进来问问，不过我觉得这不会有结果。"

===Original===
They were holding their conference in the office. Outside, the station was dozing in the sun. The house dog slept in the yard, and a stray wild pigeon had come down into the quadrangle, and was picking at some grain that was spilt there. From the garden came the shouts of the children and the happy laughter of Mary Grant.

"There she is now," said Pinnock. "Hadn't I better get her to come in and get the thing over?"

He went out, and came back very soon. "Mrs. Gordon and Miss Grant are coming," he said. "She said she would like Mrs. Gordon to be with her."

Before long they came in and sat down. Mary Grant had no idea what she was wanted for. She greeted Blake with a glad smile, and waited to hear what Pinnock had to say. It did not take the lawyer long to put the story before her: but it was some time before she could understand it. Nothing so tragic had ever entered her life before, and she seemed almost stunned.

Mrs. Gordon moved to her side and took her hand.

"It is very terrible for you—for us all, dear," she said. "You must listen to what Mr. Pinnock says, and make up your mind. He can advise you best what to do."

Again Pinnock went through the case. As a full understanding broke in on her, she drew herself up; the look of distress and perplexity left her face, and her eyes were full of scorn and anger.

"Hello, what's coming now?" thought Pinnock. "I hope she says nothing rash."

She tried to speak once or twice, but the words seemed to choke her.

"What do you advise me to do, Mr. Pinnock?" she said, turning to him suddenly.

"I advise you to give me power to act for you in the matter as I think best," said Pinnock, who saw that matters were likely to slip beyond his control. "From what Mr. Blake tells me, I daresay this woman can give you a lot of trouble and annoyance. Whatever you pay her, you won't miss the money. You will save the family here from being turned out; you will avoid scandal; and if there should be any foundation for Mr. Blake's story, it may mean that if you don't settle you lose everything."

===Chinese===
他们正在办公室里开会。外面，牧场在阳光下昏昏欲睡。家犬在院子里睡着，一只迷路的野鸽子飞进院子，在啄食撒在那里的谷粒。花园里传来孩子们的喊叫声和玛丽·格兰特快乐的笑声。

"她来了，"平诺克说，"我最好请她进来把事情了结了吧？"

他出去了，很快回来了。"戈登太太和格兰特小姐来了，"他说，"她说她希望戈登太太和她一起。"

不久她们进来坐下。玛丽·格兰特不知道叫她来做什么。她高兴地微笑着向布莱克打招呼，等着听平诺克要说的话。律师很快把故事告诉了她：但她花了一些时间才理解。她的人生中从未遭遇过如此悲剧，她似乎几乎惊呆了。

戈登太太走到她身边，握住她的手。

"这对你——对我们所有人来说都太可怕了，亲爱的，"她说，"你必须听平诺克先生的话，做出决定。他最能建议你该怎么做。"

平诺克又把案子讲了一遍。当她完全理解时，她挺直了身子；痛苦和困惑的表情从她脸上消失，她的眼中充满了轻蔑和愤怒。

"哎呀，这是要干嘛？"平诺克心想，"希望她别说冲动的话。"

她试着说了几次，但话似乎哽在喉咙里。

"你建议我怎么做，平诺克先生？"她突然转向他问道。

"我建议你授权我代表你处理此事，按我认为最好的方式，"平诺克说，他看到事情可能要失控了。"据布莱克先生告诉我，这个女人肯定会给你带来很多麻烦和困扰。无论你付给她多少钱，你都不会心疼这笔钱。你会保住这里的家人免于被赶出去；你会避免丑闻；而且如果布莱克先生的故事有任何根据，那可能意味着如果你不和解，就会失去一切。"

===Original===
From him Mary Grant turned to the old lady.

"Mrs. Gordon," she said, "do you advise me to pay this money?"

"My dear, I don't advise at all. Don't consider us in the matter at all. It is for you to say."

"Then I will pay nothing. It is a cruel, infamous, wicked slander. These poor, ignorant people don't know what they are doing. Sooner than pay one penny in compromise, I will walk off this station a pauper. God will not let such villainy win. Mrs. Gordon, surely you don't think that I ought to blacken my father's and mother's name by paying money to keep this claim quiet?"

Here Pinnock broke in on her speech. "But if they should manage to produce evidence—"

"Let them produce it, and let the judge believe it if he likes. You and I and everybody know that it is a lie; even if they win the case, it is still a lie. I will pay nothing—not one halfpenny. My mother's name is more than all the money in the world, and I will not blacken it by compromises. Mr. Pinnock, the case is to be fought out, and if we lose we shall still know that justice is on our side; but if we pay money—"

Mrs. Gordon took her hand, and lifted it to her lips.

"I think you are quite right, my dear. You put us all to shame for even thinking of it."

"I am very sorry, Mr. Blake," the girl went on, "very sorry indeed that you should have come here on such an errand. You saved my life, and if I could pay you for that I would; but this offer is an insult, and I hope that you will never come here again. Whether I am turned out of the old station or not, I hope that you will never come here again." And with that the two ladies walked out, leaving the lawyers looking at each other.

"I am afraid, Mr. Blake," said Pinnock at last, "that we have lost any hope we might ever have had of settling this case."

But Blake, as he rode homewards, felt that he had lost forever a much higher hope. He had played for a high stake on two chances. One of them had failed him. There remained only the chance of pulling Peggy's case through; and he swore that if hard work, skill, and utter unscrupulousness could win that case, it should be won.

===Chinese===
玛丽·格兰特从他转向老太太。

"戈登太太，"她说，"你建议我付这笔钱吗？"

"亲爱的，我完全不建议。这件事完全不要考虑我们。由你决定。"

"那我一分钱也不付。这是残忍、无耻、邪恶的诽谤。这些可怜、无知的人不知道他们在做什么。与其付一分钱和解，我宁愿作为穷光蛋离开这个牧场。上帝不会让这样的恶行得逞。戈登太太，你肯定不会认为我应该用钱来掩盖这个索赔，从而玷污我父母的名声吧？"

这时平诺克打断了她的话。"但如果他们能拿出证据——"

"让他们拿出来吧，如果法官愿意相信就让他相信。你、我、所有人都知道这是谎言；即使他们赢了官司，这仍然是谎言。我一分钱也不付——半个便士也不付。我母亲的名字比世上所有的钱都重要，我不会通过妥协来玷污它。平诺克先生，这个案子必须打到底，如果我们输了，我们仍然知道正义在我们一边；但如果我们付钱——"

戈登太太握住她的手，举到唇边。

"我觉得你完全正确，亲爱的。我们竟然考虑这件事，真是丢脸。"

"我很抱歉，布莱克先生，"姑娘继续说，"真的很抱歉你为此事而来。你救了我的命，如果我能报答你，我会的；但这个提议是一种侮辱，我希望你永远不要再来了。无论我是否被赶出这个老牧场，我希望你永远不要再来了。"说完，两位女士走了出去，留下两位律师面面相觑。

"恐怕，布莱克先生，"平诺克最后说，"我们失去了可能和解的任何希望。"

但布莱克骑马回家时，感到他永远失去了一个更高的希望。他曾用两个机会下注高风险。其中一个让他失望了。现在只剩下佩格的案子能否打赢的机会；他发誓，如果努力、技巧和彻底的不择手段能赢得这场官司，那就必须赢。

## XXII A Nurse and Her Assistant / 护士与她的助手

===Original===
While they were waiting for the great case to come on a sort of depression seemed to spread itself over the station. The owner was mostly shut up in her room with her thoughts; the old lady was trying to comfort her, and Ellen Harriott, with sorrow always at her heart, went about the household work like an automaton. No wonder that as soon as breakfast was over all the men cleared out to work on the run. But one day it so happened that Carew did not go out with the others. The young Englishman was a poor correspondent, and had promised himself a whole quiet day to be spent in explaining by letter to his people at home the mysterious circumstances under which he had found and lost Patrick Henry Considine. Ellen Harriott found him in the office manfully wrestling with some extra long words, and stopped for a few minutes' talk. She had a liking for the young Englishman, and any talk was better than to be left alone with her thoughts.

===Chinese===
他们在等待那桩大案开审期间，牧场（station）里弥漫着一种低落的情绪。女主人整天把自己关在房间里独自沉思；老太太试图安慰她，而埃伦·哈里奥特（Ellen Harriott）心头始终压着悲伤，做家务活像个机械人。难怪早餐一结束，所有男人都出去干活了。但有一天偏偏卡鲁（Carew）没有跟其他人一起出去。这位英国年轻人不擅长写信，他打算安安静静地用一整天给国内的家人写信，解释他发现又失去帕特里克·亨利·康西丁（Patrick Henry Considine）的离奇经过。埃伦·哈里奥特在办公室找到他，他正费力地对付几个特别长的单词，她停下来聊了几分钟。她对这个英国年轻人颇有好感，聊聊天总比独自沉浸在思绪中强。

===Original===
"These are bad times for the old station, Mr. Carew," she said. "We don't know what is going to happen next."

Carew was not going to haul down the flag just yet. "I believe everything'll come all right in the long run, don't you know," he said. "Never give up first hit, you know; see it out—eh, what?"

===Chinese===
"这牧场（station）的日子不好过啊，卡鲁先生，"她说，"谁也不知道接下来会怎么样。"

卡鲁还不打算认输。"我相信到头来一切都会好的，你知道的，"他说，"千万别在第一回合就放弃，明白吗？坚持到底——呃，是不是？"

===Original===
"I want to get away out of this for a while," she said. "I am run down. I think the bush monotony tells on women. I don't want anyone to fall sick, but I do wish I could get a little nursing to do again—just for a change. I would nurse Red Mick himself."

===Chinese===
"我想离开这里一阵子，"她说，"我累垮了。我觉得丛林的单调生活对女人的打击很大。我不希望有人生病，但我真希望能再做一阵子护理工作——哪怕只是换个环境。我甚至愿意去护理红米克（Red Mick）本人。"

===Original===
Is there anything in telepathy? Do coming events sometimes send warnings on ahead? Certain it is that, even as she spoke, a rider on a sweating horse was seen coming at full speed up the flat; he put his horse over the sliprails that led into the house paddock without any hesitation, and came on at a swinging gallop.

"What is this?" said Ellen Harriott, "more trouble? It is only trouble that comes so fast. Why, it is one of Red Mick's nephews!" By this time the rider was up to them; without dismounting he called out "Miss! Please, Miss! There's been an accident. My uncle got run agin a tree and he's all smashed in the head. I'm off to the Doctor now; I'll get the Doctor here by tomorrow night, and would you go out and do aught you can for Mick? There's no one out there but old Granny, and she's helpless like. Will you go?"

===Chinese===
心灵感应这种事存在吗？未来的事有时会不会提前发出警告？确凿无疑的是，就在她说话的当口，一个骑着浑身冒汗的马的人正从平地上全速冲来；他毫不犹豫地让马越过通往住宅围场的滑轨栏杆，一路疾驰而来。

"怎么回事？"埃伦·哈里奥特说，"又出事了？只有坏事才会来得这么快。哎呀，是红米克的一个侄子！"这时骑手已经到了跟前；他没下马就喊道："小姐！拜托了，小姐！出了事故。我叔叔撞到了树上，脑袋全砸烂了。我现在去请医生；明天晚上前能赶到。你能去帮他看看吗？那里只有老奶奶在，她也动不了。你去吗？"

===Original===
"Is he much hurt?"

"I'm afraid he's killed, Miss. I found him, He'd been out all night and the side of his head all busted. After a dingo he was—I seen the tracks. Coming back from Gavan Blake's he must 'a' seen the dorg off the track, and the colt he was on was orkard like and must have hit him agen a tree. The colt kem home with the saddle under his belly, and I run the tracks back till I found him. Will you go out, Miss?"

"Yes," said Ellen, "I will go. And you hurry on now, and get the Doctor. Tell the Doctor I've gone out there." Like an arrow from the bow the young fellow sent his big thoroughbred horse across the paddocks, making a bee line over fences and everything for Tarrong, while Ellen Harriott hurried in to pack up a few things.

===Chinese===
"伤得很重吗？"

"我怕他已经死了，小姐。我找到他的。他在外面待了一整夜，脑袋一侧全砸烂了。他是在追一只野狗——我看到了脚印。从加文·布莱克（Gavan Blake）那儿回来的路上，他一定是看到野狗离开了路面，而他骑的那匹小马又不太听话，准是把他撞到了树上。小马自己跑回了家，鞍子掉到了肚子底下，我顺着脚印往回找，才找到了他。你去吗，小姐？"

"去，"埃伦说，"我去。你快走，去请医生。告诉医生我已经去了。"年轻人像离弦之箭一般策马穿过围场，翻过栅栏，直奔塔龙而去。埃伦·哈里奥特则急忙进去收拾了几样东西。

===Original===
"Can I help you at all?" said Carew, following her into the house. "I'd like to be some use, don't you know; but in this country I seem to be so dashed useless."

"You will be a lot of use if you will come out with me. I shall want someone to drive the trap out, and I may want help with the patient. You are big and strong.

"Yes, and it's about the first time my strength has even been of any use to anybody. I will go and get the trap ready while you dress."

===Chinese===
"我能帮上什么忙吗？"卡鲁跟在她身后进了屋。"我倒想出点力，你知道的；但在这个国家我好像一点用也没有。"

"你要是跟我一起去就帮大忙了。我需要有人赶马车，可能还需要帮手照料病人。你人高马大的。"

"是啊，这大概还是头一回我的力气能对谁有用。你去换衣服，我去把马车备好。"

===Original===
Hurriedly they packed food and blankets into the light buggy, and set off. Miss Harriott knew the tracks well, and the buggy fairly flew along till they came up the flat to Red Mick's. As they drew near the hut a noise of talking and crying came through the open door.

"What's up now?" said Carew. "Crowd of people there."

"No"—Ellen Harriott listened for a second. "No," she said, "he is delirious. That is the old woman crying. Hurry up, Mr. Carew—take the horse out of the buggy and put him in the stable, and then come in as quickly as you can. I may want help."

===Chinese===
他们匆忙把食物和毯子装上轻便马车，出发了。哈里奥特小姐对路线很熟，马车一路飞驰，很快穿过平地到了红米克的住处。靠近棚屋时，从敞开的门里传来说话声和哭喊声。

"怎么了？"卡鲁说，"里面好多人。"

"不——"埃伦·哈里奥特听了一秒钟，"不，"她说，"他神志不清了。是老太太在哭。快，卡鲁先生——把马从车上卸下来牵进马厩，然后尽快进来。我可能需要帮忙。"

===Original===
Leaving Carew to unharness the horse, she went inside. In the inner room on a bunk, lay Red Mick. Eye, nose, forehead, and mouth were all one unrecognisable lump, while fragments of bark and splinters still stuck to the skin. In the corner sat the old mother, crying feebly. Disregarding the old woman, Ellen made a swift examination of Mick's injuries, but as soon as he felt her touch on his face he sprang to his feet and struck at her.

Just as he did so, Carew rushed in and threw his arms round the madman. In that grip even Red Mick had no power to move.

"Just hold him quiet," said Ellen, "till I have a look"—and she rapidly ran her fingers over the wound. "Very bad. I think there must be a bit of the skull pressing on the brain. We can't do much till the Doctor comes. I think he will be quiet now. Will you make a fire and boil some water, so that I can clean and dress the wound. That will ease him a little. And get the blankets in; we can make up some sort of place on the floor to sleep. One of us will have to watch all night. Cranny, you must go to bed, do you hear? Come and sit by Mick till I put Granny to bed."

===Chinese===
她把解马的事交给卡鲁，自己走了进去。里间的铺板上躺着红米克。眼睛、鼻子、额头和嘴巴全成了一团无法辨认的肿块，树皮碎片和木刺还扎在皮肤里。角落里坐着老母亲，无力地哭泣着。埃伦没有理会老太太，迅速检查了米克的伤势，但他一感觉到有人碰他的脸，立刻跳起来朝她挥拳。

就在他动手的瞬间，卡鲁冲了进来，一把抱住这个疯了的人。在他怀里，连红米克也动弹不得。

"先按住他，"埃伦说，"让我看看"——她飞快地用手指摸过伤口。"很严重。我怀疑有一块碎骨压到了脑组织。医生来之前我们做不了太多。我想他现在会安静些。你去生火烧点水，我好清洗和包扎伤口。这样他会好受些。把毯子拿进来；我们在地上凑合铺个睡觉的地方。我们俩得有人通宵守着。克兰尼（Cranny），你去睡觉，听见了吗？过来坐米克旁边，等我把老太太安顿好。"

===Original===
By degrees they got things shipshape—put the old woman to bed, and cleaned and dressed Mick's wounds. Then they settled down for the long night in the sickroom. A strange sickroom it was; but many a hospital is less healthy. Through wide cracks between the slabs there came in the cool, fresh air that in itself is worth more than all the medicines in the pharmacopoeia. The patient had sunk into an uneasy slumber when Ellen made her dispositions for the night.

"You go and lie down now," she said, "in the other room, on the sofa. I will call you if I want you. Get all the sleep you can, and in a couple of hours you can take my place. He may talk, but don't let that disturb you. I will call out loud enough if I need you."

"Mind you do," said the Englishman. "I sleep like a blessed top, you know. Sleep anywhere. Well, good night for the present. He looks a little better since you washed him, doesn't he?"

===Chinese===
他们一点一点把一切收拾妥当——安顿老太太上床，清洗并包扎了米克的伤口。然后他们在病房里坐下来，准备度过漫漫长夜。这是一间奇特的病房；但许多医院还没有这里健康。木板墙的大裂缝透进来清新的空气，光是这空气就抵得过药典里所有的药。病人陷入了不安的浅睡时，埃伦开始安排夜间的值守。

"你先去睡吧，"她说，"在隔壁房间的沙发上。需要你的话我会叫你。能睡多少睡多少，过两个小时你来替我。他可能会说梦话，但别让那打扰你。需要你的话我会大声叫。"

"你可得叫啊，"英国人说，"我睡得跟个陀螺似的，你知道。哪儿都能睡。好吧，先道个晚安。他被你洗过之后看起来好些了，是不是？"

===Original===
He threw himself on the couch in the inner room, and before long a titanic snore showed that he had not overrated his sleeping powers.

Ellen Harriott sat by Red Mick's bedside and thought over the events of the last few weeks. As she thought she half-dozed, but woke with a start to find her patient broad awake again and trying to get at something that was under his bunk. Quietly she drew him back, for his struggles with Carew had left him weak as a child.

He looked at her with crazed eyes.

"The paper," he said, "for the love of God, the paper. I have to take it to Gavan. 'Twill win the case. The paper."

She tried to pacify him, but nothing would do but that she should get the mysterious paper. At last, to humour him, she dived under the bunk and found an iron camp-oven, and in it a single envelope. Just to see what was exciting him she opened the envelope, and found a crumpled piece of paper which she read over to herself. It was the original certificate of the marriage between Patrick Henry Keogh and Margaret Donohoe; if Ellen had only known it, she held in her hand the evidence to sweep away all her friend's troubles. It so happened, however, that it conveyed nothing to her mind. She had heard much about Considine, but not a word about Keogh, and the name "Margaret Donohoe" did not strike her half-asleep mind as referring to Peggy. She put the paper away again in the camp-oven; then, feeling weary, she awoke Carew and lay down on the couch while he watched the patient.

===Chinese===
他扑倒在里间的沙发上，不一会儿就发出了惊天动地的鼾声，证明他对自己的睡眠能力并无夸大。

埃伦·哈里奥特坐在红米克思边，回想过去几周发生的事。想着想着打起了瞌睡，突然惊醒，发现病人又完全清醒了，正试图去够铺板底下的什么东西。她轻轻把他拉回来，因为他和卡鲁的搏斗已经让他虚弱得像个孩子。

他用疯狂的眼神看着她。

"那张纸，"他说，"看在上帝的份上，那张纸。我得把它交给加万。会赢官司的。那张纸。"

她试图安抚他，但他非要拿到那张神秘的纸不可。最后，为了顺着他，她钻到铺板下面，找到一个铁皮野营烤箱，里面有一封信。她打开信封想看看是什么让他这么激动，发现里面是一张皱巴巴的纸，她默读了一遍。那是帕特里克·亨利·基奥（Patrick Henry Keogh）与玛格丽特·多诺霍（Margaret Donohoe）的结婚证书原件；如果埃伦知道的话，她手里拿着的正是消除她朋友所有麻烦的证据。然而，她对此毫无概念。她听说过很多关于康西丁的事，但对基奥这个名字一无所知，而"玛格丽特·多诺霍"这个名字在她半梦半醒的脑子里也没联想到佩吉（Peggy）。她把纸放回了烤箱；然后感到疲惫，她叫醒了卡鲁，自己躺到沙发上，由他来看护病人。

===Original===
Next morning the Doctor arrived with a trail of Red Mick's relations after him; among them they arranged to take him into Tarrong to be operated on, and Ellen Harriott and Carew drove back to Kuryong feeling as if they had known each other all their lives.

===Chinese===
第二天早上医生到了，后面跟着一长串红米克的亲戚；大家商量后决定把米克送到塔龙去做手术。埃伦·哈里奥特和卡鲁驱车返回库里昂（Kuryong），彼此的感觉像是相识了一辈子。

===Original===
As they drove along she wondered idly which of Red Mick's innumerable relatives the paper referred to, and why Mick was so anxious about it; but by the time they arrived at home the matter passed from her mind, except that she remembered well enough what was written on the odd-looking little scrap.

===Chinese===
回程路上，她漫不经心地想着那张纸说的是红米克哪一位数不清的亲戚，以及米克为什么那么着急；但等到回到家时，这事已经从她脑海中淡去了，只是那张模样古怪的小纸片上写的内容她记得清清楚楚。

===Original===
"I will give you a certificate as a competent wardsman if ever you want one," she said to Carew as he helped her out of the buggy. "I don't know what I'd have done without you."

"You'd have managed somehow, I'll bet," he said, looking at the confident face before him. "Quite a bit of fun, wasn't it? I hope we have a few more excursions together."

And she felt that she rather hoped so, too.

===Chinese===
"如果你需要的话，我可以给你开一份合格护工的证明，"卡鲁扶她下马车时，她对他说，"没有你我真不知道该怎么办。"

"我敢打赌你自己也能应付，"他说着，望着眼前这张充满自信的脸。"挺有意思的，不是吗？希望我们能再一起出去几趟。"

她觉得，自己也确实如此希望。

## XXIII Hugh Goes in Search / 休踏上寻人之路

===Original===
Who does not remember the first exciting news of the great *Grant v. Grant* will case? The leading Q.C.'s. watched eagerly for briefs; juniors who held even the smallest briefs in connection with it patronised their fellows, and explained to them intricate legal dodges which they themselves had thought out and "pumped into" their learned leaders. "Took me a doose of a time to get him to see it, but I think he has got it at last," they used to say. The case looked like lasting for years, for there would be appeals and counter-appeals, references, inquiries and whatnot; and in getting ready for the first fight the lawyers on each side worked like beavers.
Blake let it be known among the clans that he was going to fight the case for Peggy, and that there was going to be a lawsuit such as the most veteran campaigner of them all had never even dimly imagined—a lawsuit with the happiness of a beautiful woman and the disposal of a vast fortune at stake. Word was carried from selection to selection, across trackless mountain-passes, and over dangerous river crossings, until even Larry, the outermost Donohoe, heard the news in his rocky fastness, miscalled a grazing lease, away in the gullies under the shadows of Black Andrew mountain. By some mysterious means it even reached Briney Doyle, who was camped out near the foothills of Kosciuszko, running wild horses into trap-yards. This occupation had taken such hold on him that he had become as wild as the horses he pursued, and it was popularly supposed that the other Doyles had to go out with horses to run him in whenever they wanted him.

===Chinese===
有谁不记得那场轰动一时的"格兰特诉格兰特"（Grant v. Grant）遗嘱案的最初消息？资深御用大律师们（Q.C.）急切地等待着聘用；就连手握最小案子委托的初级律师也对自己的同行摆出居高临下的态度，向他们解释自己想出来并"灌输给"博学的前辈们的那些复杂法律手段。"我费了好大劲才让他明白，不过我觉得他终于懂了，"他们过去常常这样说。这案子看起来要打好几年，因为会有上诉和反上诉、提交审议、调查等等没完没了的程序；而在准备第一场交锋时，双方的律师都像海狸一样拼命工作。
布莱克（Blake）在族人中放出风声，说他要为佩吉（Peggy）打这场官司，说将要打一场连最有经验的老将都闻所未闻的官司——一场关乎一位美丽女子的幸福和一笔巨大财产处置的官司。消息从一个农庄传到另一个农庄，越过人迹罕至的山隘，渡过危险的河流渡口，直到最偏僻的多诺霍家（Donohoes）的拉里（Larry）在他那座崎岖的隐居处——一处被误称为牧业租赁地的地方——在布莱克·安德鲁山（Black Andrew Mountain）阴影下的沟壑里听到了消息。通过某种神秘的方式，消息甚至传到了科修斯科山（Kosciuszko）山麓附近露营的布赖尼·多尔（Briney Doyle）那里，他正在把野马赶进陷阱围栏。这项营生如此迷住了他，以至于他变得像他追逐的那些马一样野性难驯，人们普遍认为，其他多尔家（Doyle）的人每次想找他，都得骑马出去追他回来。

===Original===
Peggy brought in the copy of her marriage certificate, an old and faded piece of paper which ran—"This is to certify that I, Thomas Nettleship, duly ordained clergyman of the Church of England, have this day solemnized a marriage between William Grant, Bachelor, and Margaret Donohoe, Spinster."
The name of Pike's Hotel and the date were nearly illegible, but there the document was; and though it was not the original certificate, it was pretty clear that Peggy could never have invented it. Its production made a great impression. It certainly went far to convince Blake.
He had cross-examined all the witnesses, had checked their accounts by each other, had followed William Grant's career at that time, had got on to the history of the bush missionary; and everything fitted in. Martin Doyle—Black Martin's son Martin—was letter-perfect in his part. Peggy could give the details of the ceremony with unfaltering accuracy fifty times a day if need be, and never contradict herself. So at last he gave up trying to find holes in the case, and determined to go in and win.

===Chinese===
佩吉拿出了她的结婚证书副本，一张陈旧褪色的纸，上面写着——"兹证明本人托马斯·内特尔希普（Thomas Nettleship），英国国教会正式按立牧师，于今日主持威廉·格兰特（William Grant），单身汉，与玛格丽特·多诺霍（Margaret Donohoe），未婚女子，之婚礼。"
派克旅馆（Pike's Hotel）的名字和日期几乎无法辨认，但文件确实在此；虽然这不是原件，但很明显佩吉绝不可能伪造它。这份文件的出示给人留下了深刻印象。它确实很大程度上说服了布莱克。
他盘问了所有证人，用他们的证词互相核对，追踪了威廉·格兰特当时的生活轨迹，查阅了那位丛林传教士的历史；一切都吻合。马丁·多尔（Martin Doyle）——黑马丁（Black Martin）的儿子马丁——把台词背得滚瓜烂熟。佩吉每天需要的话，可以把婚礼的细节说上五十遍，每次都准确无误，从不自相矛盾。所以他最终放弃了在案子里挑毛病的念头，决心投入其中，争取胜利。

===Original===
On the other side there was trouble in the camp—no witnesses could be found, except Martin Doyle, and he was ready to swear to the wedding. At last it became evident that the only chance of overthrowing Peggy's case was to find Considine; but the earth seemed to have swallowed him up.
The influence of the Chief of Police was brought to bear, and many a weary mile did the troopers of the Outer Back ride in search of the missing man. One of them followed a Considine about two hundred miles across country, and embodied the story of his wanderings in a villainously written report; brief and uncouth as the narrative was, it was in itself an outline picture of bush life. From shearers' hut to artesian borers' camp, from artesian well to the opal-fields, from the opal-fields to a gold-rush, from the gold-rush to a mail-coach stable, he pursued this Considine, only to find that, in the words of the report, "the individual was not the same."

===Chinese===
另一方面，营地里则麻烦重重——除了马丁·多尔，找不到任何证人，而他随时准备为婚礼发誓作证。最后事态变得很清楚，推翻佩吉案子的唯一机会就是找到康西丁（Considine）；但他似乎人间蒸发了。
警方总长（Chief of Police）动用了影响力，偏远内陆（Outer Back）的骑警们为了寻找这个失踪的人，骑马跋涉了许多英里疲惫的路程。其中一个骑警追踪了一个名叫康西丁的人，穿越了大约两百英里的乡间，把他的漂泊经历写成了一份文笔糟糕的报告；尽管叙述简短粗陋，它本身就是一幅丛林生活的缩影。从剪羊毛工的棚屋到自流井钻探队的营地，从自流井到蛋白石矿区，从蛋白石矿区到淘金热现场，从淘金热现场到驿站马车棚，他一路追踪这个康西丁，结果只发现——用报告里的话说——"此人并非同一人。"

===Original===
Things looked hopeless for Mary Grant, when help came from an unexpected quarter. A letter written in a rugged, forcible fist, arrived for Charlie Gordon from a young fellow named Redshaw, once a station-hand on Kuryong, who had gone out to the back-country and was rather a celebrity in his way. His father was a pensioner at the old station, and Redshaw junior, who was known as Flash Jack, evidently kept in touch with things at Kuryong. He wrote
> "Dear Sir,"
> "I hear from Gannon the trooper that you want to find Keogh. When he left the coach that time, he went back to the station and got his horses, and cleared out, and he is now hiding in Reeves's buffalo camp at the back of Port Faraway. If I hear any more will let you know.
> "J. Redshaw, alas 'Flash Jack.' "

===Chinese===
玛丽·格兰特（Mary Grant）的前景看来毫无希望了，这时意想不到的地方伸来了援手。一封用粗犷有力的笔迹写成的信，寄给了查理·戈登（Charlie Gordon），寄信人是一个名叫雷德肖（Redshaw）的年轻人，曾在库里昂（Kuryong）当过牧场（station）帮手，后来去了偏远内陆，在他那一行里算是个名人。他父亲是老牧场（station）的一位退休金领取者，小雷德肖人称"闪亮杰克"（Flash Jack），显然与库里昂的事保持着联系。他写道：

> "先生台鉴，"
> "我从骑警甘农（Gannon）那里听说您要找基奥（Keogh）。他那次离开马车后，回到牧场（station）取了马，就走了，现在躲在遥远港（Port Faraway）后面的里夫斯（Reeves）水牛营地里。如有更多消息我会通知您。"
> "J. Redshaw，即 '闪亮杰克'。"

===Original===
"What's all this?" said Pinnock, when Charlie and Carew brought him the letter. "Who is J. Redshaw, and why does he sign 'alas Flash Jack?' "
"He means *Alias*, don't you see? *Alias* Flash Jack. He is a man we used to have on the station, and his father used to work for us—I expect he wants to do us a good turn."
"It will be a good turn in earnest, if he puts you in the way of finding Considine," said the lawyer. "You will have to send Hugh up. The old man knows you and Carew, and if he saw you coming he would take to the woods, as the Yankees say. Even when you do get him the case isn't over, because the jury will side with Peggy. They'll sympathise with her efforts to prove herself an honest woman. It isn't marrying too much that will get her into trouble—it's the other thing. But we have the date and place of her alleged marriage with William Grant; and if this old Considine can prove, by documents, mind you, not by his own simple word—because it's a hundred to one the jury wouldn't believe him—I say, if he can prove that she married him on that very day and at that very place, then she's beaten. No one on earth could swallow the story of her marrying two different people on the same day."
"Hugh can go," said Charlie. "He'll have to do his best this time. It all depends on getting hold of this Considine, eh? Well, Hugh'll have to get him. If he fails he needn't show his face amongst us any more."

===Chinese===
"这都是什么意思？"查理和卡鲁把信拿给平诺克（Pinnock）看时，他问道。"这个J. Redshaw是谁，他为什么签名写'即闪亮杰克'？"
"他意思是*化名*（Alias），你没看出来吗？化名'闪亮杰克'。他是我们以前牧场（station）的人，他父亲以前为我们干活——我想他是想帮我们一个忙。"
"要是他能帮你们找到康西丁，那可真是帮了大忙了，"律师说。"你们得派休（Hugh）去。老头子认识你们俩，要是他看到你们来，就会像美国人说的那样'钻进树林'。就算你们真找到他，案子还没完，因为陪审团会站在佩吉一边。他们会同情她努力证明自己是个正经女人。让她惹上麻烦的不是结了太多次婚——而是另一件事。但我们有她所谓与威廉·格兰特结婚的日期和地点；如果这个老康西丁能证明——记住，是用文件证明，不是靠他本人的话——因为陪审团相信他的可能性微乎其微——我是说，如果他能证明她就是在那一天、那个地方嫁给了他，那她就输了。世上没人能相信一个人同一天嫁给了两个不同的人这种故事。"
"休可以去，"查理说。"这次他得尽全力。全看能不能找到这个康西丁，对吧？嗯，休得把他找到。如果他找不到，就别在我们面前露脸了。"

===Original===
Mary Grant was called in and told the great news, and then Pinnock started out to find Hugh. But before the lawyer could see him, Mary met him in the garden.
Hugh did not see that he could be of any use in the case, and wanted to be quit of Kuryong for good. Seeing Mary day after day, he had become more and more miserable as the days went by. He determined at last to go away altogether, and, when once he had made up his mind, only waited for a chance to tell her that he was going. The chance came as she left the office after consulting with Pinnock.
"Miss Grant," he said, "if you don't mind, I think I will resign my management of this station. I will make a start for myself or get a job somewhere else. You will easily get someone to take my place."
She looked at him keenly for a while.
"I didn't expect this of you," she said, bitterly. "The rats leave the sinking ship. Is that it?"
His face flushed a dull red. "You know better than that," he said. "I would stop if I could be of any use, but what is there I can do?"
"Why do you want to leave?"
"I want to get away from here—I want to get out of the hills for awhile."
Mary knew, as well as if he had told her, that what he wanted was to go where he could forget her and see whether absence would break the chain; and triumph lit up her eyes, for it was pleasant even in the midst of her troubles to know that he still cared. Then she came to a swift decision.
"Will you do something for me away from the hills, then?" she said.
"Where?"
"Up North. I want someone to find that man Considine that your brother and Mr. Carew met. You know how important it is to me. Will you do it for me?"
Hugh would have jumped at the chance to risk his life for her lightest wish.
"I will go anywhere and do my best to find anyone you want," he said; "When do you want me to start?"
"See Mr. Pinnock and your brother about that. They will tell you all about it; and if you do manage to find this man, why, you can talk about leaving after that if you want to. Will you go for me?"
"Yes. I will go, Miss Grant; and I will never come back till I find this man—if he is alive."
She laid her hand on his arm.
"I know you will do all you can," she said, "but in any case, whether you find him or not—come back again!"

===Chinese===
玛丽·格兰特（Mary Grant）被叫来告知了这个重大消息，然后平诺克（Pinnock）动身去找休（Hugh）。但在律师见到他之前，玛丽在花园里遇到了他。
休觉得自己在这个案子里帮不上什么忙，想彻底离开库里昂（Kuryong）。日复一日地见到玛丽，随着日子一天天过去，他变得越来越痛苦。他最终决定一走了之，一旦下定决心，就只等一个机会告诉她自己要走。机会在她与平诺克商议完离开办公室时出现了。
"格兰特小姐（Miss Grant），"他说，"如果您不介意的话，我想辞去这个牧场（station）的管理职务。我要自己创业或者去别的地方找个活儿。您很容易就能找到人接替我。"
她锐利地看了他一会儿。
"我没想到你会这样，"她苦涩地说。"落井下石，船沉鼠散。是这样吗？"
他的脸涨成了暗红色。"您心里清楚不是那样，"他说。"如果我能帮上忙，我会留下，但我能做什么呢？"
"你为什么要走？"
"我想离开这里——我想暂时离开这座山。"
玛丽心里一清二楚，好像他亲口告诉过她似的：他想去一个能忘记她的地方，看看距离是否能斩断这份情丝；胜利的光芒点亮了她的眼睛，因为在她所有的烦恼之中，知道他依然在乎，也是一种甜蜜。接着她迅速做出了决定。
"那么，你愿意为我做一件事，离开这座山吗？"她说。
"去哪里？"
"去北方。我要找个人，找到你兄弟和卡鲁先生（Mr. Carew）遇到的那个康西丁（Considine）。你知道这对我有多重要。你愿意为我去做吗？"
休哪怕只是她最轻微的愿望，也会毫不犹豫地拿命去冒险。
"您要我找谁，我都会去任何地方尽力找到，"他说；"您希望我什么时候动身？"
"去问平诺克先生和你兄弟吧。他们会把一切告诉你；如果你真能想办法找到这个人，那么，到时候你想谈离开的事也可以。你会为我去吗？"
"会的。我会去的，格兰特小姐；在我找到这个人之前——如果他还活着的话——我不会回来。"
她把手放在他的手臂上。
"我知道你会尽你所能，"她说，"但无论如何，不管你找没找到——都要回来！"

## XXIV The Second Search for Considine / XXIV 第二次搜寻康西丁

===Original===
Before leaving Hugh was fully instructed what to do if he compassed the second finding of Considine. He was to travel under another name, for fear that his own would get about, and cause the fugitive to make another hurried disappearance.

He took a subpoena to serve on the old man as a last resource.

Charlie was emphatic. "Go up and get hold of the old vagrant, and find out all about it. Don't make a mess of it, whatever you do. Remember the old lady, and Miss Grant, and the youngsters, and all of us depend on you in this business. Don't come back beaten. Don't let anything stop you. Get him drunk or get him sober﻿—friendly or fighting﻿—but get the truth, and get the proofs of it. Choke it out of the old hound somehow."

===Chinese===
出发之前，休·戈登（Hugh Gordon）已经得到了详尽的指示：若能找到康西丁（Considine），该怎么做。他必须换个名字旅行，以免自己的名号传出去，让那个逃亡者再次仓皇消失。

他带了一份传票，准备在万不得已时交给那老头。

查理·戈登（Charlie Gordon）态度坚决："上去找到那个老流浪汉，把事情查个水落石出。无论如何别把事搞砸了。想想老太太，想想格兰特小姐（Miss Grant），想想那些孩子们——我们全靠你了。别灰头土脸地回来。别让任何事挡住你。灌醉他也好，让他清醒着也好——客气也好，硬来也好——总之要套出真相，拿到证据。想方设法从那老猎狗嘴里撬出来。"

===Original===
Hugh said that he would, and departed, weighed down by responsibility, to execute his difficult mission. He had to go into an untravelled country to get the truth out of a man who did not want to tell it; and the time allowed was short, as the case could not be postponed much longer.

He travelled by sea to Port Faraway, a tropical sweltering township by the Northern seas of Australia, and when he reached it felt like one of the heroes in Tennyson's Lotus Eaters﻿—he had come "into a land wherein it seemed always afternoon."

Reeves, the buffalo shooter, was a well-known man, but to find his camp was another matter. No one seemed to have energy enough to take much interest in the quest.

===Chinese===
休答应照办，随后动身上路，沉甸甸的责任压在心头，去执行这项艰难的任务。他必须深入一片人迹罕至的荒野，从一个根本不愿开口的人嘴里掏出真相；而留给他的时间又很短，因为案子已经拖不了太久了。

他搭船去了远港（Port Faraway），那是一个位于澳大利亚北部海滨的热带小镇，闷热难当。一到那里，他就觉得自己像丁尼生笔下的"食莲人"——来到了"一片仿佛永远是午后的地方"。

水牛猎手里夫斯（Reeves）是个出了名的人，但找到他的营地却是另一码事。似乎谁都没有足够的精力对这番寻找表现出多少兴趣。

===Original===
Hugh interviewed a leading citizen at the hotel, and got very little satisfaction. He said, "I want to get out to Reeves's camp. Do you know where it is, and how one gets there?"

"Well," said the leading citizen, putting his feet up on the arms of his long chair and gasping for air, "Le's see! Reeves's camp﻿—ah! Where is he camped now?"

"I don't know," said Hugh. "I wish I did. That's what I want to find out."

"Hopkins'd know. Hopkins, the storekeeper. He sends out the supplies. Did you ask him?"

"No," said Hugh. "I didn't. I'll go and ask him now."

"Too hot to bustle round now," said the leading citizen, lighting his pipe. "What'll you have to drink? Have some square; it's the best drink here."

===Chinese===
休在旅馆里访谈了一位当地名流，却没得到多少有用的信息。他说："我想去里夫斯的营地。你知道在哪儿，怎么去吗？"

"嗯，"那位名流把脚跷在长椅扶手上，喘着气说，"让我想想！里夫斯的营地——啊！他现在驻扎在哪儿？"

"我不知道，"休说，"我真希望我知道。这正是我想弄明白的。"

"霍普金斯（Hopkins）应该知道。霍普金斯，就是那个杂货店老板。供给品都是他送出去的。你问过他没有？"

"没有，"休说，"还没问。我现在就去找他。"

"这会儿热得要命，别到处跑了，"名流说着点起了烟斗，"喝点什么？来杯方瓶威士忌吧，这里最好的酒。"

===Original===
Hugh thought it well to fall in with the customs of the inhabitants, so he had a stiff gin-and-water at nine in the morning, a thing he had never done, or even seen done, in his life before. Then he went over in the blazing sunlight to the storekeeper, and asked whether he knew where Reeves' camp was.

"That I don't," said the storekeeper. "I send out what they want by a Malay who sails a one-masted craft round the coast, and goes up the river to their camp, and brings the hides back. They send a blackfellow to let me know when they want any stuff, and where to send it."

"Perhaps I could go out with the next lot of stuff," said Hugh. "When will they want it, do you think?"

"Well, they mightn't want any more. They might go on now till the wet season, and then they'll come in."

"When is the wet season, then?"

"Oh, a couple of months, likely. Perhaps three months. Perhaps there won't be none at all to speak of. What'll you have?"

===Chinese===
休觉得最好入乡随俗，于是上午九点就喝了一大杯掺水金酒——这是他这辈子从未做过、甚至从未见过的事。然后他顶着烈日去找杂货店老板，问他是否知道里夫斯的营地在哪儿。

"我可不知道，"店老板说，"供给品都是让一个马来人用单桅小船沿海岸线运过去，再沿河送到他们营地的，回来时顺便把兽皮捎回来。他们需要什么东西，就派个土著黑人来告诉我，把东西送去哪儿。"

"也许我可以跟下一批货一起去，"休说，"你觉得他们什么时候需要？"

"嗯，他们可能不需要了。也许会一直干到雨季，到时候他们自己就回来了。"

"那雨季什么时候到？"

"哦，大概两个月吧。也许三个月。也许压根儿没什么雨季可言。你喝点什么？"

===Original===
"Oh, I have just had a drink, thanks. Fact is, I'm a bit anxious to get out to this camp. It's a bit important. You don't know where they are for certain?"

"Lord knows! Anywhere! Might be on one river, might be on another. They'll come in in the wet season. Better have a drink, anyhow. You must have something. What'll it be﻿—square? Beer? Can't stand beer in this climate, myself."

"Oh, well," said Hugh desperately, "I'll have another square. Make it a light one. Do you think I can get anyone who knows where they are camped to go out with me?"

"Tommy Prince'd know, I expect. He was out in that country before. But he's gone with a bullock-team, drawing quartz to the new battery at the Oriental. At least I saw him start out three weeks ago. Said he was in a hurry, too, as the battery couldn't start until he got the quartz hauled."

===Chinese===
"哦，我刚喝过，谢谢。说真的，我急着想去那个营地。这事很重要。你也不知道他们确切在哪儿吗？"

"天晓得！哪儿都可能！可能在一条河上，可能在另一条河上。雨季他们就回来了。不管怎样，再喝一杯吧。你总得喝点什么。来点什么——方瓶威士忌？啤酒？这种气候我可受不了啤酒。"

"好吧，"休无奈地说，"那就再来一杯方瓶威士忌。淡一点的。你觉得能不能找个人知道他们的营地在哪儿，给我带路？"

"汤米·普林斯（Tommy Prince）应该知道。他以前去过那片地方。不过他已经跟着牛车队走了，给东方矿区的新选矿厂拉石英矿石。我至少三周前看到他出发的。他还说赶时间，因为选矿厂等他把矿石拉回去才能开工。"

===Original===
"Perhaps he didn't start," said Hugh; "perhaps he put it off till after the wet season?"

"Well," said the storekeeper, meditatively, "he might, but I don't think he would. There's no one else, that I know of, can find them for you. Lord knows where they are. They camp in one place till the buffalo are all shot, and then they shift to new ground. Perhaps ten miles, perhaps thirty. Have another drink? What'll you have?"

"No, not any more, thanks. About this Tommy Prince, now; if I can find him he might tell me where to go. Where can I find him?"

"Down at the Margaret is where he camps, but I think he's gone to the Oriental by this time﻿—sure to be. That's about forty miles down past the Margaret. There was a fellow came in from the Margaret for supplies, and he'll be going back tomorrow﻿—if he can find his packhorses."

===Chinese===
"也许他没出发，"休说，"也许他推迟到雨季之后了？"

"嗯，"店老板若有所思地说，"有可能，但我觉得他不会。据我所知，没有别人能帮你找到他们。天知道他们在哪儿。他们在一个地方扎营，直到水牛全猎完，然后搬到新地方。也许十英里，也许三十英里。再喝一杯？来点什么？"
